# Code and structure review — October 2026

A read-only review of the repository at commit 795697b, made before the UX, agent and structure work
planned in [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md). Nine readers each mapped one area; a second pass tried to refute
every load-bearing claim (46 confirmed, 23 confirmed with a correction, 1 refuted); the agent-architecture
judge checked the design proposals against the code and primary sources. The corrections are listed under
*Corrections from the verification pass* and take precedence over the area findings where they differ.
Raw data: `docs/plan/review.json`, `docs/plan/verification.json`, `docs/plan/designs.json`,
`docs/plan/redteam.json`.

Line numbers refer to commit 795697b (before the one-off formatting commit). After it, locate code by
selector, function name, test title or quoted string.

## Summary

- **Strengths.** A real trust boundary (internal-only API, signed assertion, CSRF header in both tiers),
  SQL-enforced usage counters reserved before every paid call, at-most-once handling of paid background
  work, a durable PostgreSQL job queue, "models propose, code decides" with quote grounding, fixture
  providers that keep CI and Playwright free of paid services, and an accessibility gate in e2e.
- **Why the start confuses.** No onboarding, placement or explanation of the method; an all-Dutch
  interface; a 1–3 minute wait as the first experience; nine overlapping destinations; the level is
  chosen in three places that disagree (A1 stories, pre-A1 path).
- **Why practice bores.** Three interaction mechanics over 4,800 templated topic activities; nothing
  shuffled or adaptive; typed recall never checked; English glosses under the options give answers away;
  points and streak ignore course work.
- **Why it looks flat.** Three CSS redesigns stacked by import order, conflicting `:root` blocks, 356 hex
  colours, about 610 dead lines, one `@keyframes`, no `:active` state, colour only on episode covers.
- **Agents.** Every model feature is a validated single call or a fixed pipeline; no tool calling, strict
  structured output, streaming or learner memory; the production deployment (gpt-4.1-mini, NoAutoUpgrade)
  stops serving on 2027-04-14.
- **Structure.** Provider calls inside row locks, duplicated paid-call and error boilerplate with
  differing failure policy, an untyped HTTP contract, a single-threaded job loop without lease renewal,
  client-island pages with hand-rolled fetching, components with 3,000-character lines.

## Areas

### Backend platform and delivery

SHAPE. apps/api/src/dlp is about 13.4k lines of Python (13,643 including migrations). It is a synchronous FastAPI + SQLAlchemy 2.0 service, organised into a horizontal api/ layer, a db/ layer, vertical domains/, and a providers/ adapter package:
- dlp/main.py (127): app factory, lifespan (preflight log + JobLoop thread), one http middleware (span, X-Request-Id, nosniff, no-store), a catch-all 500 handler, and 13 routers
- dlp/config.py (233): pydantic-settings Settings with production rules in _enforce_environment_rules, plus get_settings() cached by lru_cache
- dlp/cli.py (219): argparse ops commands (preflight, load-fixture, acceptance, export-recording, reset-learner-data, write-episode, run-jobs)
- dlp/release.py (85): the migration job (alembic upgrade + mission load + dlp_app DML role under an advisory lock)
- dlp/observability.py (50): Azure Monitor OTel with a manual request span and one histogram
- dlp/api/: deps.py (RequestContext, principal/csrf/request-id deps) plus routes_{health,missions,practice,progress,speech,curriculum,library,topics,topic_conversations,coach,content_review,stories,videos}.py
- dlp/db/: base.py (Base with naming convention, new_id, utcnow), session.py (global sync engine, session_scope, get_session), registry.py (imports all models for Alembic)
- dlp/domains/{coaching,content,content_review,curriculum,feedback,identity,jobs,practice,progress,speech,stories,topic_conversations,usage,videos}: each has models.py/service.py(/schemas.py). practice/ and topic_conversations/ add workflow.py (LangGraph).
- dlp/providers/: base.py (ABCs ChatModel, SpeechToText, TextToSpeech, BlobStore, VideoRenderer), registry.py (composition root, lru_cache get_providers), chat_openai_compatible.py, speech_azure.py, speech_local.py, blob_azure.py, video_azure.py, video_local.py, fixtures.py, preflight.py
Largest files: videos/service.py 561, stories/service.py 533, api/routes_practice.py 431, content/schemas.py 421, curriculum/service.py 381, practice/turns.py 339, coaching/service.py 331. Nothing is extreme. videos/service.py and stories/service.py are the closest to god-modules: each mixes request intake, a paid writer/validator retry loop with usage accounting, job handlers, learner views and learner actions.

REQUEST PATH. Browser → Next.js /api/[...path] proxy (apps/web/app/api/[...path]/route.ts) → the proxy strips identity, checks CSRF/Origin, signs a 60 s HS256 assertion and forwards to the internal-only API. In the API, context_dep validates the assertion (identity/assertions.py validate_assertion: iss/aud/exp, allowlist, idp, fixture gating), enforces X-Requested-With on unsafe methods and upserts the Learner. All routes are sync def, so they run in the threadpool. One request = one session_scope transaction, committed when the route returns normally and rolled back when it raises. Routes deliberately RETURN a JSONResponse instead of raising so that usage and failed-turn rows still commit. Paid calls go through usage.reserve → provider → usage.commit/release (SQL-enforced counters for daily and total). A practice turn runs 2 sequential LLM calls (LangGraph propose_action→validate_action→compose_reply), then TTS, then a blob put, all inside the HTTP request while holding a FOR UPDATE lock on the practice session. Nothing streams to the client except the MP4 byte ranges in routes_videos.media. There is no SSE, WebSocket or async I/O anywhere.

JOBS. jobs table: claim_next uses UPDATE … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED with a lease, backoff has full jitter, failed jobs go dead after max_attempts, and enqueue is idempotent. A single daemon JobLoop thread per API process polls every 2 s. Handlers are registered by import side effect (@jobs.registry.register in stories, videos and content_review). They pull settings and providers from global singletons and manage their own commits: commit a claim, reserve usage, commit, re-lock the domain row FOR UPDATE, call the provider, then settle. Interrupted claims are marked failed/"interrupted" so a paid call is never replayed. Leases are never renewed. Production runs exactly 1 API replica (main.bicep:376).

PROVIDERS. Every adapter is synchronous httpx or a synchronous SDK. One OpenAI-compatible client serves both Ollama and Azure OpenAI v1. Structured output works by injecting the schema as text, using response_format json_object, pulling JSON out with a regex and running a repair/retry loop. There is no tool calling and no streaming. Azure auth uses managed identity (DefaultAzureCredential) or keys. Fixture providers drive CI and E2E. Preflight reports each Azure adapter as "ok" only when DB rows show it has done real live work.

DELIVERY. CI (ci.yml) compiles Bicep, builds both images, runs ruff + 328 pytest tests against real Postgres with fixture providers, runs the benchmark dry runs, and runs the web lint/unit/Playwright E2E. Deploy (deploy.yml, manual, OIDC) requires exact-commit main CI, rebuilds the images in ACR, then azure_release.py runs the migration job and does az containerapp update --image to digest-pinned images with revision health waits, followed by an anonymous live check. deploy_current.sh is the same flow from a laptop. Bicep provisions: Log Analytics + App Insights, a budget, 3 user-assigned identities, ACR, a VNet, a private PG Flexible B1ms, Key Vault (RBAC) with per-secret grants, Storage (shared key disabled), Speech, Azure OpenAI (pinned deployments), a Container Apps env, the migration job, an internal API (0.25 vCPU/0.5 Gi, min = max = 1) and the web app with Easy Auth.

VERDICT. Security and cost discipline are strong: trust boundary, allowlist, production guards, usage reservations, idempotency and a migration-first release. It is not a clean ports-and-adapters design. Domains import the composition root and adapter internals, one adapter imports a domain, and preflight queries domain tables. The whole stack is synchronous and request-bound, the job runner is single-threaded with no lease heartbeat, the API contract is untyped (no response_model), and observability is minimal. These are the main obstacles to agentic features and to a more interactive, streaming UI.

**Strengths**

- Clear, enforced trust boundary: internal-only API, short-lived HS256 assertion with iss/aud/exp/lifetime checks and allowlist re-check (domains/identity/assertions.py:63-100), CSRF header re-enforced in the API (api/deps.py:47-51), fixture identity refused in production (config.py:149-151, main.py:54-55).
- Production configuration is validated at startup with explicit invariants: Azure-only providers, PAID_USAGE_ENABLED approval, TLS Postgres, real allowlist, 32+ char key (config.py:145-181).
- Cost control is first-class: SQL-enforced reserve/commit/release counters for daily and total scope with dedup by request id (domains/usage/service.py:104-172), applied before every paid call, plus Azure budget alerts (infra/main.bicep:83-95).
- At-most-once semantics for paid background calls: durable claim before the call and 'interrupted' marking instead of replay (stories/service.py:219-222, videos/service.py:289-292, content_review/service.py:159-162,190-198).
- 'Model proposes, code decides' pattern implemented with LangGraph and code validators (practice/workflow.py, topic_conversations/workflow.py, stories/validator.py), with prompt_version tags and per-call metadata persisted (practice/turns.py:186-200).
- Narrow provider interfaces with local, fixture and Azure implementations selected in one registry (providers/base.py, providers/registry.py), so CI and E2E run without paid services.
- Durable PostgreSQL job queue with SKIP LOCKED claims, leases, jittered backoff, dead-lettering and idempotency keys (domains/jobs/service.py:65-140), with tests including lease recovery (tests/test_jobs.py:65-76).
- Hardened subprocess handling for ffmpeg: argument lists, timeouts, address-space caps, bounded inputs (domains/speech/audio.py:46-81,126-150).
- Migration-first, gated release: exact-commit CI verification, digest-pinned images, advisory-locked migration job with a separate admin identity, DML-only runtime role, readiness tied to the exact migration head (release.py, scripts/azure_release.py, routes_health.py:32-44).
- Least-privilege Azure: managed identities, per-secret Key Vault grants, private Postgres, Storage shared-key disabled, Speech/OpenAI local auth disabled, pinned model versions (infra/main.bicep:96-265).
- Integration-heavy test suite (328 tests) against real PostgreSQL with failure injection (FixtureChatModel fail_first/fail_calls), recorded Azure speech fixtures, plus Playwright E2E in CI.
- Readable, small modules (no file over 561 lines) and extensive decision records (docs/DECISIONS.md, docs/ENGINEERING.md).

**Findings**

| # | Severity | Finding | Evidence | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | high | **Long provider calls run inside DB transactions that hold row locks and pooled connections** — A conversation turn can hold a DB connection and a FOR UPDATE lock for up to (120s x 3 attempts + backoff) x 2 calls + TTS. Job handlers hold domain row locks across LLM calls on purpose to prevent duplicate paid work. With a 10-connection pool, concurrent dashboard loads plus slow LLM calls can exhaust the pool (pool_timeout=10 then 500). PostgreSQL never times out an idle-in-transaction connection. The learner waits for the whole pipeline with no partial feedback. The chat semaphore is per ChatModel instance (chat_openai_compatible.py:105), and chat and chat_strong are separate instances (registry.py:159-160), so real concurrency is 4, not 2. | practice/turns.py:156-237 (reserve, run_turn with 2 sequential chat calls, then TTS + blob put in _attach_character_audio:265-293) all inside the request session; routes_practice.py:230 `_load(session, ctx, session_id)` defaults for_update=True; stories/service.py:270-278 re-locks the episode `with_for_update()` then calls chat.complete; videos/service.py:201-208 same; content_review/service.py:192-224 comment 'Hold the row lock again across the call'; db/session.py:22-24 pool_size=5, max_overflow=5, pool_timeout=10, only statement_timeout (no idle_in_transaction_session_timeout/lock_timeout); config.py:69,73 chat_timeout_seconds=120, chat_max_attempts=3; chat_openai_compatible.py:242 backoff sleep up to 8s | Split each paid call into claim/commit → call outside any transaction → short settle transaction, keeping the existing at-most-once status rows. Set idle_in_transaction_session_timeout and lock_timeout in connect_args. Move the TTS of the character reply out of the turn transaction: return the text first and fetch audio lazily, since the audio endpoint already exists at routes_practice.py:314. Consider async I/O (httpx.AsyncClient, async SQLAlchemy with psycopg3) for chat endpoints. |
| 2 | high | **No streaming or tool calling: blocks interactive and agentic UX** — Character replies, feedback, writing assessment, translations and coach plans are all request/response with multi-second latency and nothing to show until the end. There is no way to stream tokens over SSE, show step progress for multi-step LLM work, or let a model call domain functions as tools. These are the prerequisites for a tutor-agent experience and for a more lively UI. Background generation (stories, videos) only exposes status by polling GET endpoints. | Only StreamingResponse is for video bytes (api/routes_videos.py:121). All routes are sync `def` (grep: no `async def` outside main.py). ChatModel exposes only complete/complete_once returning a full ChatResult (providers/base.py:48-89), with no stream or tools parameters. The request body only ever uses response_format json_object (chat_openai_compatible.py:177-184). The web proxy already passes upstream.body through as a stream (apps/web/app/api/[...path]/route.ts:128). docs/ENGINEERING.md:156 'Streaming is not part of 0.1'. | Add `stream()` (async iterator of deltas plus a final usage record) and optional `tools` to the ChatModel port, implemented in OpenAICompatibleChatModel (Azure OpenAI v1 supports both). Add SSE endpoints for conversation replies and for job progress (job status/events), e.g. GET /events or per-resource /stream, settling usage when the stream ends. The proxy needs no change for SSE apart from forwarding text/event-stream (content-type is already forwarded). |
| 3 | medium | **CLI run-jobs does not register video job handlers; an unknown kind crashes the drain and leaves the job leased** — Running `python -m dlp.cli run-jobs` while video_script/video_render jobs are queued raises KeyError, aborts the drain and burns one attempt. The job stays leased until the lease expires. Registering handlers through import side effects is fragile in general. | cli.py:167-173 imports only content_review and stories services before drain(). Video handlers are registered only via import side effect at videos/service.py:324,413. jobs/service.py:118 `handler = registry.get(job.kind)` sits outside the try block, so the KeyError propagates out of process_one after claim_next already committed attempts+1 and status 'leased' (jobs/service.py:145-149). docs/ENGINEERING.md:15 claims 'the standalone job CLI imports the same handler'. | Have an explicit handler-registration module (e.g. dlp/jobs_registry.py or a `register_all()` called by both main.create_app and the CLI). Move `registry.get` inside run_job's try so an unknown kind marks the job dead with a clear error. |
| 4 | medium | **Job runner robustness: single thread, no lease heartbeat, unused settings, no retention or metrics** — Head-of-line blocking: one long story write delays every video poll and content review. Scaling the API to 2+ replicas, or running `dlp.cli run-jobs` against production while the API loop runs, lets a second worker reclaim an expired lease mid-call. That worker then marks the domain row 'interrupted', and the first worker's work becomes 'obsolete'. The jobs table grows without bound. | JobLoop is one daemon thread processing sequentially (jobs/service.py:167-201). Lease is job_lease_seconds=60 (config.py:137) with no renewal while handlers can run for minutes (chat 120s x attempts; SceneCardRenderer renders synchronously in start(), video_local.py:91-104). Safety relies on a single replica (infra/main.bicep:376 minReplicas=maxReplicas=1). Retry loops call session.commit() between attempts (stories/service.py:269, videos/service.py:200), briefly releasing the row lock. job_max_attempts (config.py:138) is never read: enqueue hard-codes 5 (jobs/service.py:78). The 'failed' status (jobs/models.py:13) is never set. The lease_owner check at jobs/service.py:153 is not under a row lock. No cleanup of done/dead jobs. Job status is not exposed to clients except through domain rows. | Add lease heartbeat/renewal (or a lease longer than the maximum handler time, derived from settings), a bounded worker pool (N threads or an async worker), per-kind concurrency, retention cleanup, and use of settings.job_max_attempts. Optionally run the worker as a separate Container Apps job/app sharing the image, so the HTTP API can scale independently. Publish queue depth and age as metrics. |
| 5 | medium | **Transaction outcome depends on 'return vs raise' convention** — Whether usage rows, failed turns and STT accounting persist depends on each route choosing to return a JSONResponse instead of raising HTTPException. That is invisible at call sites and easy to break when refactoring or adding agent endpoints. The ProviderError policy also differs by domain: curriculum/service.py:238-241 releases usage on ProviderError, while stories/service.py:280-284, videos/service.py:210-214 and content_review/service.py:231-235 commit the estimate. | db/session.py:44-60 commits on normal return, rolls back on exception; routes_practice.py:158-165 'Returned, not raised: the request's transaction must commit'; routes_practice.py:308-311 _speech_error; routes_curriculum.py:61-63 'Returning a response commits already completed billable work'; routes_stories.py:44-48 and routes_videos.py:34-38 return JSONResponse for 429/503; docs/DECISIONS.md:119 | Make billing persistence explicit: settle usage in its own short transaction (or a separate session) that commits independently of the request outcome. Then let routes raise typed errors freely. Unify the ProviderError billing policy in one helper. |
| 6 | medium | **Inconsistent error model and duplicated per-router exception mapping** — Every new endpoint has to copy the mapping boilerplate. Error bodies are free-form {detail} strings, some English sentences and some technical, with no machine-readable codes for the UI to localise (the app is trilingual nl/en/fa). Internal hostnames or exception text can leak to the browser. | Domain exceptions disagree on the attribute: AudioError.reason (speech/audio.py:25-29), PracticeError.reason (routes_practice.py:172), CurriculumError/VideoError/StoryError .detail (routes_curriculum.py:58, routes_videos.py:33). Duplicated `_run` wrappers: routes_stories.py:37-48, routes_videos.py:29-38, routes_curriculum.py:54-70. Repeated try/except blocks in routes_practice.py (12 HTTPException re-raises). Raw provider messages forwarded to clients: routes_speech.py:39-42 `detail=f"speech-to-text unavailable: {exc}"` where speech_azure.py:84 embeds the raw ConnectError text. The only global handler is for Exception (main.py:101-109). | Introduce a DomainError base (status, code, message, optional params) and register FastAPI exception handlers for DomainError, UsageLimitExceeded, ProviderUnavailable and ProviderError once in create_app. Emit RFC 9457 problem+json with stable `code` values the web maps to localized copy. Delete the per-router _run wrappers. |
| 7 | medium | **Not a clean ports-and-adapters layout: leaky boundaries, service locator, circular domains** — The ports exist (providers/base.py), but domains depend on concrete modules, and settings/providers are global lru_cache singletons. Tests therefore monkeypatch globals (tests/test_videos.py:219, tests/test_speech_recovery.py:62) instead of injecting dependencies. The cross-domain web (videos→stories→curriculum→coaching→curriculum) makes it hard to add an orchestration or agent layer cleanly. | Domains import the composition root and adapter internals: `from dlp.providers.registry import Providers, get_providers` (stories/service.py:40, videos/service.py:47, content_review/service.py:24, plus 9 other domain modules) and `from dlp.providers.chat_openai_compatible import schema_instruction` (stories/service.py:39, videos/service.py:46, coaching/service.py:27, topic_conversations/workflow.py:12). An adapter imports a domain: providers/video_local.py:19 `from dlp.domains.speech.audio import run_tool`. The adapter-layer preflight queries domain tables via raw SQL (providers/preflight.py:36-48). Service locator in job handlers: stories/service.py:321, videos/service.py:326,415, content_review/service.py:243. Circular domain deps: curriculum/topics.py:201 (function-local import of coaching.service) vs coaching/service.py:22-24; feedback/service.py:27-28 vs practice/acceptance.py:23. routes_stories.py:30 imports the videos service. View-model building lives in routes (routes_practice.py:83-155) and in services (video_view, episode_view). | Introduce an application/use-case layer per feature that receives ports (ChatModel, TTS, BlobStore, Clock, UsageLedger, JobQueue) through constructor or FastAPI Depends. Move schema_instruction into providers/base or a prompt utility. Move run_tool into a shared infra module. Have job handlers receive dependencies from the runner rather than calling get_providers(). Move the preflight evidence SQL into a domain read model. Break the cycles by extracting shared read models such as coaching observations. |
| 8 | medium | **Untyped HTTP contract: no response models, OpenAPI off outside dev, no type checker in CI** — UI work (task 3) will change many payloads. Without schema-backed responses, every drift between API dicts and TS types surfaces at runtime only. JSONB columns (checks, state, model_meta) are untyped dicts, so key names are implicit contracts. | No `response_model` anywhere (grep). Routes return `dict`/`Any` built by hand (routes_practice.py:83-155, videos/service.py:431-454). OpenAPI/docs only in development (main.py:75-77). Ruff selects only E,F,I,B,UP (pyproject.toml:62-64); no mypy/pyright step in CI (.github/workflows/ci.yml:57-65). 18 `# type: ignore` in src. The web keeps hand-written TS types (apps/web/lib/client/videos.ts, stories.ts). | Add Pydantic response models per endpoint (start with today, practice session/turn, stories, videos). Keep the OpenAPI schema generated in CI even if not served in production, and generate the TS client/types from it (e.g. openapi-typescript). Add pyright/mypy (strict on new modules) to CI. |
| 9 | medium | **Duplicated 'paid LLM call' protocol and writer/critic loop across domains** — Every new LLM feature, including any agent step or tool call, will re-implement accounting, idempotency and failure policy, and policies have already diverged (release vs commit estimate on failure). | The same claim→reserve→commit→relock→call→settle sequence appears in stories/service.py:255-302, videos/service.py:187-230, content_review/service.py:190-238, curriculum/service.py:226-246, practice/turns.py:156-188 and coaching/service.py:311. The generate→validate_draft→retry-with-feedback_text loop is duplicated between stories/service.py:255-302 and videos/service.py:187-230. Token estimates are ad hoc per site (`//3`, `//2`, constants such as TOKENS_PER_TURN_ESTIMATE=1800 at practice/turns.py:28). | Extract a single `LlmGateway`/`paid_call()` application service: it takes (learner, call_id, messages, schema/tools, budget), reserves and settles usage in its own transaction, applies one documented failure policy, records model-call telemetry (prompt_version, tokens, latency) and supports streaming. Extract a generic `generate_with_critic(writer, validator, max_rounds)` used by stories, videos and future agents. |
| 10 | medium | **Structured output relies on prompt-injected schema + regex extraction instead of native structured outputs** — This works for both Ollama and Azure, but it spends prompt tokens on schema text, needs repair round-trips (more latency and cost), and cannot use strict JSON-schema mode or function/tool calling, which Azure OpenAI v1 supports natively. | chat_openai_compatible.py:84-89 schema_instruction puts the JSON schema in a system message; :177-184 response_format {type: json_object}; :64-81 extract_json_object regex fallback; :202-215 repair loop that appends 'That was not valid' turns; tokens estimated when usage is missing (:218-219, 267-268). | For kind == 'azure_openai', send response_format {type: 'json_schema', json_schema: {name, schema, strict: true}} and drop the in-prompt schema. Keep the current path for Ollama (which also supports a `format` JSON schema). Add a `tools` parameter for agent work. |
| 11 | medium | **Minimal observability: plain-text logs, no correlation, a single metric, sampled spans, no alerts** — In production a failure shows only as a status code in a 10%-sampled span. There is no per-request log line, no link between web and API traces, and no metrics for LLM latency/tokens per prompt_version, job queue depth or usage remaining. Agent features would be impossible to debug or evaluate. | logging.basicConfig with a plain format (main.py:43-44); request_id is not attached to log records (only the unhandled-error log, main.py:104-105); uvicorn runs with --no-access-log (apps/api/Dockerfile:32) and the middleware logs nothing (main.py:81-99); App Insights exports only logger 'dlp.telemetry' with sampling_ratio=0.1 and all auto-instrumentation disabled (observability.py:22-28); one histogram (observability.py:30); spans record_exception=False (observability.py:39-40); the web proxy does not forward traceparent (route.ts:19); Log Analytics daily cap 0.1 GB (infra/main.bicep:74); no alert rules or diagnostic settings in Bicep (grep: none). LLM call metadata exists only in DB rows (practice/turns.py:186-200, jobs.last_error). | Use JSON logs with a request_id/trace_id contextvar filter. Propagate W3C traceparent through the proxy. Emit OTel GenAI semantic-convention spans/metrics for each model call without prompt content (model, prompt_version, tokens, latency, outcome). Add job metrics. Add Azure Monitor alert rules (5xx rate, job dead count, budget) in Bicep. Keep the existing no-bodies/no-identifiers rule. |
| 12 | low | **GET endpoints with side effects, including enqueuing paid work** — Prefetching, retries or several open tabs can trigger writes and paid generation. Every read becomes a write transaction. This complicates caching and any future read replicas or ETags. | GET /today (routes_stories.py:60-63) → stories.today_episode → ensure_series with FOR UPDATE (stories/service.py:61-68) and ensure_buffer → queue_episode, which enqueues a paid story_episode job (stories/service.py:178-189, 370-372). Every authenticated request writes learner.last_seen_at (identity/service.py:27-30 via api/deps.py:78). | Make story buffering an explicit POST or a background scheduler job. Update last_seen_at at most once per N minutes. |
| 13 | low | **Delivery/IaC gaps: imperative app updates, rebuild-not-promote, no blob retention, public data-plane endpoints** — Bicep describes only the first deployment state. Later releases mutate apps imperatively, so a Bicep redeploy could revert env vars. Images are rebuilt rather than promoted, so the deployed artifact is not byte-identical to the CI-tested one (lock-pinned, but still a rebuild). | App updates use `az containerapp update --image ... --set-env-vars` outside Bicep (scripts/azure_release.py:170-178), so settings drift from infra/main.bicep. deploy.yml rebuilds images in ACR (deploy.yml:44-47) instead of promoting the image CI built and tested (ci.yml:23-28). The web CI job installs the API without the hashed lock (ci.yml:104). No Storage lifecycle/retention policy for learner recordings and videos (infra/main.bicep:202-211; also docs/IMPROVEMENT_REVIEW.md:41). Speech/OpenAI publicNetworkAccess 'Enabled' (infra/main.bicep:226,242); KV/Storage network rules are defaults. The API is sized 0.25 vCPU/0.5 Gi while running HTTP + job loop + ffmpeg remux (infra/main.bicep:332,376; videos/media.py via poll_render). deploy_current.sh hard-codes the ACR name and repo (scripts/deploy_current.sh:6-8). The image includes apps/api/tests (.dockerignore has no tests exclusion; Dockerfile:21). | Deploy app revisions through Bicep (or azd) with image digests as parameters. Push CI images to ACR and promote by digest. Add Storage lifecycle rules and private endpoints or network ACLs for OpenAI/Speech/KV/Storage. Size the API (or a separate worker) for ffmpeg. Exclude tests from the image. |
| 14 | low | **Assertion jti is issued but not enforced; raw provider errors reach clients** — Replay within the 60 s TTL is possible for anyone who can reach the internal API; the risk is low given internal ingress. Error strings can disclose internal endpoints. | The web sets jti (apps/web/lib/server/assertion.ts:34), but validate_assertion neither requires nor tracks jti (domains/identity/assertions.py:67-100), although docs/ENGINEERING.md:140-142 lists jti. Provider exception text is surfaced: speech_azure.py:84 `f"Azure Speech endpoint unreachable: {exc}"` → routes_speech.py:40, routes_practice.py:279-282; jobs.last_error stores str(exc) + traceback (jobs/service.py:124). | Require jti and keep a short TTL replay cache (in-process LRU is enough with 1 replica, or a table/Redis if scaled). Sanitize provider exception messages at the adapter boundary and return codes instead. |
| 15 | low | **Configuration and packaging fragilities** — Small issues that will bite when restructuring packages or adding pricing/cost dashboards. | REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[4] (config.py:19) resolves to /usr/local in the installed image, so it is patched via DLP_CONTENT_DIR/DLP_API_DIR (Dockerfile:12-13; routes_health.py:36; release.py:22). FIXTURES_DIR in production code points at tests (providers/fixtures.py:30). job_max_attempts is unused (config.py:138). usage._estimate ignores the PricingEntry.unit scale (domains/usage/service.py:204-210 vs usage/models.py:68). Preflight builds a second, unclosed Providers instance (providers/preflight.py:95). | Make content/api paths explicit settings, load fixture data via importlib.resources or a test-only path, remove or wire unused settings, normalise pricing units, and reuse get_providers() in preflight. |
| 16 | low | **Test strategy couples to globals; no coverage, migration-drift or contract checks** — The suite is strong on behaviour (328 tests, real Postgres), but refactors toward DI/async will need to rewrite many monkeypatch-based tests. Model/schema drift between ORM models and migrations is not checked automatically. | Settings and providers are lru_cache singletons mutated via monkeypatch (tests/test_videos.py:35-36,219-269; tests/test_speech_recovery.py:38-188); env pinned before import (tests/conftest.py:18-40); no dependency_overrides usage; no coverage, no `alembic check`/autogenerate drift test in CI (ci.yml:57-65); Azure OpenAI is covered only via the generic client tests (tests/test_chat_client.py) and fixtures; no load test. | Introduce app/provider factories that accept dependencies, so tests can use app.dependency_overrides. Add `alembic check` (or a metadata compare test), coverage reporting, and recorded-response contract tests for Azure OpenAI (json_schema/streaming/tool calls) before adopting them. |

**Constraints this area must respect**

- Model proposes, code decides: the LLM may interpret and phrase, but application code is authoritative for actions, completion, scores and progression (docs/DECISIONS.md D-12; docs/LEARNING_AGENTS.md:41-51; docs/ENGINEERING.md:59-61). Any 'agent' design must keep this invariant (no open tool loop with arbitrary DB/web access per LEARNING_AGENTS.md:47-48).
- Every paid provider call is reserved in usage counters before it runs and settled afterwards; uncertain paid calls are never silently replayed (docs/DECISIONS.md D-07; docs/ENGINEERING.md:198-205; stories/service.py:219-222).
- Paid usage in Azure only when PAID_USAGE_ENABLED is explicitly true; production refuses fixture providers, dev auth, the memory blob store and non-TLS Postgres, and requires Azure chat/speech/blob (config.py:145-181; DECISIONS D-17).
- The API stays internal-only behind the web proxy; identity only via the short-lived signed assertion; X-Requested-With header required on unsafe methods (api/deps.py:47-79; docs/ENGINEERING.md:131-148).
- Telemetry must never export bodies, prompts, headers, full URLs or user identifiers (observability.py:1; docs/ENGINEERING.md:34-35).
- Schema changes only through the `dlp.release migrate` job (Alembic) with the admin identity; the runtime uses a DML-only role (release.py; docs/ENGINEERING.md:30-31). New tables need Alembic migrations.
- Releases require a successful exact-commit main CI run and digest-pinned images; migration runs before the app images change (deploy.yml:30-37; scripts/azure_release.py:14-39,169-178).
- Production currently runs a single API replica with the in-process job loop (infra/main.bicep:376); scaling out requires fixing job leasing first.
- Typed input never counts as speaking practice; no fictitious scores, certificates or CEFR mastery claims (DECISIONS D-16, D-17; LEARNING_AGENTS.md:53-54).
- Azure short-audio STT limits recordings to 60 s (config.py:147-148; MAX_AUDIO_SECONDS).
- Learner support languages are Dutch (nl-BE), English and Persian (fa); feedback/translations are trilingual (config.py:71-72; Learner.support_language default 'fa' in identity/models.py:23).
- Content is versioned in the repository and validated at migration time (release.py:31-35); content review by models never publishes (LEARNING_AGENTS.md:87-89).
- Cross-platform developer workflow via scripts/run.py on Windows/WSL/macOS/Linux (DECISIONS D-11).

**Open questions**

- FastAPI 0.141.1 / Starlette 1.6.0: does the yield-dependency exit (session commit in db/session.py:57-60) run before or after the response is sent? If after, a commit failure could follow a 2xx response. Not verified against the installed version.
- What request timeout does the Container Apps ingress apply, and how long can the web proxy fetch (route.ts:116) wait for the API? No explicit timeouts were found; long LLM turns may hit platform limits. Not verified.
- Is .github/workflows/deploy.yml actually configured and in use, or is scripts/deploy_current.sh the real release path? docs/ENGINEERING.md:232-233 says the workflow is 'not yet configured', and a later commit (d8dc7cd) added scripts/setup_github_deploy.sh, which was not read.
- How many learners/allowlisted users will production serve? This determines whether async I/O, a separate worker and multi-replica scaling are worth their cost (Learner model docstring says 'Release 0.1 has exactly one learner').
- Is latency of the practice turn (2 LLM calls + TTS) the main cause of the 'boring/slow' feel? No production telemetry is exported to confirm it.
- Owner preference for an agent runtime on Azure: keep LangGraph in-process on Container Apps (already a dependency) or adopt Azure AI Foundry Agent Service. This has cost/region (West Europe) and data-handling implications. Not decided in docs.
- Does apps/web/lib/server/identity.ts (easyauth) need changes for any new streaming endpoints? Not read.
- Contents of docs/STATUS.md, docs/VERIFICATION.md and docs/GO_LIVE.md (live-verified items, known issues) were not reviewed in detail.
- coaching/service.py, feedback/service.py and curriculum/topics.py paid-call paths were only partially read; their exact usage/failure policies beyond the grep hits are unverified.

### Learning domains (API)

FastAPI app (main.py:111-123) mounts 13 sync routers; every route uses context_dep (assertion auth + CSRF header + get_or_create_learner). Domains are Python packages with models/service/schemas; static content is JSON under content/ loaded via lru_cache keyed on mtime (curriculum/path.json, practice/<stage>.json, library/<stage>.json, conversations/pilot.json); only missions are copied into DB (missions, mission_steps). Model calls go through providers.chat (small) / chat_strong with complete() (retry+repair) or complete_once(); usage.reserve/commit/release guards every paid call; jobs (Postgres queue, in-process JobLoop) run story_episode, video_script, video_render, content_review.

PER DOMAIN
1) Curriculum/path (curriculum/service.py, routes_curriculum.py): 12 stages (STAGE_IDS pre-a1..c2), each with grammar (+1 MC practice q per topic), ~37 vocab, lesson (story, reading/listening MC, speaking/writing Task) and final Test. Tables curriculum_practice (one latest row per learner/stage/skill), curriculum_attempts. Code: objective marks (75% rule), word bounds, "all four practised" gate, fresh owned recording, pass = all 4 skills. Model (chat_strong, temp 0): per-criterion met+quote+trilingual feedback; code keeps `met` only if quote is a substring of learner text. All stages open; no placement.
2) Topic practice (curriculum/topics.py, routes_topics.py): 100 topics x 4 skills x 12 stages; table topic_practice (latest only, sticky completed, latest_passed); also upserts curriculum_practice and appends practice_observations. Same graders as (1).
3) Library (library.py, routes_library.py): read-only 500+ words/100 stories per stage; no DB, nothing recorded.
4) Writing feedback (curriculum/writing.py): POST /curriculum/{stage}/writing-feedback; model proposes <=5 corrections (category spelling/grammar/word_order/punctuation/word_choice); code applies only unique whole-word, non-overlapping fragments. Result NOT persisted.
5) Coaching (coaching/service.py, routes_coach.py): tables practice_observations (append-only, idempotent per source+request_id; passed, failed_refs, summary, response) and coach_plan_requests (cache). candidate_plan is pure code: retry latest failed (needs 2 assessed failures for "repeated"), transfer topic in same category, least-practised skill -> exactly 3 items; model may only reorder (validate_ranking). Only topic_practice and conversation sources feed it.
6) Topic conversations (topic_conversations/*): 10 blueprints (schema enforces exactly 10), table topic_conversations (history JSONB, step_index, assisted_steps), max 6 turns, 3 active, 20 starts/day. Linear LangGraph interpret->validate->compose: one model call returns TopicInterpretation; code validate_meaning (quote substring, required_groups anchors, choice aliases + negation regex, injection regex); replies are authored text only.
7) Missions/practice (practice/*, content/*, feedback/*, routes_practice.py, routes_missions.py): 4 A2 missions (appointment-change, course-message, lunch-order, shop-return), base + transfer checkpoint (no help/typed/retry). Tables practice_sessions(state JSONB), practice_turns, evidence_records, feedback_reports, skill_records. Turn graph propose_action (model) -> apply_action (code state machine over slots/choices) -> compose_reply (model for appointment, fixed lines for service; contradiction filter). Feedback: model points must cite evidence handles; unverifiable points dropped.
8) Stories (stories/*, routes_stories.py): story_series (bible, rolling memory), story_episodes, vocab_items, learning_days. Job writes episode with chat_strong temp 0.7; deterministic validate_draft (Dutch share, length, sentence length, coverage vs cumulative stage vocab+saved words, question evidence substring, gij/ge) with one feedback rewrite. Learner: read(+10), answer once (+3/correct), choose a/b (+2, steers next episode), rate (+-1, unused), translate paragraph to fa (model, cached), save glossary word, read-aloud (+5).
9) Words: SM-2 on saved words only, self-graded again/hard/good/easy, +1 point/review; "learned" = interval>=21d.
10) Videos (videos/*): video_lessons; script job reuses story validator + >=20s; renderer (Azure avatar or local cards); questions/points/words duplicated from stories.
11) Content review (admin only): content_reviews; model issues must cite exact path+quote; never publishes.
12) Speech: POST /speech/transcribe, /speech/synthesize; audio_assets (meta.transcript for every recording).
13) Progress: GET /progress (skill_records only), /usage, /export (owner only; omits stories, vocab, learning_days, videos).

POINTS/STREAK: stories/today.py add_points upserts learning_days (Europe/Brussels day), DAILY_GOAL=20, streak = consecutive days with points>0 ending today/yesterday. Only stories, videos, word reviews and read-aloud award points; missions, topics, curriculum, conversations, writing feedback award nothing; GET /today next_step only considers read/choose/review/wait/explore.

LEARNER MODEL: Learner has no level/goals (identity/models.py); story_series.stage_id is the de-facto level (default a1). Progress fragmented across skill_records (per mission), curriculum_practice, topic_practice, curriculum_attempts, practice_observations, learning_days, vocab_items. No mistake log, no error taxonomy persisted, no mastery estimate, no placement, SRS only for explicitly saved words; ratings, read-aloud missed words, unknown_words metrics unused.

SERVER ANSWER-CHECKING INVENTORY: (a) objective_result: exact option-index equality, all questions required, ceil(75%) pass (curriculum lesson/test, topic reading/listening). (b) steps.submit_answer: per-question index equality, unlimited retries, step completes when every question attempted (even wrong). (c) submit_writing: word bounds + case-insensitive substring must_include; completes even if missing. (d) assess_productive: model rubric + quote-substring grounding + word bounds + language_is_dutch. (e) validate_corrections: model corrections, exact-unique-fragment application. (f) validate_meaning: model intent + quote substring + normalized anchor phrase containment + alias/negation regex. (g) apply_action: model-proposed action validated against authoritative slots/choices. (h) feedback points: evidence-handle + quote-substring. (i) story/video answer_questions: index equality, once only. (j) read_aloud_result: multiset token match of transcript vs paragraph. (k) vocab review: self-reported grade. Grammar practice and library story questions are graded only in the browser and never stored. No cloze, typed short-answer, dictation, ordering or matching grader exists.

DUPLICATION/CONSISTENCY: usage reserve/commit boilerplate in ~11 sites with differing failure policies; three _run error mappers and four error classes (reason vs detail); ProviderError -> 503 or 502 depending on router; stories/videos duplicate generation loop, _question_view, answer_questions, rate, retry, glossary save; two conversation engines; two word_count, three normalise, two _wav_seconds; lazy imports to dodge cycles; day boundaries differ (Brussels vs UTC).

**Strengths**

- Consistent 'model proposes, code decides' discipline: every model output is schema-validated and grounded (quote must be a substring of learner text in assess_productive, validate_meaning, feedback points, writing corrections, content review path+quote) before it affects state (curriculum/service.py:188-203, topic_conversations/workflow.py:69-86, feedback/service.py:180-196, curriculum/writing.py:45-80).
- Robust paid-call accounting: SQL-enforced per-learner daily/total limits with reserve -> commit/release around every provider call (usage/service.py:74-135) and explicit PAID_USAGE_ENABLED gate required in production (config.py:162-163).
- Idempotency and replay safety everywhere (request ids on sessions, turns, attempts, observations, reviews; payload fingerprints refuse conflicting replays) with pg row/advisory locks (coaching/service.py:43-69, topic_conversations/service.py:163-177).
- Durable Postgres job queue with leases, SKIP LOCKED, backoff, and at-most-once handling of interrupted paid calls (jobs/service.py:84-156; stories/service.py:219-222; content_review/service.py:159-162).
- Story/video generation already implements an evaluator-optimizer loop: generate -> deterministic level gate (validator.py) -> one rewrite with actionable feedback -> honest failure (stories/service.py:255-316).
- Append-only, content-versioned practice_observations log with failed_refs gives a clean event source for any future learner model or agent (coaching/models.py:14-36).
- Trilingual (nl/en/fa) content contract enforced by schema validators across curriculum, topics, library, conversations (topics.py:100-109, topic_conversations/schemas.py:86-95).
- Broad, behaviour-pinning test suite (~35 files) including concurrency, provider failures, allowance exhaustion and privacy (tests/test_*.py).
- Reusable linguistic utilities already exist: lemma/stem coverage, SM-2 scheduler, read-aloud token alignment, phrase anchor matcher.

**Findings**

| # | Severity | Finding | Evidence | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | high | **No unified learner model: level, skill state and history are fragmented and there is no placement** — Seven separate stores describe what the learner did (skill_records, curriculum_practice, topic_practice, curriculum_attempts, practice_observations, learning_days, vocab_items) with no aggregate of what the learner knows or gets wrong. There is no placement/diagnostic, no per-skill estimate, no current-stage field on the learner, and different features pick the stage differently (series stage, client-provided stage, passed attempts). Missions (all A2) and curriculum lesson practice do not write observations, so the coach cannot see them. | apps/api/src/dlp/domains/identity/models.py:13-27 (Learner has no level/goal fields); stories/service.py:55-58 default_stage returns 'a1' unless a final check was passed; stories/models.py:24 story_series.stage_id is the de-facto level; api/routes_coach.py:17 stage_id supplied by client; progress/models.py:16-32 skill_records per mission; curriculum/models.py:14-63 curriculum_practice/topic_practice keep only the latest response; coaching/models.py:14-36 observations only from topic_practice\|conversation (coaching/service.py:82) | Introduce a learner-profile read model (e.g. learner_profile + skill_state + mistakes tables) populated from an activity-event stream that every domain writes to (reuse record_observation's idempotent append pattern); add a short placement flow that sets the starting stage; make all features read the stage from the profile. |
| 2 | high | **Points, streak and daily goal ignore most practice; /today only knows stories, words and videos** — Completing a mission step, topic practice (any skill), a curriculum lesson skill, a guided conversation or requesting writing feedback earns zero points and does not count toward the streak. The daily plan never recommends topic practice, missions, conversations, the coach plan or the curriculum path, which matches the owner's complaint that it is unclear how the app helps them learn. | add_points callers only in stories/service.py:396,417,419,432,434,520, videos/service.py:493,514,516, stories/vocab.py:123; api/routes_stories.py:60-89 next_step is read\|choose\|review\|wait\|explore; stories/today.py:15-16 DAILY_GOAL=20 | Move points/streak out of the stories package into a shared progress/activity service; award points for every completed learning action (with anti-farming rules already used: once per request id); make /today a cross-domain plan that includes the coach's next activity, a mission or conversation, due words and the episode. |
| 3 | high | **Static practice banks are heavily templated and offer only 2 multiple-choice questions per receptive activity** — The '100 situations per skill' are largely variations of one template from A2 upward, each with two MC questions, which explains the 'limited and boring' feedback. The generated serial (stories) is the only non-templated content path. | content/practice/a2.json and b1.json: question prompt 'Welke uitleg past bij de aanpak in deze tekst?' appears in all 100 topics, listening 'Welke reactie beantwoordt het verzoek in de boodschap?' in all 100, speaking prompt prefix identical for all 100 (verified by reading the JSON); c1/pre-c2/c2 share all 100 reading texts; content/library/a2.json has 1 distinct story question across 100 stories; topics.py:27 questions min_length=2; docs/DECISIONS.md:189-191 and docs/STATUS.md:26-27 acknowledge templating | Generate per-learner exercise sets with the existing validator gate (validate_draft + StageProfile) and the content_review agent for QA, or regenerate banks offline; add more questions and item types per activity. |
| 4 | high | **Exercise variety on the server is limited to MC index checks, rubric-graded free production, word-bag read-aloud and self-graded flashcards** — There is no cloze/gap fill, typed short answer with normalised comparison, word ordering, matching, dictation (listen and type), conjugation/article drills or minimal pairs. Vocabulary review never tests recall (the learner grades themselves). Story/video questions can only be answered once with no retry or explanation beyond the evidence sentence. | curriculum/service.py:151-171 objective_result; practice/steps.py:99-129 submit_answer; practice/steps.py:205-231 submit_writing; curriculum/service.py:206-246 assess_productive; stories/service.py:493-508 read_aloud_result; stories/vocab.py:84-124 self-graded review; stories/service.py:400-420 and videos/service.py:497-517 answer once | Add an exercise engine with typed item kinds and deterministic graders (reuse library.normalise / topic_conversations.schemas.contains for accent/case-insensitive comparison, lemma_key for inflection tolerance), escalating to the grounded model rubric only for open answers; let vocab review use typed recall or MC from glossary data. |
| 5 | medium | **Mistakes are not captured as structured, reusable data** — The richest error signals (writing corrections by category, criteria not met with feedback, missed read-aloud words, wrong comprehension answers, mission feedback 'error' points with quote+correction) are either discarded or buried in heterogeneous JSON. Nothing schedules review of mistakes. | curriculum/writing.py:89-151 returns corrections (with category taxonomy) but nothing is stored; routes_curriculum.py:158-167; stories/service.py:521-523 stores only matched_words/target_words, not missed words; stories/service.py:413 wrong story answers only inside episode.answers JSON; feedback/service.py:194-203 error points only inside feedback_reports.report JSON; curriculum/service.py:273-279 save_practice overwrites evidence and records no observation | Persist a learner_mistakes (or error_events) table: source, stage, skill, category, learner quote, correction, item ref, created_at; feed it into SRS (vocab_items or a generalised review_items) and into the coach/agent context. |
| 6 | medium | **Spaced repetition covers only words the learner explicitly saves** — 500+ library words and ~37 core lesson words per stage, story/video glossary terms the learner did not tap, missed read-aloud words and grammar items never enter review. 'Learned' is just interval>=21 days on a self-graded card. | stories/vocab.py:1-6 docstring and save_word (47-65); stories/models.py:76-102 vocab_items; library.py:16-22 LibraryWord never scheduled; curriculum schemas Vocabulary (schemas.py:58-62) never scheduled | Generalise vocab_items into review items (word, phrase, grammar point, mistake) with automatic enrolment from lesson vocabulary and observed errors; keep the existing SM-2 schedule() as the scheduler. |
| 7 | medium | **Adaptive signals are stored but never used** — Story and video generation adapts only to the series stage and saved words; it ignores ratings, comprehension accuracy, read-aloud misses, mistakes and topic practice history. | stories/service.py:444-446 and videos/service.py:520-522 rate() store rating, never read; stories/service.py:230-236 writer gets saved[:60] + 140 random stage words; stories/service.py:109-118 pick_theme is a seeded shuffle; stories/validator.py:231-234 metrics.unknown_words stored in episode.checks only | Feed a compact learner profile (weak skills, recent mistakes, liked/disliked themes, due words) into writer_messages/script_messages and into theme selection. |
| 8 | medium | **GET requests trigger writes and paid background generation** — Opening the home page queues a chat_strong episode job (temperature 0.7, up to two paid calls). This is intentional and tested, but it conflicts with the module's own contract and makes cost depend on page views. | api/routes_stories.py:1 docstring 'Reads are free' vs routes_stories.py:60-63 -> stories/service.py:370-372 today_episode -> ensure_buffer (178-189) -> queue_episode -> jobs.enqueue (159-160); videos/service.py:470 library() calls stories.ensure_series; routes_missions.py:58 GET creates skill_records; pinned by tests/test_stories.py:164-170 | Keep the 'write ahead' behaviour but move it to an explicit POST or a scheduled job per learner; make GETs side-effect free. |
| 9 | medium | **Paid-call boilerplate duplicated with inconsistent failure policy** — On ProviderError some sites release reservations (assess_productive, writing_feedback, feedback, mission turns) while others commit the estimate as possibly billed (coach, conversations, stories, videos, content review, translate). Token estimates use //2 in some and //3 in others. Paid-flag checks appear in conversations, coach, stories, videos, content review, translate but not in assess_productive, writing_feedback, feedback or mission turns. | reserve/commit/release blocks in curriculum/service.py:226-246, curriculum/writing.py:129-151, coaching/service.py:300-319, topic_conversations/service.py:212-230, stories/service.py:255-287 and 473-485, videos/service.py:187-216, content_review/service.py:199-236, feedback/service.py:160-174, practice/turns.py:156-188, speech/service.py:57-67 | Create one paid_model_call(session, settings, learner_id, kind, estimate) context manager/decorator with a single documented policy, used by all domains and by any future agent tool. |
| 10 | medium | **Stories and videos duplicate the generation pipeline and learner actions** — Videos import stories internals (videos/service.py:36-39) and copy their logic; any change to grading/points must be made twice. | stories/service.py:214-316 vs videos/service.py:169-235 (reserve -> commit -> reload -> complete -> validate -> retry); _question_view stories/service.py:326-334 == videos/service.py:420-428; answer_questions stories 400-420 vs videos 497-517; rate 444-446 vs 520-522; retry_failed 449-458 vs 536-549; glossary save stories/vocab.py:68-74 vs videos/service.py:525-533 | Extract a 'generated lesson' module (generate-validate-retry loop, questions, glossary, rating, points) used by both. |
| 11 | medium | **Two different conversation engines with different contracts and very little content** — Role-play is the most engaging feature but exists as 4 A2 missions and 10 scripted pilots; the bank schema hard-blocks adding an 11th conversation. LangGraph is used only as a linear pipeline. | practice/workflow.py:58-131 (2 model calls, model-written reply for appointment scenarios); topic_conversations/workflow.py:89-126 (1 call, authored replies only); both build and compile a linear StateGraph per call with no conditional edges; topic_conversations/schemas.py:103 ConversationBank requires exactly 10 conversations; content/missions/* are 4 missions, all cefr_target A2 | Unify into one role-play engine (scenario + goals + code-validated state + optional model-phrased reply anchored on authored lines), relax max_length=10, and generate/validate new scenarios per stage. |
| 12 | medium | **Inconsistent error handling across routers** — Same failure produces different status codes and body shapes depending on the feature; some responses include request_id, others not. | routes_curriculum.py:54-72 _run (ProviderError -> 503, 55P03 -> 409); routes_stories.py:37-48 and routes_videos.py:29-38 separate _run copies without OperationalError mapping; routes_practice.py:236-239, 279-282, 420-423 and routes_speech.py:39-42 map ProviderError -> 502; error classes PracticeError(reason) practice/service.py:16-20, CurriculumError(detail) curriculum/service.py:41-45, VocabError(detail) stories/vocab.py:26-30, AudioError(reason) | Define a DomainError base with status/detail/code and register FastAPI exception handlers once in main.py; return a uniform error envelope. |
| 13 | low | **Read-aloud points are only awarded if the first attempt reaches 50%** — A learner who improves on a second try never earns the read-aloud points for that paragraph, which discourages retrying. | stories/service.py:515-520 'if not previous and share >= 0.5'; previous is set after any attempt (521-523); docs/DECISIONS.md:215-216 says points are awarded once per paragraph when at least half the words are recognised | Track an 'awarded' flag per paragraph and award on the first attempt that crosses the threshold. |
| 14 | low | **Read-aloud upload is read unbounded** — transcribe_upload rejects oversize later (speech/service.py:41-42) but the whole body is read into memory first. | api/routes_stories.py:180 audio.file.read() vs routes_speech.py:29 and routes_practice.py:265 read(settings.max_upload_bytes + 1) | Use the same bounded read as the other speech routes. |
| 15 | low | **Owner export omits stories, word bank, learning days and videos** — The newest and most-used learner data is missing from the portability export. | api/routes_progress.py:47-110 (no story/vocab/video/learning_days queries); stories/service.py:528-533 export() exists but is not wired | Include stories.service.export, vocab all_items, learning_days and video_view rows in /export. |
| 16 | low | **'Completed' does not mean correct in several flows** — Intentional per docs, but progress indicators built on 'completed' overstate learning and give no mastery signal. | practice/steps.py:121-124 step completes when every question was attempted (answered dict includes wrong answers); practice/steps.py:218-231 writing completes with missing must_include words; curriculum/service.py:270-271 and topics.py:241 productive practice completed=True on any substantive attempt | Expose separate attempted / correct / mastered states in a unified progress API so the UI can show real progress. |
| 17 | low | **Grammar practice and library story questions are graded only in the browser and never stored** — Grammar performance (one MC item per grammar topic) is invisible to the server and cannot feed any learner model. | apps/web/components/CurriculumControls.tsx:17 compares answers to answer_index client-side; apps/web/components/CurriculumLesson.tsx:88 grammar section; curriculum/service.py:136 stage_view returns stage.model_dump(exclude={'test'}) including lesson/grammar answer keys; library.py:156-165 story_detail returns answer_index | Route grammar items through a server grading endpoint that records observations/mistakes. |

Relevant to latency and to any tool-calling agent design. | providers/chat_openai_compatible.py:84-88 schema_instruction, 175-184 response_format {'type':'json_object'}, 98-106 sync httpx.Client and BoundedSemaphore(max_concurrent=2) | Evaluate strict json_schema structured outputs / tool calling on the Azure OpenAI deployment and streaming for role-play replies (verify model support first). |

**Constraints this area must respect**

- Model proposes, code decides: models may interpret, phrase or suggest, but never award passes, change objective marks, select a learner, replace a transcript, or advance state without code validation (curriculum/service.py:1-5; docs/DECISIONS.md D-12, D-18; docs/LEARNING_AGENTS.md:39-57).
- Four skills stay separate; no merged score, no CEFR certification claim, no pronunciation scoring (curriculum/service.py:33-38 POLICY; practice/acceptance.py:42-43 no_score_or_level_stored; DECISIONS D-16, D-20).
- Typed input never counts as speaking practice (practice/turns.py:253-257; acceptance A03).
- Points reward doing and are never a judgement of the learner's Dutch (stories/today.py:1; DECISIONS D-19).
- Every paid provider call must be reserved against usage counters first; production requires Azure providers and PAID_USAGE_ENABLED=true (usage/service.py; config.py:149-171).
- Replays/retries must never double-charge or double-score: request ids, client_turn_id, payload fingerprints (pinned in test_topics, test_coaching, test_topic_conversations, test_turns).
- All fixed and generated content must remain labelled unreviewed/generated (content_status fields; content/schemas.py LocalizedText.review_status).
- Trilingual support nl/en/fa with Persian RTL help ladder (content/schemas.py:69-83; curriculum/schemas.py:15-18); Belgian Standard Dutch in all prompts (stories/prompts.py:12-18).
- Coach must need at least two assessed attempts before labelling repeated difficulty, ignore stale content versions, and the learner may ignore any plan (LEARNING_AGENTS.md:53-57; tests/test_coaching.py:56-102).
- Content review is admin-only and never publishes (routes_content_review.py:17-20; content_review/service.py:1).
- Tests pin: GET /today queues an episode on first visit (test_stories.py:164-170); story/video answers are graded once and re-answer is 409 (test_stories.py:258-291); SM-2 intervals 1->6->>=15 and 10-minute lapse (test_stories.py:123-136); read-aloud response has no 'score' key (test_stories.py:384); topic banks need >=100 distinct topics per skill (test_topics.py:62); exactly ten pilot conversations (test_topic_conversations.py:145); complete_once never retries (test_single_model_call.py:74).
- Background work runs in the in-process JobLoop on a warm 0.25 vCPU API (DECISIONS D-17); chat adapter concurrency is 2 per process (chat_openai_compatible.py:98).

**Open questions**

- Whether library story questions are graded only client-side in the web app (grammar practice is confirmed client-only in CurriculumControls.tsx; library story component not inspected).
- Whether the deployed Azure OpenAI model (STATUS.md mentions gpt-4.1-mini) is used for both chat and chat_strong in production and whether it supports strict json_schema structured outputs/tool calling (not verified in infra).
- Whether the learner's support language (Learner.support_language, default 'fa') is ever updated server-side; no route updating it was found in the API.
- Actual production usage limits (Bicep parameters may override config.py defaults of 200 model calls/day, 200k tokens/day, 1800 audio s/day, 300 video s/day).
- Whether the owner is willing to relax the 'no score/no level' policy to show mastery estimates, or wants estimates labelled as practice indicators only.
- Whether the platform is intended to remain single-learner (identity/models.py:14 'Release 0.1 has exactly one learner') or support multiple learners, which affects agent cost design.
- Whether the templated content banks should be regenerated offline, replaced by on-demand generation, or retired in favour of the serial and role-play.

### Model layer and agent readiness

HOW IT WORKS. Every model call goes through the `ChatModel` interface (apps/api/src/dlp/providers/base.py:48-92), which has two methods. `complete` retries. `complete_once` sends exactly one HTTP request. Its contract is at base.py:75-81 and apps/api/tests/test_single_model_call.py enforces it.

There is one real client, `OpenAICompatibleChatModel` (providers/chat_openai_compatible.py:91-243). It uses raw httpx with no openai SDK; pyproject only lists httpx and langgraph. The same client serves Ollama (`llama3.1:8b`) and Azure OpenAI.

On Azure it uses Chat Completions only. With `AZURE_CHAT_API_VERSION=v1` the URL is `{endpoint}/openai/v1/chat/completions` (chat_openai_compatible.py:43-48; Bicep sets v1 at infra/main.bicep:344). Authentication is a managed-identity bearer token (scope `https://ai.azure.com/.default`, registry.py:41-54) or an api-key.

The request body is: model, messages, temperature and `max_tokens`. When a schema is given, it also adds `response_format: {type: json_object}` and a system message with the Pydantic JSON schema (`schema_instruction`, :84-88, :174-184). The body never contains tools, `stream`, a strict `json_schema` or a seed. `ChatMessage.role` allows only system, user or assistant (base.py:12).

On the reply side, `extract_json_object` pulls out the JSON and Pydantic validates it. `complete` then:
- repairs schema errors by appending the bad reply plus the error (at most `CHAT_MAX_ATTEMPTS=3`);
- doubles `max_tokens` when `finish_reason=length`;
- retries 408/409/425/429/5xx with jittered backoff;
- fails fast on ConnectError.

A BoundedSemaphore per model instance limits concurrency. Token usage is read from the final response only.

There are two tiers: `providers.chat` (small) and `providers.chat_strong`. The strong tier falls back to small; Bicep points STRONG at 'chat-small' when no strong model is configured (main.bicep:346). The live model is gpt-4.1-mini (docs/STATUS.md:10). `FixtureChatModel` answers by prompt_version using keyword rule tables (fixtures.py:33-88). Prompts are inline Python strings with version tags; there are no template files.

INVENTORY (feature | entry | prompt | output contract | validation | model calls per user action | state | failure handling):
1. **Mission role-play turn** | practice/workflow.py:run_turn, called from practice/turns.py:submit_turn (POST /practice/sessions/{id}/turns[/speech]) | Dutch `propose_action_messages` (propose-action-v3: scenario, slots, appointment state, last 6 turns), then `character_reply_messages` (character-reply-v1: persona, phase anchor `fixed_line`, code decision note) | `ProposedActionReply` → `CharacterReply{reply_nl}` | `apply_action` in code (practice/actions.py:93-188); `_contradicts_decision` keyword guard; service scenarios always use fixed lines | 2 logical calls via `complete` (1 for service scenarios), each up to 3 HTTP requests; reserves 2 calls / 1800 tokens | PracticeSession.state.appointment / step_progress; PracticeTurn.model_meta (prompt versions, tokens, latency) | If the first call fails, the turn is marked failed and returns 502 with reservations released. If the reply call fails, the fixed line is used.

The partner reply is always authored text (hint / cue / accepted + next cue / success). | 1 `complete_once` call (0 for hint/repeat), 400 output tokens | TopicConversation.history JSON, step_index, assisted_steps; a PracticeObservation on finish | Commits a conservative usage estimate, does not add the turn, returns 503.

Any violation rejects the whole reply. | 1 | Not persisted | 503.
