11. **Offline benchmark** | benchmarks/language/harness.py | Generic prompt (benchmark-v1), 40 cases | — | — | — | — | Not tied to the production prompts.

CLASSIFICATION. On the spectrum from single prompted call → validated generation pipeline → tool-using agent → multi-agent, the system sits at **validated generation pipeline**, on purpose:
- Features 3, 4, 5, 7, 9 and 10 are single calls with a post-hoc validator.
- Features 1, 2, 6 and 8 are fixed pipelines. Features 1 and 2 are LangGraph graphs with three linear nodes, no conditional edges, no ToolNode and no checkpointer (practice/workflow.py:123-131; topic_conversations/workflow.py:118-126). Features 6 and 8 are generate → deterministic validator → one rewrite loops.

Nothing is a tool-using agent: the client has no tool schema or tool role. Nothing is multi-agent. docs/LEARNING_AGENTS.md calls these "roles", and says they have "no open tool loop".

WHERE AGENTS WOULD HELP:
- (a) **A tool-using study guide** using existing deterministic functions as tools: candidate_plan, history_page, vocab.due_items, today.streak, stories.today_episode, videos.today_summary, topics.topic_page, library.vocabulary_page. This replaces today's coach, which only reorders 3 items. It directly targets the owner's "confused at start".
- (a) **A generative conversation partner** in place of the canned cue repetition, anchored the way mission compose_reply already is.
- (a) **A multi-round writing / grammar tutor** with memory of recurring errors.
- (b) **Agents are the wrong tool for:** grading, final checks, apply_action, SRS, points and usage. The per-turn interpretation hot path should also stay a single call.
- (c) **Seams that already exist:** the ChatModel interface and tiers, the durable PostgreSQL job runner, usage reserve/commit/release with request-id deduplication, `paid_model_guard`, prompt version tags, validators, fixture rule tables, and LangGraph already installed.
- (c) **What is missing:** tool and function calling, strict structured outputs, streaming, a Responses API / reasoning-model path, LLM spans and per-feature metrics, evals built on the production prompts, a learner memory/profile, per-run agent budgets, and a lease heartbeat.

AZURE AI PROVISIONING. infra/main.bicep:237-265 provisions one `Microsoft.CognitiveServices/accounts` of kind 'OpenAI' with `disableLocalAuth`, deployments `chat-small` and an optional `chat-strong` (NoAutoUpgrade), and the role 'Cognitive Services OpenAI User' for the API identity. It also provisions Application Insights. There is no Foundry project, AI Search/vector store, or agent service. The API is a single replica (0.25 vCPU) running the job loop in-process.

**Strengths**

- Clean provider seam: application code depends only on ChatModel (providers/base.py) and a single registry decides local/Azure/fixture (providers/registry.py:57-77), so a new agent runtime or Responses-API adapter can be added behind one interface.
- Consistent 'models propose, code validates' discipline with explicit validators per feature (apply_action, validate_meaning, validate_ranking, resolve_handles, validate_productive_reply, validate_corrections, validate_draft, validate_reply) — exactly the guardrail layer an agent's tool results need.
- Grounding by quotation everywhere: model claims must quote exact learner text or content paths (topic_conversations/workflow.py:72-74, curriculum/service.py:197, feedback/service.py:183-193, content_review/service.py:66-68).
- Cost safety is engineered, not hoped for: SQL-enforced reserve/commit/release with request-id dedup (usage/service.py:104-172), conservative commit on uncertain failures for complete_once paths, PAID_USAGE_ENABLED gate, advisory-lock paid_model_guard (coaching/service.py:51-57).
- complete_once contract plus a dedicated test suite prevents silent multiplication of paid calls (base.py:65-89; tests/test_single_model_call.py).
- Durable PostgreSQL job runner with leases, idempotency keys and at-most-once 'running/generating' claims for paid background generation (jobs/service.py; stories/service.py:219-222; content_review/service.py:159-162).
- Prompt versions are stored with results (PracticeTurn.model_meta, FeedbackReport.prompt_version, StoryEpisode.prompt_version, VideoLesson.prompt_version) and fixture replies are keyed by version, enabling regression tests per prompt.
- Story/video writer already implements a validator-in-the-loop rewrite with actionable findings (stories/validator.py:238-263), a proven pattern to generalise.
- Prompt-injection framing ('untrusted quoted evidence, never instructions') is present in every learner-text prompt, plus a regex guard in validate_meaning.
- Privacy-aware telemetry: no prompts/bodies exported (observability.py:1), provider errors never forwarded (chat_openai_compatible.py:115-117).

**Findings**

| # | Severity | Finding | Evidence | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | high | **Conversation partner never speaks generatively; only 10 conversations exist** — Whenever an answer is not accepted, the partner repeats exactly the same authored cue. The model only classifies intent and quotes. Only 10 of the roughly 1,200 topic×stage situations have a conversation at all. This is the most likely cause of the owner's 'very limited and boring'. The mission role-play (practice/workflow.py) already has a generative compose_reply step anchored on a fixed line and guarded by `_contradicts_decision`, but the topic conversations do not use it. | apps/api/src/dlp/domains/topic_conversations/workflow.py:106-116 (compose returns step.hint / step.cue / accepted+next cue / success — all authored); topic_conversations/schemas.py:103 `conversations: list[Blueprint] = Field(min_length=10, max_length=10)`; docs/LEARNING_AGENTS.md:4-5 'ten now also have a short, turn-by-turn conversation'; MAX_TURNS = 6 (topic_conversations/service.py:23) | Add a bounded generative partner reply to topic conversations using the mission pattern: the model phrases the reply, the authored cue/accepted text is the anchor, and a contradiction guard plus fallback to authored text apply. This becomes a second call, so 'one complete_once per turn' in LEARNING_AGENTS.md must be consciously revised. Scale the number of blueprints with an offline generation pipeline (validator + content_review queue) instead of hand-writing them. Optionally let the partner call read-only tools (lookup a word in library_for(stage).vocabulary, give the next hint rung). |
| 2 | high | **The 'coach' is a paid reorder of three deterministic items and cannot explain how the app helps** — The owner felt confused at the start and unsure how the app helps them learn Dutch. Nothing in the LLM layer explains the path, adapts to stated goals, or links the features together: the word bank, story serial, videos, topic practice, missions and the curriculum path are all separate. The only 'coach' model call changes the order of three cards. The underlying deterministic signals already exist: observations, failed_refs, due words, streak, today's episode and video readiness. | apps/api/src/dlp/domains/coaching/service.py:289-331 (_rank_once: prompt 'Order these three existing Dutch practice activities'; PlanRanking exactly 3 ids, coaching/schemas.py:18-21); candidate_plan is pure code (160-221); apps/web/components/PracticeCoach.tsx:53 button 'Stem de volgorde op mij af' | Build a tool-using study-guide agent. It is the clearest case where an agent adds visible value. Expose read-only tools wrapping existing functions: coaching.candidate_plan / history_page, stories.vocab.due_items / stats, stories.today.streak / recent_days, stories.service.today_episode, videos.service.today_summary, curriculum.topics.topic_page / topic_detail, curriculum.service.catalogue. The agent produces a short natural-language plan and an onboarding explanation. Activity IDs and deep links are restricted to tool-returned IDs and validated in code (generalise validate_ranking). Keep a deterministic fallback, cache by history_version as CoachPlanRequest does, and cap steps and tokens per run. |
| 3 | medium | **Chat client cannot do tool calling, strict structured outputs, streaming or reasoning/Responses models** — An agent loop needs the following, and none of it exists today: - `tools` / `tool_choice` in the request and tool_calls parsed from the reply; - a tool role message to feed results back; - a ChatResult that can carry tool calls. The client also uses json_object mode with the schema pasted into the system prompt, rather than strict `response_format: {type: json_schema, strict: true}`. That makes the repair loop necessary. `max_tokens` and `temperature` block newer reasoning deployments. | providers/base.py:12 `Role = Literal["system", "user", "assistant"]`; chat_openai_compatible.py:174-184 body has only model/messages/temperature/max_tokens/response_format json_object; chat_openai_compatible.py:43-48 Chat Completions URLs only; docs/GO_LIVE.md:49-51 'This client requires Chat Completions, JSON mode, `temperature` and `max_tokens`. A reasoning-only deployment with different request semantics is not interchangeable.'; docs/ENGINEERING.md:156 'Streaming is not part of 0.1.' | Extend the interface without breaking callers: - add `tools: list[ToolSpec] \| None`, `tool_choice`, and a `ChatResult.tool_calls` field; - add a `tool` role to ChatMessage; - add an optional strict json_schema mode; - add an optional `stream()` iterator for learner-facing chat. Keep `complete_once` semantics for bounded paths. Optionally add a second adapter for the Azure Responses API (or `max_completion_tokens`) behind the same ChatModel. The rule that application code never imports a vendor SDK still holds. |
| 4 | medium | **Semantic validators sit outside the repair loop, so grounded-but-imperfect replies become 503s** — The provider's `complete` repair loop only fixes JSON/schema errors. Domain validation runs after usage has been committed. A single bad correction span therefore discards all five corrections, and the learner sees 'temporarily unavailable', even though the model answered and was paid for. This is a cheap win that does not need agents. | curriculum/writing.py:55-65 raises ProviderError on any non-unique or partial span, and 149-151 returns the whole reply as failed; curriculum/service.py:190-191 'assessment response did not cover the required criteria'; coaching/service.py:320-325 falls back; only stories/service.py:291-302 and videos/service.py:220-230 feed validator findings back to the writer | Generalise the story pattern into a reusable helper: generate → domain validator returns findings → one targeted retry with the findings → keep the best valid result. For writing, drop only the invalid corrections instead of rejecting all of them. Account for the extra call through usage.reserve. |
| 5 | medium | **Usage accounting undercounts retried calls; failure policies differ between legacy and complete_once paths** — With `complete` making up to 3 HTTP requests (plus truncation doubling), real token spend can be about 3× what is committed. A refused or failed call may still be billed, yet the legacy paths refund it. A multi-step agent loop would amplify this gap. | chat_openai_compatible.py:216-233 (token usage taken from final response only; attempts returned but earlier attempts' tokens dropped); feedback/service.py:173-174 commits 1 call regardless of result.attempts; practice/workflow.py:112-114 a failed compose_reply is not added to model_calls, so turns.py:186-188 commits fewer calls; legacy paths release on ProviderError (feedback/service.py:169-172, curriculum/service.py:238-241, writing.py:143-146, turns.py:177-179) while complete_once paths commit the estimate (topic_conversations/service.py:223-228); docs/LEARNING_AGENTS.md:76-77 'Legacy retrying workflows retain their existing limits.' | Accumulate input and output tokens across attempts in `_complete`, and commit `attempts` as model_calls. Apply the complete_once policy everywhere: commit the estimate on uncertain failure. For agents, reserve a per-run budget (max steps × estimate) under a run id and commit per step. |
| 6 | medium | **No LLM observability or production-prompt evals** — No telemetry shows per-feature model latency, tokens, attempts, validator rejections or fallback rates. There is no golden set for the production prompts (intent classification, rubric judgements, correction grounding), so neither prompt changes nor agent introduction can be measured. Episode ratings (StoryEpisode.rating) are stored but not used as an eval signal. | observability.py:1 'Never export bodies, prompts...'; observability.py:22-28 disables httpx/azure_sdk auto-instrumentation; only http.request spans exist (39-50); topic_conversations/service.py:236-241 history stores no model/prompt_version/tokens; writing.py returns an unpersisted dict; benchmarks/language/harness.py:39-46,116-120 uses its own generic prompt 'benchmark-v1', not propose_action_messages / messages_for / feedback_messages; benchmarks/language/README.md:13-15 dry runs are plumbing only | Add OpenTelemetry GenAI-style spans that carry only metadata: feature, prompt_version, deployment, input/output tokens, attempts, latency, validator outcome, reply_source. This fits the no-body rule. Extend benchmarks/language with per-feature case files that call the real message builders and validators, run them in CI against the fixture provider and on demand against Azure, and record the results in DECISIONS.md as the README already prescribes. |
| 7 | medium | **No learner memory or profile shared across features** — No component remembers recurring error categories, weak grammar points, preferred topics or the learner's stated goal. That is why every interaction feels stateless. An agent without memory would add little over today's pipelines. | practice/prompts.py:98,123 history[-6:]; topic_conversations/workflow.py:46 history[-2:]; coaching/service.py:292-293 sends only id/skill/title/basis/evidence_ids; writing.py:89-151 corrections not persisted; stories/service.py:230-237 is the only feature using learner data (saved words); docs/GO_LIVE.md:35-36 'no ... separate vector store' | Start with a deterministic, SQL-derived learner profile: - skills attempted and passed from PracticeObservation; - frequent failed_refs; - FeedbackReport point kinds; - writing correction categories, once persisted; - VocabItem lapses and due count; - the streak. Expose it as a read-only tool or prompt block. Only add an LLM-written summary later, validated and stored with a version. A vector store is not needed for this. |
| 8 | medium | **Usage caps are sized for one or two calls per action** — A tool-using agent with 3–5 rounds per message would exhaust 80 calls per day within a single study session. Reservations are hard-coded per feature rather than per run. | infra/main.bicep:43-49 dailyModelCalls=80, dailyTokens=80000 (production defaults); config.py:125-128 200 calls/200k tokens locally; practice/turns.py:27-28 MODEL_CALLS_PER_TURN=2, TOKENS_PER_TURN_ESTIMATE=1800; docs/DECISIONS.md D-17 'A budget alert is not a hard currency cap.' | Add per-feature or per-run budget policies (max_steps, max_tokens) reserved up front through usage.reserve, raise caps deliberately for the agent features, and surface the remaining allowance in the agent UI. Fill the PricingEntry table so `estimated_cost` (usage/service.py:194-200) becomes real. |
| 9 | medium | **Wrong place for agents: grading, progression, actions, scheduling, points** — Several functions must stay deterministic and outside any agent's write authority: - `apply_action` (practice/actions.py); - objective_result, validate_productive_reply and submit_attempt (curriculum/service.py); - SM-2 `schedule` (stories/vocab.py); - `add_points` / `streak` (stories/today.py); - usage reserve/commit; - content publishing (the review has no publishing authority). An agent here would trade reproducibility, auditability and cost control for no learner benefit. | docs/ENGINEERING.md:59-61 'Dates, availability, permissions, task completion, usage limits and submissions are decided in application code.'; docs/DECISIONS.md D-12 'The model interprets and phrases; it never decides.'; docs/LEARNING_AGENTS.md:53 'No helper awards a final-check pass, locks a stage or declares CEFR mastery.'; curriculum/service.py:3-4 'Models may propose language judgements, but cannot select a learner, replace an audio transcript or change objective marks.'; D-19 'Points reward doing and are never a judgement of the learner's Dutch.' | If agents are introduced, give them read-only tools plus narrowly scoped 'propose' tools whose effects go through the existing validators (for example, propose an activity id that is then validated). Never give them tools that write results, passes, points or schedules. |
| 10 | low | **Wrong place for agents: the per-turn interpretation hot path** — Interpreting a learner utterance is a classification task, and it is fast and cheap today. Wrapping it in an open tool loop would add latency and cost on every turn and make it non-deterministic, with no gain in understanding. | topic_conversations/workflow.py:95-96 a single complete_once with 400 tokens; docs/LEARNING_AGENTS.md:61-63 'A submitted conversation response uses one `complete_once` request with a 400-token output ceiling.'; tests/test_single_model_call.py:1 'New role invocations cannot silently multiply paid model requests.'; docs/VERIFICATION.md:42 live typed turn 0.9 s on gpt-4.1-mini | Keep interpret → validate as one bounded call. Put agentic behaviour beside it, in the study guide, the tutor Q&A or the writing coach, not inside it. |
| 11 | medium | **Agent opportunity: multi-round writing and grammar tutor** — Learners get one-shot corrections with no follow-up. They cannot ask 'why?', request an exercise on the same rule, or have the app remember that they repeatedly confuse word order. | curriculum/writing.py:20 MAX_CORRECTIONS = 5, single pass, not persisted; feedback/service.py:128-131 at most three points per step; stories/service.py:461-490 translation is the only on-demand help in the reader | Build a tutor agent, invoked deliberately by the learner, with tools for: - validate_corrections, applying spans only through code; - curriculum.library vocabulary_page / story_detail; - topics.topic_detail (language_focus, objectives); - stories.vocab.save_word; - a deterministic 'make a drill' that picks existing questions or topics. Answers must be grounded in the tool output, or labelled as generated as the episodes already are. Persist the correction categories as memory. |
| 12 | low | **Agent opportunity: writer self-checks before returning (story and video)** — The writer only learns about vocabulary and sentence-length problems after a full draft and a paid rewrite. Generation runs as a background job, so a few extra calls are tolerable. | stories/service.py:255-302 (at most 2 attempts, rewrite after validation); docs/VERIFICATION.md:42 the first live episode failed on sentence length; stories/validator.py:121-133 coverage(), 162-235 validate_draft() | Optionally expose validator functions as tools, for example `check_text(paragraphs)` returning coverage, unknown words and sentence lengths, so the writer can check itself before final output. Keep validate_draft as the final authority and keep the ≤2 final-draft budget, or explicitly revise D-18 ('two model calls per episode at most'). |
| 13 | low | **Background job lease is shorter than an LLM call, and the job loop is single-threaded** — A long writer call can exceed its lease. The at-most-once 'generating → failed interrupted' claim stops a duplicate paid call but can fail a healthy job if a second worker claims it. Story, video and review generation also block render polling and each other. Longer agent runs would make this worse. | config.py:137 job_lease_seconds=60 vs config.py:69,73 chat_timeout_seconds=120 and chat_max_attempts=3; jobs/service.py:84-156 has no lease heartbeat; jobs/service.py:167-201 one JobLoop thread processes jobs serially; docs/STORY_ENGINE.md:90-91 'A local 8B model takes one to three minutes per episode'; infra/main.bicep:376 minReplicas=maxReplicas=1 | Add lease heartbeat or extension around provider calls, or set the lease longer than the worst-case call budget. Consider a separate worker container or a concurrency of more than one for LLM jobs before running agent work in jobs. |
| 14 | low | **LangGraph is a dependency used only for fixed linear pipelines** — The framework that would host a real agent (conditional routing, tool nodes, persisted state) is already installed but none of its agent features are used. | pyproject 'langgraph>=1.0,<2'; practice/workflow.py:123-131 and topic_conversations/workflow.py:118-126 use START→a→b→c→END with no conditional edges, ToolNode or checkpointer; practice/workflow.py:151-157 compiles the graph on every turn | If agents are adopted, use LangGraph for the agent loop: conditional edges, tool nodes wrapping domain functions, and a PostgreSQL checkpointer for agent state. Compile graphs once at module load. Otherwise consider plain functions and drop the dependency. |
| 15 | low | **Concurrency semaphore is per model instance and held during backoff** — Effective concurrency can reach 2× MAX_CONCURRENT_MODEL_CALLS against the same Azure deployment when no strong model exists. A retrying call keeps its slot while it sleeps. | providers/registry.py:159-160 builds separate chat and chat_strong instances; chat_openai_compatible.py:105 BoundedSemaphore per instance; chat_openai_compatible.py:188-242 the `with self._semaphore` block includes time.sleep backoff | Share a semaphore per endpoint/deployment and release it during backoff sleeps. |
| 16 | low | **Writing corrections are neither idempotent nor persisted** — A client retry repeats a paid call. Corrections are lost, so they cannot feed a learner profile or an eval set. | curriculum/writing.py:131 `call_id = uuid.uuid4().hex` (no request-id dedup); routes_curriculum.py:158-167 returns the dict without storing it | Store corrections keyed by (learner, request_id, text hash) the way FeedbackReport and CoachPlanRequest are, and reuse them on retry. |
| 17 | low | **Mission prompts hard-code level A2** — This is consistent with today's four A2 missions, but it blocks reusing the mission engine or feedback for other stages. Story and video prompts, by contrast, are conditioned on StageProfile. | practice/prompts.py:103 '(niveau A2)', 135 'op niveau A2'; feedback/service.py:118-119 'op niveau A2'; content/missions/*/mission.json cefr 'A2' | Parameterise the level from the mission document or StageProfile (stories/bible.py PROFILES) when missions expand. |

**Constraints this area must respect**

- docs/ENGINEERING.md:57-58 — 'Application code depends on the narrow interfaces in `providers/base.py`, never on a vendor SDK.'
- docs/ENGINEERING.md:59-61 — 'Dates, availability, permissions, task completion, usage limits and submissions are decided in application code. A model may *propose* an action ... the code validates it'.
- docs/DECISIONS.md D-12 — 'The model interprets and phrases; it never decides.' The single call that both decides and replies was rejected 'because the reply would then have to be parsed to find out what the model "did", and the code could no longer be the authority'.
- docs/DECISIONS.md D-18 — 'The model proposes; a deterministic validator (`stories/validator.py`) decides' and 'two model calls per episode at most'.
- docs/LEARNING_AGENTS.md:47-48 — 'It has no open tool loop, arbitrary database tool, web access or ability to write lesson files.'
- docs/LEARNING_AGENTS.md:53 — 'No helper awards a final-check pass, locks a stage or declares CEFR mastery.'
- docs/LEARNING_AGENTS.md:61-63 — 'Browsing, starting a conversation, explicit hint/repeat actions and the default coach plan make no model call. A submitted conversation response uses one `complete_once` request with a 400-token output ceiling.'
- docs/LEARNING_AGENTS.md:97 — 'No new queue service or agent hosting resource is needed.' (Adding Foundry Agent Service would revise this.)
- apps/api/src/dlp/providers/base.py:75-81 complete_once contract, enforced by apps/api/tests/test_single_model_call.py ('New role invocations cannot silently multiply paid model requests.').
- docs/GO_LIVE.md:49-51 — 'This client requires Chat Completions, JSON mode, `temperature` and `max_tokens`. A reasoning-only deployment with different request semantics is not interchangeable.'
- docs/GO_LIVE.md:35-36 — there is no 'separate vector store' (also no Service Bus or Translator) in the Azure design.
- docs/DECISIONS.md D-17 — 'Paid use is switched on deliberately (`PAID_USAGE_ENABLED`), never by the presence of credentials' and 'A budget alert is not a hard currency cap.' The production config also requires Azure chat with managed identity (config.py:158-167).
- apps/api/src/dlp/observability.py:1 — 'Never export bodies, prompts, headers, full URLs or user identifiers.' Any LLM tracing must stay metadata-only.
- apps/api/src/dlp/domains/coaching/service.py:3-4 — 'Selection is ordinary code. Optional model ordering receives only three vetted activities and their observed outcomes, not a learner's free-form answers.'
- apps/api/src/dlp/domains/topic_conversations/schemas.py:115 — 'A model proposes meaning only; it cannot choose a reply or alter scenario facts.' (A generative partner would consciously revise this.)
- apps/api/src/dlp/domains/curriculum/service.py:3-4 — 'Models may propose language judgements, but cannot select a learner, replace an audio transcript or change objective marks.'
- docs/DECISIONS.md D-19/D-20 — points 'are never a judgement of the learner's Dutch'; 'No accent or pronunciation score is computed or shown'.
- docs/LEARNING_AGENTS.md:19-20 — 'Raw model names, prompt identifiers and evidence UUIDs stay out of learner-facing feedback.'
- docs/LEARNING_AGENTS.md:83-88 — the content reviewer receives authored content only (no learner data) and 'has no publishing authority'.
- Generated content must remain labelled as generated/unreviewed (docs/VIDEO_LESSONS.md 'Honesty'; docs/STORY_ENGINE.md:92-93).
- Usage caps in production default to 80 model calls and 80k tokens per day (infra/main.bicep:43-49). Any multi-step agent must fit within, or deliberately raise, these.

**Open questions**

- Which Azure model and deployment would host an agent? Live chat is gpt-4.1-mini (docs/STATUS.md:10) and the strong deployment parameters are empty in infra/parameters.example.json:18-19. The owner's private parameter values were not read.
- Does the chosen West Europe deployment support tool calling, strict json_schema and the Responses API? Not verified in this repo; GO_LIVE says availability is subscription-dependent.
- Would the owner accept moving from the provisioned `kind: 'OpenAI'` account (infra/main.bicep:237-243) to an Azure AI Foundry (AIServices) resource/project for Foundry Agent Service, or prefer an in-app LangGraph agent on the existing deployment? Not decided anywhere in the docs.
- Does the local Ollama `llama3.1:8b` handle OpenAI-style tool calls reliably enough for laptop parity? Not verified.
- What cost per study session is acceptable? The current caps (80 calls / 80k tokens per day in production) assume one or two calls per action.
- Privacy: may learner free text and a derived profile be sent to an agent and kept as memory? The observability policy forbids exporting bodies, and the docs say nothing about LLM memory retention.
- Can the Next.js `/api/[...path]` proxy pass streamed (SSE/chunked) responses to the browser? Not inspected in this area; only video byte-range streaming exists (routes_videos.py:121).
- Is the 6-turn / 3-goal conversation cap (topic_conversations/service.py:23; LEARNING_AGENTS.md:16-17) a pedagogical decision the owner wants to keep, or a cost bound that could be relaxed with a generative partner?

### Web app architecture

apps/web ("dlp-web" 0.2.0) uses Next 16.3.5, React/React-DOM 19.3.0, TypeScript 5.9.3 in strict mode, ESLint 9.39 with eslint-config-next 16.3.5 (core-web-vitals + typescript), jose 6.2.12, @fontsource-variable DM Sans + Vazirmatn, Playwright 1.56.0 and @axe-core/playwright 4.13. Those versions come from package.json and package-lock. There is no UI library, animation library, data-fetching library, i18n library, CSS framework or formatter. `server-only` is imported but is not in package.json or the lockfile.

**Request path.** The browser only calls the same-origin `/api/*`. `app/api/[...path]/route.ts` is a Node-runtime catch-all BFF. For each request it:
- picks the request id;
- resolves the principal with `lib/server/identity.ts::resolvePrincipal` (a localhost-only fixture in development, or Azure Easy Auth `X-MS-CLIENT-PRINCIPAL` headers);
- enforces CSRF (`X-Requested-With: fetch` on unsafe methods, plus Sec-Fetch-Site and Origin);
- signs a 60-second HS256 assertion (`lib/server/assertion.ts::issueAssertion`: iss dlp-web, aud dlp-api, jti);
- forwards an allow-list of headers to `API_INTERNAL_URL` with a 6 MB body cap;
- streams the upstream body back.

`proxy.ts` is Next 16's renamed middleware. It sets a per-request CSP nonce (`script-src 'nonce' 'strict-dynamic'`, `connect-src 'self'`, `font-src 'self'`), `no-store` caching and HSTS in production. `next.config.ts` adds the security headers and an opt-in `output: standalone`. The Dockerfile is a two-stage node:22-alpine build that runs as a non-root user.

**Rendering and data.** `app/layout.tsx` is `force-dynamic` (needed for the nonce). It wraps everything in the client `LanguageProvider` and `AppShell`. Every route `page.tsx` is a 4–11 line async server component that only awaits params and renders one client component. 41 of 42 components are `"use client"`; only `Icon.tsx` is not. There is no server-side data fetching, no `loading.tsx`, no Suspense or streaming, no Server Actions and no `generateMetadata`.

Each feature component fetches on mount with `useEffect` + `AbortController` + `useState`, using `lib/client/api.ts` (`apiFetch`/`apiJson`/`ApiError`/`newRequestId`). `apiFetch` also fires a `dlp:connectivity` window event, which `ConnectionBanner` consumes. Shared cache, deduplication and invalidation do not exist. Polling is hand-rolled with `setTimeout` in TodayHome, VideoLibrary and ContentReviewQueue. Retries are `attempt`/`revision` counters. Stale-response guards use a "result keyed by JSON.stringify(params)" pattern, chosen to satisfy the project rule "no state-setting inside effect bodies" (docs/ENGINEERING.md:98).

**State.** State is local `useState`, with up to 24 hooks in one component. Cross-cutting state uses module singletons with `useSyncExternalStore`:
- the language mode in localStorage;
- `PhraseAudioController`;
- the `audio-focus` recording lock.

A React context (`lib/client/navigation.ts`) lets steps register a leave-guard. AppShell intercepts link clicks so writing autosave finishes before navigation. Drafts, test attempts, conversations and read-marks persist in sessionStorage/localStorage under `taalstudio.*` keys. The concurrency engineering is careful: idempotent request ids reused on retry, `DraftSaver` serial writes, `MicrophoneRequestGate`, a single audio focus, and focus moved after commit.

**Types.** Types are hand-written and scattered:
- `lib/types.ts` (header says "release 0.1"; covers missions and practice);
- `lib/client/{curriculum,topics,learning-agents,stories,videos}.ts`;
- many inline component types.

`LearningCopy` is defined in a component (`LanguageSupport.tsx`) and imported by lib files. There are three different multilingual shapes. FastAPI exposes `/openapi.json` only in development, and its routes return untyped `dict` with no `response_model`, so generating types from OpenAPI would yield empty response schemas today.

**i18n.** Interface chrome is hard-coded Dutch JSX. Translatable learning text uses `<LearningText text={{nl,en,fa}}/>`, with 51 inline literal objects spread across components and no message catalogue. Persian RTL is applied per span with `lang="fa" dir="rtl"` and the `.fa` class. `<html lang="nl">` is fixed. The support mode defaults to nl-en on the server, so it flashes for Persian users. The formal register (u/uw) appears in 9 files, while the rest uses je/jij.

**Styling.** Ten global plain-CSS files (~2,845 lines, ~105 KB) are all imported in the layout, so they load on every route. `:root` tokens are redefined four times with conflicting values:
- globals.css: green accent;
- design.css: another green;
- learning.css: blue accent, which wins by import order;
- stories.css: warm tones.

`.button` is defined three times. The CSS uses 356 unique hex colours, ~61 font-size declarations of 7–10px, one @keyframes, no dark mode and about 35 apparently unused legacy classes. docs/ENGINEERING.md:276 records a deliberate "restrained navy and blue" palette.

**Component size.** `wc -l` understates size because JSX is written as very long single lines; byte size is the better measure.

| Component | Lines | Bytes | Longest line (chars) |
|---|---|---|---|
| SpeakingStep | 464 | 20.8 KB | — |
| SkillTopicPractice | 186 | 19.6 KB | — |
| TopicConversation | 189 | 19.0 KB | — |
| LearningLibrary | 128 | 15.5 KB | — |
| LessonShell | 371 | 15.4 KB | — |
| EpisodeReader | 168 | 14.8 KB | — |
| TodayHome | 173 | 13.9 KB | — |
| CurriculumLesson | 100 | 13.8 KB | 1,597 |
| CurriculumTest | 115 | 13.5 KB | 3,274 |
| CurriculumControls | 163 | 12.5 KB | — |
| VideoPlayer | 162 | 12.1 KB | — |
| VideoLibrary | 142 | 10.4 KB | — |

All components total 5,130 lines and 323 KB. design.css alone is 1,711 lines.

**Information architecture.** The shell has nine nav items, six on mobile. Overlapping features: a generated serial (/verhalen) versus a static "Story Time" inside /learn; SRS words (/woorden) versus a stage word bank; missions versus topic conversations. Level is chosen in four places with different defaults. There is no onboarding.

**Tests.**
- 6 node:test unit files over pure lib modules (run with `--experimental-strip-types`).
- 18 Playwright specs with ~80 tests: Chromium only, 1 worker, real API stack via `scripts/run.py e2e`, many `page.route` mocks.
- Axe on 5 top screens and the mission lesson.
- Screenshots are attached but never compared.

`tsconfig` excludes `tests`, so specs are not typechecked. There are no component or hook tests. CI runs lint, typecheck, unit tests and e2e.

**Strengths**

- Up-to-date stack: Next 16.3.5 (uses new proxy.ts and async params), React 19.3.0, TS 5.9 strict, ESLint 9 flat config with Next core-web-vitals + typescript rules.
- Strong security boundary: BFF proxy strips client identity, CSRF via custom header + Sec-Fetch-Site/Origin (route.ts:46-78), short-lived signed HS256 assertion with jti (assertion.ts:15-39), server-only modules, per-request nonce CSP with strict-dynamic (proxy.ts:5-31), HSTS in prod, X-Frame-Options DENY.
- Careful concurrency/resilience engineering: idempotent request ids reused on retry (LessonShell.tsx:96-176, SpeakingStep.tsx:181-185, SkillTopicPractice.tsx:141-143), AbortController everywhere, DraftSaver serial autosave (draft-saver.ts), MicrophoneRequestGate against late permission results, global audio focus + recording lock (audio-focus.ts), navigation guard waiting for autosave (AppShell.tsx:71-103).
- Response streaming through the proxy (route.ts:128 returns upstream.body) — compatible with future SSE/streamed agent replies.
- Accessibility basics present: skip link + focusable main (layout.tsx:43, AppShell.tsx:179), :focus-visible outline (design.css:59-62), lang/dir on Persian spans, aria-pressed/aria-current usage, focus moved to new content after commit (SkillTopicPractice.tsx:124, TopicConversation.tsx:102), axe WCAG 2.1 AA checks and no-horizontal-overflow checks at 320/390/1440 in CI (design.spec.ts:37-80), prefers-reduced-motion respected (design.css:1684).
- Honest-learning guardrails encoded in UI copy (no fake scores, synthetic voice labelled, unreviewed content labelled via ContentLabel).
- Pure, unit-tested client logic for the trickiest parts (phrase-audio, draft-saver, microphone gate, audio focus, sentence split, alphabet).
- Typed endpoint-client objects already exist for newer features (`stories`, `words` in stories.ts:50-85, `videos` in videos.ts:25-37) — a good pattern to generalise.
- Production-ready container: standalone output, non-root user, telemetry disabled, health route with commit SHA (app/health/route.ts).

**Findings**

| # | Severity | Finding | Evidence | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | high | **Server components unused: every page is a client island that fetches on mount (no RSC data, no Suspense/loading.tsx)** — Every first paint shows a text placeholder ('Je dag voorbereiden…', 'Je leerpad laden…' – class .path-loading), then a browser→Next→FastAPI round trip. The server already knows the principal and can sign assertions, but no server-side fetch helper exists. No skeletons or streaming, and dynamic routes have no metadata (learn/[stageId]/page.tsx, missions/[missionId]/page.tsx, speech-check/page.tsx lack titles). | app/page.tsx:4-5, app/leerpad/page.tsx:4-5, app/videos/page.tsx:4-5 (thin wrappers); 41/42 components start with "use client" (only components/Icon.tsx is not); TodayHome.tsx:27-30, CurriculumPath.tsx:15-19, VideoLibrary.tsx:62-65 fetch in useEffect; no loading.tsx files exist; layout.tsx:37 force-dynamic | Add lib/server/api.ts. It should read headers() in server components, call resolvePrincipal + issueAssertion, and fetch FastAPI directly. Fetch initial data in async page.tsx and pass it into client islands as props/initialData. Add per-segment loading.tsx skeletons and Suspense boundaries, plus generateMetadata for episode/video/stage titles. Keep the /api proxy for mutations, audio and polling. |
| 2 | high | **Ad-hoc fetching with no cache, dedupe or invalidation; repeated endpoints, waterfalls and three hand-rolled pollers** — Each component reimplements loading/error/retry/abort/stale-guard state. That causes duplicated network traffic, inconsistent freshness (e.g. after saving a word, Today's counts are stale until reload) and large components. The key-matching pattern (SkillTopicPractice.tsx:32-34, LearningLibrary.tsx:59-60) exists only to satisfy lint rules that a query library handles natively. | 'curriculum' catalog fetched separately by CurriculumPath.tsx:17, CurriculumProgress.tsx:19, CurriculumTest.tsx:43, ContentReviewQueue.tsx:28; LessonShell.tsx:45-56 runs mission→progress→sessions→each session detail sequentially; lib/client/learning.ts:38 always fetches hard-coded 'missions/appointment-change' (used by Settings/Progress only for profile); polling in TodayHome.tsx:10-37, VideoLibrary.tsx:11-72, ContentReviewQueue.tsx:62-66; StoryLibrary.tsx:18-22 shows pending episodes but never polls; UsagePanel.tsx:38-42 has no AbortController; topic-conversations catalog fetched in both TopicConversation.tsx:21 and :36 | Adopt TanStack Query v5 (or SWR) over apiJson. Use typed query-key factories per domain (curriculum, today, words, stories, videos, topics, coach), refetchInterval while status is queued/generating/rendering, invalidateQueries after mutations, and useMutation with optimistic updates (word save, rating, grade). Hydrate from RSC initialData. Parallelise LessonShell.loadMission with Promise.all. |
| 3 | high | **Hand-written, fragmented API types with no contract with FastAPI** — Drift between API and UI is caught only by e2e. Multilingual text has three shapes: LocalizedText {nl,en?,fa,review_status} in types.ts:6, LearningCopy {nl,en?,fa?}, and flat meaning_en/meaning_fa or text_nl/text_fa/text_en fields in GlossaryItem, WordItem and FeedbackPoint. lib imports a type from a component, which inverts the dependency direction. | lib/types.ts:1 ('release 0.1'); types also in lib/client/curriculum.ts:4-31, topics.ts:5-27, learning-agents.ts:4-30, stories.ts:5-43, videos.ts:6-23; inline types in LearningLibrary.tsx:12-16, WritingCoach.tsx:9-10, UsagePanel.tsx:7-20, StatusPanel.tsx:8-13, SpeechCheck.tsx:10-18, CurriculumProgress.tsx:10-11, ContentReviewQueue.tsx:9-12; LearningCopy defined in components/LanguageSupport.tsx:6 and imported by lib/client/curriculum.ts:1, topics.ts:1, learning-agents.ts:1; apps/api/src/dlp/main.py:75-77 (openapi only in development); API routes return `-> dict` with no response_model (e.g. routes_missions.py:49, routes_progress.py:28) | Add Pydantic response models to FastAPI routes, export openapi.json in CI, and generate TS with openapi-typescript (+ openapi-fetch typed client) into lib/api/schema.d.ts. Move LearningCopy/LocalizedText into lib/i18n or lib/types. Optionally validate at the boundary with zod. Short term: consolidate all hand types under lib/api/<domain>.ts. |
| 4 | high | **CSS architecture is a stack of global overrides: 4 conflicting :root token blocks, triple .button, 356 hex colours, tiny type** — Visual identity changed through successive layers ('restrained navy and blue', docs/ENGINEERING.md:276) instead of edits, so tokens are untrustworthy and colour is ad hoc. Very little motion or feedback styling exists, which matches the owner's 'boring/limited' impression. Skill colours exist as data (components/LearningOverview.tsx:7-42 sage/sand/lilac/peach, used only by ProgressOverview .icon-tile), and episode cover gradients exist (stories.css tone-1..6), but neither forms a system. | globals.css:1-18 (--accent #0b5d4b), design.css:2-17 (--accent #225b49, --radius 18px), learning.css:2-12 (--accent #2455b5, --radius 16px — wins by import order layout.tsx:10-19), stories.css:2-12 (warm + tone-1..6); .button at globals.css:144, design.css:409, learning.css:37; .card at globals.css:101 and design.css:855; body font-size 14px (design.css:19) then 15px (learning.css:13); font-size 7–10px declared ~61 times (e.g. .brand-caption 8px design.css:113 & learning.css:19, .eyebrow 10px learning.css:36); only one @keyframes (stories.css writing-dots); no prefers-color-scheme; ~35 apparently unused classes (mission-hero, art-*, hero-*, skill-card*, site-header, site-nav, studio-card, stage-locked…); all ~105KB CSS loaded on every route | Create one token source covering colour scales, semantic tokens, per-skill accent tokens (reading/listening/speaking/writing), a type scale in rem with a minimum of 12px, spacing, radius, shadow and motion durations/easings, plus a dark theme. Move components to CSS Modules (or Tailwind v4 @theme) and delete override layers and dead classes. Build primitives (Button, Card, Badge, Tabs, ProgressRing/Bar, Skeleton, Toast, Dialog, Chip) and add purposeful motion (Motion for React or CSS View Transitions) respecting reduced-motion. Update docs/ENGINEERING.md:276. |
| 5 | high | **God-components with multi-thousand-character JSX lines and no formatter** — Line counts hide the real size. Flows such as the final test (intro/in-progress/locked/results) and the conversation (restore/start/active/409/finished) are encoded as boolean soups across many useState hooks, which is hard to change safely during a UX redesign. | CurriculumTest.tsx:102 is a single 3,274-char line; CurriculumLesson.tsx:81-95 lines up to 1,597 chars; byte sizes: SpeakingStep 20.8KB/464 lines, SkillTopicPractice 19.6KB/186 lines (24 useState refs), TopicConversation 19.0KB/189 lines (17 useState, 7 useEffect), LearningLibrary 15.5KB (24 useState refs), LessonShell 15.4KB/371, EpisodeReader 14.8KB, TodayHome 13.9KB, CurriculumLesson 13.8KB, CurriculumTest 13.5KB, CurriculumControls 12.5KB, VideoPlayer 12.1KB; no prettier/biome/editorconfig in repo; 36 inline style={{}} occurrences | Add Prettier (or Biome) and reformat. Split by view state into subcomponents (e.g. TestIntro/TestWorkspace/TestResults; ConversationSetup/ConversationThread/Composer/Summary). Move flow state into useReducer or a small state machine, and extract hooks (useTopicDraft, useConversationSession, usePolling). |
| 6 | medium | **Heavy duplication of interaction primitives** — The same learning interaction looks and behaves differently across features: hold-to-talk versus click-to-record, and four different answer-check visuals. This contributes to the inconsistent, confusing feel and multiplies redesign effort. | Multiple-choice UIs: CurriculumControls.tsx:12-19, QuestionList.tsx:26-128, EpisodeReader.tsx:147-168, VideoPlayer.tsx:141-162. Recorder UIs: SpeakingStep.tsx:333-346 (hold), SpeechCheck.tsx:109-118 (hold), CurriculumControls.tsx:96-163 (click toggle), EpisodeReader.tsx:101-126 (toggle). Audio: playback.ts usePlayback, phrase-audio.ts, CurriculumControls.tsx:25-92. Glossary/Questions/rating/friendly() near-identical in EpisodeReader.tsx:13-20,128-168 and VideoPlayer.tsx:13-20,122-162. countWords in WritingStep.tsx:23 and CurriculumControls.tsx:94 with different regexes. skillNames in learning.ts:62 and curriculum.ts:33, SKILL_LABEL LessonShell.tsx:27, skills LearningOverview.tsx:7. Stage lists: practiceStages learning-agents.ts:31, stageLabels stories.ts:45, stageLabel() curriculum.ts:35. Paginated filtered list: SkillTopicPractice.tsx:20-69, LearningLibrary.tsx WordLibrary/StoryTime; pager x3 (LearningLibrary.tsx:50, SkillTopicPractice.tsx:66, ContentReviewQueue.tsx:127). **Constraints this area must respect**

- Browser must never call FastAPI directly. All browser traffic goes through same-origin /api with X-Requested-With: fetch on unsafe methods, and the proxy signs the assertion (docs/DECISIONS.md D-03/D-04; route.ts:46-104).
- CSP from proxy.ts:8-20: script-src nonce + strict-dynamic; font-src/connect-src/img-src 'self' (+data:/blob:); style-src allows 'unsafe-inline'. External CDNs (Google Fonts, hosted Lottie/animations, analytics) need CSP changes, and third-party scripts need the nonce. Root layout must stay dynamic for per-request nonces (layout.tsx:37, ENGINEERING.md:28-29).
- Project lint rule: no state-setting inside effect bodies (docs/ENGINEERING.md:98; eslint-config-next 16 react-hooks rules). New code must respect it; a query library would ease this.
- Primary app navigation must wait for writing autosave (D-17; AppShell.tsx:71-103, lib/client/navigation.ts). Any new shell/router must keep the leave-guard.
- No fictitious scores, certificates or pronunciation/accent scores; typed input never counts as speaking practice; read-aloud compares words only (D-16, D-17, D-20). Gamification (points/streaks/XP) must stay evidence-based and honest.
- Audio is never autoplayed or prefetched; playback requires an explicit click and one speaker at a time via audio-focus (unit tests phrase-audio.test.mjs:29, e2e phrase-audio.spec.ts, library-writing.spec.ts:92).
- Unreviewed or generated content must stay visibly labelled (ContentLabel, course-note copy); synthetic voices and the virtual presenter must be labelled.
- Dutch always remains the practice language; support lines are English and/or Persian with lang/dir set; Persian uses RTL and the Vazirmatn font (ENGINEERING.md:267-270).
- Idempotent request ids must be reused on retry for session/turn/test/story/video creation (LessonShell.tsx:96, SpeakingStep.tsx:148-185, CurriculumTest.tsx:69).
- Docs currently mandate a 'restrained navy and blue' design (ENGINEERING.md:276). A more colourful redesign is a conscious revision and must update the docs.
- E2E tests are tightly coupled to Dutch visible copy (413 role/text/label locators), 83 data-testid attributes and some CSS classes. UI/copy changes require coordinated test updates; keep data-testids stable where possible.
- Unit tests run with node --experimental-strip-types (Node >= 22.6, package.json engines). lib modules imported by them must stay free of React/Next imports and path aliases (they import ../../lib/...ts directly).
- Production identity is Azure Container Apps Easy Auth headers; sign-out is /.auth/logout (AccountSettings.tsx:90). Onboarding must work behind Easy Auth, with no custom login.
- Container build uses NEXT_OUTPUT=standalone (Dockerfile:8-10); no public/ directory exists today.

**Open questions**

- How `import "server-only"` resolves: it is not in package.json or package-lock (presumably Next's built-in alias); not verified by building.
- Actual client bundle sizes and route-level JS/CSS weight were not measured (no build was run, per read-only rules).
- Whether the API team will add Pydantic response models so OpenAPI-generated TS types become meaningful (routes currently return untyped dict); also whether /openapi.json can be exported in CI (it is enabled only in development, main.py:75-77).
- Whether the owner wants English/Persian as a full interface language (navigation, buttons) or only as support lines under Dutch content.
- Whether a server-side learner profile endpoint (level, goals, preferred support language) exists or is planned in the API; the web currently derives the level in four different places.
- The ~35 'unused' CSS classes come from a heuristic grep (dynamic class names such as tone-N, grade-*, kind-*, status-* were excluded manually); confirm before deleting.
- Whether streaming (SSE) responses from FastAPI are planned for agent/tutor features; the proxy streams bodies, but apiJson consumes only complete JSON.
- Whether Firefox/Safari (iOS) support matters to the owner; recording MIME candidates include audio/mp4, but e2e runs on Chromium only.
- Real-world latency of LLM story/video generation that drives the 5–6s polling cadence (TodayHome POLL_MS 5000, VideoLibrary 6000) and the stalled-state UX.

### Learner journey and information architecture

ENTRY AND SHELL. RootLayout (apps/web/app/layout.tsx:41-50) renders `<html lang="nl">` and wraps every page in LanguageProvider and AppShell. `/` (app/page.tsx:4-5) renders TodayHome directly. There is no welcome screen, onboarding, placement or level choice, goal setting or explanation of the method anywhere. No route or component exists for it, and the Learner table only has display_name, support_language and target_language (identity/models.py:13-27). 

AppShell (components/AppShell.tsx:14-46) shows nine sidebar destinations: Vandaag, Verhalen, Video's, Woorden, Mijn leerpad, Praktijkgesprekken, Mijn voortgang, Spraakstudio and Instellingen. The header has:
- a breadcrumb, "Leeromgeving > X", that adds no information;
- the LanguageSwitcher ("Taalhulp / language", with nl-en | nl-fa | nl-fa-en);
- a profile button that duplicates Instellingen.

Mobile shows 6 bottom tabs. Missions, Spraakstudio and Settings are not among them. All interface text is Dutch-only. English and Persian appear only where a component uses `<LearningText text={{nl,en,fa}}>` (LanguageSupport.tsx:35-43), so nav labels, headings, buttons, stat labels and the cast list are never glossed. The support mode lives in localStorage and defaults to nl-en (LanguageSupport.tsx:9-16), while the server default for support_language is "fa" (identity/models.py:23). Nothing writes the mode to the server.

FIRST VISIT. GET /today (api/routes_stories.py:60-89) runs `service.today_episode`, then `ensure_series`, then `ensure_buffer` (stories/service.py:370-379, 178-189). This queues episode 1 as a background job. `default_stage` maps pre-a1 to a1 (stories/service.py:55-58), so even an absolute beginner gets an A1 serial. 

What a real first-time user sees, step by step:
1. The heading "Goeiemorgen. Je volgende aflevering wordt geschreven."
2. A "De schrijver is bezig…" card saying "Dit duurt meestal één tot drie minuten" (TodayHome.tsx:66-75). It polls every 5 s for up to 4 minutes and then offers "Vernieuwen".
3. A streak card full of zeros, "0 woorden om te herhalen" and a "Vraag een video" card.
4. A "Vraag een verhaal" form with a CEFR dropdown, "Niveau van de verhalen".
5. A Dutch-only cast list, "Wie is wie in De Lindestraat".
6. Three link cards and an "unreviewed" course note.

The screenshots in docs/previews (today-desktop.png, today-phone.png) show the ready state with fixture data: "AFLEVERING 1 · A1 · 127 WOORDEN", "De sleutel van Sami", 0/20 points and 14 grey dots. They also predate the video card and the sixth mobile tab. So they flatter the real first visit.

DAILY LOOP. `next_step` is computed only from stories and words: read, then choose, then review, then wait, then explore (routes_stories.py:66-76). Points come only from these actions (daily goal 20, today.py:16):
- episode read: 10
- each correct answer: 3
- choice: 2
- read-aloud: 5 (stories/service.py:46)
- each word review: 1 (vocab.py:23,123)
- video watch: 10, each correct video answer: 3 (videos/service.py:55)

One episode with 3 correct answers meets the goal. Curriculum practice, topic practice, missions, conversations and Spraakstudio never award points, so a learner who works through the Leerpad keeps "0 / 20 punten" and loses the streak.

The episode reader (EpisodeReader.tsx) is the richest flow:
- audio per paragraph, with an English line under each one and Persian translation on request;
- "Lees hardop" read-aloud;
- a glossary with "Bewaar" that sends words to the SM-2 word bank;
- "Klaar met lezen", then 2-3 questions with evidence, then an A/B choice that steers the next episode, then a rating.

Saved words come back on /woorden (WordReview flashcards).

STRUCTURED COURSE: A SEPARATE WORLD. /leerpad (CurriculumPath.tsx) contains:
- "VOORGESTELD STARTPUNT", the first stage not yet passed (pre-A1 for a new learner, while the stories run at A1);
- a PracticeCoach with its own level dropdown (localStorage, default a1; PracticeCoach.tsx:11-16);
- 12 stage cards, all open: "Alle niveaus zijn open. Kies je startpunt…".

/learn/[stage] (CurriculumLesson.tsx) has a sidebar of 7-8 sections. It opens on "Woorden", a paginated static word library, even for pre-A1, where "Alfabet" is listed first. Each skill opens on a 100-topic picker ("Onderwerpen") with search, theme and status filters instead of the core lesson ("Startles"). Practising all 4 skills unlocks "Eindtoets". DECISIONS.md:189-190 itself admits the static topic and Story Time texts are templated and heavily reused, which matches the owner's "boring" verdict.

Missions (/missions, then LessonShell) are a third practice system with their own sessions and skill_records. A fourth sits inside Spreken: topic conversations.

/progress puts CurriculumProgress first (12 stages × 4 skills, each a text status such as "Nog niet geoefend"). Mission progress is hidden in a `<details>` with a hard-coded mission. It shows no streak, points, words, episodes or topic counts. /settings shows profile, JSON export, a diagnostics table and token/audio usage counters.

NET EFFECT for an adult beginner with English/Persian support:
- an all-Dutch interface;
- no story of how the pieces form a method;
- a waiting screen as the first experience;
- 3-4 level settings that disagree;
- 9 nav targets with overlapping concepts: two story systems ("Verhalen" and "Story Time"), two word systems, missions vs topic conversations;
- progress signals that count only the story loop;
- repeated honesty and technical notices;
- inconsistent formality (u vs je).

Visually, learning.css overrides earlier colour tokens with a "restrained" navy/blue palette (learning.css:1-12). Warm colour appears only on episode covers (stories.css:6-11) and the streak ring.

**Strengths**

- The episode loop is a genuinely good comprehensible-input design: per-paragraph TTS, English line under each paragraph, on-demand Persian translation, read-aloud with recognised and missed words, a glossary that feeds an SM-2 word bank, evidence-backed questions and an A/B choice that steers the next episode (EpisodeReader.tsx:36-82, 85-168).
- Today already has the bones of a daily plan: a server-computed next_step, streak, 20-point daily goal, 14-day history, due-word count, and video readiness in one call (routes_stories.py:60-89; TodayHome.tsx:48-55).
- Pre-generation keeps one unread episode buffered, so after the first visit the next episode is usually ready when the app opens (stories/service.py:178-189).
- There is a warm, colourful visual vocabulary in stories.css (six gradient cover tones, a warm streak ring, gradient goal bar) that the rest of the app does not yet use (stories.css:3-11, 49-70).
- Trilingual content infrastructure already exists: LearningCopy {nl,en,fa} everywhere in curriculum, topics and stage titles; Persian has proper lang/dir and the Vazirmatn font (LanguageSupport.tsx:35-43; layout.tsx:7-8).
- There is a beginner-ready, LLM-free day-one activity in AlphabetPractice: letter names, a listen-and-pick quiz and kat/maan contrasts, fully trilingual (AlphabetPractice.tsx:9-36). VocabularyRecall offers 5-word recall rounds (VocabularyRecall.tsx:10-40).
- The personalised practice coach (/coach/plan) already returns 3 suggestions with a trilingual reason per item (PracticeCoach.tsx:49-53; learning-agents.ts:25-30).
- Robust states: every list has loading, error and retry. Navigation is guarded while recording or saving (AppShell.tsx:71-103). An accessibility gate in e2e enforces axe WCAG 2.1 AA at three widths.
- Honest by design: no fictitious scores or certificates, and no pronunciation score (DECISIONS.md D-17, D-19, D-20). This is a strength to keep, but its presentation is currently noisy.

**Findings**

| # | Severity | Finding | Evidence | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | high | **No onboarding: the first visit lands on a waiting screen with no explanation of the method** — A brand-new adult beginner gets no welcome and is never asked their level, goal or support language. Nobody tells them how stories, word review, the leerpad, conversations and the eindtoets fit together. The very first thing they see is a model writing in the background, surrounded by zero-valued stats. The docs/previews screenshots show the ready fixture state, so this first-visit experience is invisible in the docs. | app/page.tsx:4-5 renders TodayHome with no first-run branch. A grep of the repo for onboard/welcome/placement/intake finds nothing in apps/web. Learner model has no level/goal fields (identity/models.py:13-27). GET /today queues episode 1 on the first call (stories/service.py:370-372, 178-189; README.md:91-93: "the first episode is queued on the first visit and takes one to three minutes"). The first screen is then TodayHome.tsx:53 headline "Je volgende aflevering wordt geschreven." plus TodayHome.tsx:70-71 "De schrijver is bezig… Dit duurt meestal één tot drie minuten." Polling: 5 s × 48 = 4 min (TodayHome.tsx:10-11, 33-37), then "Het duurt langer dan verwacht. Vernieuwen" (:73). Fallback copy at :95: "Je eerste aflevering komt eraan. Vernieuw zo meteen deze pagina." The only description of the method is the subtitle at :60, "Eén aflevering, een paar woorden, één gesprek. Twintig minuten volstaan." The 6-step learning loop in LEARNING_DESIGN.md:8-15 is never shown to the learner. | Add a first-run flow, either as a /welkom route or a TodayHome branch. Show it when the learner has no profile and no learning_days. Three short steps, each glossed via LearningText: 1. Choose support language (EN / FA / both). Persist it server-side via the existing Learner.support_language column plus a small PATCH /me endpoint. 2. Self-placement in plain words, e.g. "I know no Dutch", "I know some words", "I can have simple conversations", mapped to pre-a1 / a1 / a2. Store it as one learner level and also call POST /stories/level. 3. Daily minutes (5 / 10 / 20), which sets the goal target. End with a 4-card "How Taalstudio works" explainer (Read/listen → Save words → Review → Speak & write → Check), using the existing skills array icons and colours. While episode 1 is being written, start the learner on a 3-minute activity: AlphabetPractice for pre-A1, or a VocabularyRecall round from the stage library for A1. Pre-queue episode 1 at the start of onboarding so it is usually ready when onboarding ends. |
| 2 | high | **No single 'my level': three to four level controls disagree** — The learner never states a level. The app guesses differently per module and shows CEFR codes (pre-A1, A1, …) without explanation. A true beginner reads 127-word A1 stories on Today while the leerpad says they should start at pre-A1 letters and first words. | The level is set in four disconnected places: - Stories: default_stage maps pre-a1 to "a1" (stories/service.py:55-58). Today shows a select "Niveau van de verhalen" (TodayHome.tsx:139-144). The screenshot today-desktop.png shows "AFLEVERING 1 · A1 · 127 WOORDEN" for a new learner. - Leerpad: suggests the first unpassed stage, i.e. pre-A1 (CurriculumPath.tsx:20, 29: "VOORGESTELD STARTPUNT"). - PracticeCoach: its own "Mijn oefenniveau" select in localStorage, default a1 (PracticeCoach.tsx:11-16, 46). - Videos: a "Niveau" select defaulting to series.stage_id (VideoLibrary.tsx:63, 114; videos/service.py:472). Curriculum POLICY open_stage_access=True (curriculum/service.py:33-38) and the copy "Alle niveaus zijn open. Kies je startpunt" (CurriculumPath.tsx:33). | Introduce one server-side learner level, set during onboarding and editable in Settings. Show it as a header chip, e.g. "Jouw niveau: A1 · Beginner" with an EN/FA gloss. Use it as the default for the story series, the PracticeCoach (drop its localStorage key), videos and the leerpad continue panel. Keep per-request overrides as an "Advanced" disclosure. Use plain-language level names with a one-line can-do statement from path.json title/description (e.g. pre-a1 "First words: welcome"). Promote the level automatically when the stage eindtoets passes; default_stage already reads passed stages. |
| 3 | high | **The interface is Dutch-only; support languages reach only content blocks** — An adult beginner whose support languages are English or Persian cannot read the controls that tell them what to do. The support language only appears under content paragraphs. Persian users get English by default. Mixing formal and informal address reads as unpolished. | layout.tsx:41 sets html lang="nl". Nav labels are plain Dutch strings (AppShell.tsx:14-46). The Today headline has no gloss (TodayHome.tsx:59). Buttons are Dutch-only: "Lees aflevering", "Schrijf dit", "Maak een video", "Toon de betekenis". So are the streak labels "dagen op rij / Beste reeks / Actieve dagen" (TodayHome.tsx:102-107) and the cast roles (TodayHome.tsx:149; series_view returns only name/role in Dutch, stories/service.py:84). LearningText is the only gloss mechanism (LanguageSupport.tsx:35-43). The mode defaults to nl-en in localStorage (LanguageSupport.tsx:9, 12-16), but the server default is support_language="fa" (identity/models.py:23); grep confirms no endpoint writes support_language. The switcher label is "Taalhulp / language" (LanguageSupport.tsx:48). Formality is inconsistent: "Uw oefenbibliotheek… uw eigen tempo" (missions/page.tsx:6), "Wat wilt u vandaag oefenen?" (MissionCatalog.tsx:26), "Uw werk blijft staan" (ConnectionBanner.tsx:45-48), coach notices in 'uw' (coaching/service.py:214, 218, 249, 328), "wat u zei" (SpeechCheck.tsx:139), and stage descriptions "Stel uzelf…" vs "je" everywhere else. English islands: "Story Time" (CurriculumLesson.tsx:24; LearningLibrary.tsx:118, 123). | Create a small UI-copy dictionary, e.g. lib/client/ui-copy.ts with LearningCopy entries per key, and a `<UiText k="nav.today"/>` helper built on LearningText (compact variant: a second line or tooltip under nav and button labels). Show the glosses by default for stages up to A2 and let them fade (setting: "Show translations of buttons"). Persist the support mode server-side and initialise from Learner.support_language. Give cast roles en/fa in the bible (or translate on the client from a fixed map). Standardise on 'je' across UI, coach notices and content descriptions. Rename "Story Time". |
| 4 | high | **Nine competing destinations with overlapping concepts and inconsistent names and icons** — The learner cannot tell where the 'course' is. The structured path is the fifth nav item, and pages with the same name lead to different things. Today adds 6 more outbound links. The mental model becomes a set of separate apps rather than one method. | 9 sidebar items, 6 on mobile (AppShell.tsx:14-46, 195-206).
