**Constraints this area must respect**

- D-17 (docs/DECISIONS.md:170-175): no fictitious scores, certificates or reviewed-content claims. Unreviewed/generated content must stay honestly labelled. Consolidating labels is allowed; removing honesty is not.
- D-19 (docs/DECISIONS.md:203-210): points reward doing and are never a judgement of the learner's Dutch. The daily goal is 20. The streak counts consecutive active days and a missed day resets it (today.py:52). Awarding points for curriculum/missions is an explicit extension of D-19's scope and should be recorded as a new decision.
- D-20 / LEARNING_DESIGN.md:37-39: no pronunciation or accent score anywhere; transcripts are not pronunciation evidence.
- D-16: typed input never counts as speaking practice.
- D-18 (docs/DECISIONS.md:187-201): episodes are generated ahead with at most 2 pending (409). The next episode waits for the learner's choice or 12 hours. A failed draft is shown as a failure, never as a story.
- LEARNING_DESIGN.md:31-35: Dutch remains the practice language. Final checks withhold translations, transcripts and model answers. A self-report never unlocks a stage (LEARNING_DESIGN.md:48), so self-placement may set a starting level but must not mark stages passed.
- LEARNING_DESIGN.md:43-46 states 'One visible next step' and 'A restrained navy/blue palette'. The owner now asks for more colour, so the palette choice must be consciously revised, ideally recorded in DECISIONS.md.
- Accessibility gate: e2e design.spec.ts:37-80 requires zero axe WCAG 2.0/2.1 A/AA violations and no horizontal overflow at 1440, 390 and 320 px on /, /leerpad, /progress, /settings and /speech-check. More colour must keep AA contrast.
- E2E copy and selector coupling that must be updated with any IA/copy change:
- headings 'Kleine stappen. Echte gesprekken.', 'Elke stap vertelt iets.', 'Jouw leeromgeving.', 'Jouw stem. Jouw Nederlands.', and the Today regex /aflevering|woorden wachten|Kies waar je vandaag mee begint/ (design.spec.ts:4-22)
- nav names 'Hoofdmenu' / 'Mobiel hoofdmenu'
- link names 'Mijn voortgang' (design.spec.ts:96), 'Praktijkgesprekken' (resilience.spec.ts:11), 'Mijn leerpad Twaalf niveaus met woorden, grammatica en toetsen.' (stories.spec.ts:75), mobile "Video's" (videos.spec.ts:155)
- the img 'N dagen op rij' (design.spec.ts:48)
- 12 li in data-testid curriculum-path
- test ids today-home, learning-overview, episode-card, video-tile, word-review
- Persian content must keep lang="fa" dir="rtl" and the Vazirmatn font (LanguageSupport.tsx:41; layout.tsx:8). The support-mode localStorage key is 'taalstudio.language-support'.
- Paid usage is metered (usage counters, 429 handling, PAID_USAGE_ENABLED; avatar video about $1/min per D-22). Auto-queuing content during onboarding or waiting must respect allowances and the 2-pending limit.
- The release targets one allowlisted learner ('Release 0.1 has exactly one learner', identity/models.py:14), but all data is learner-scoped. New profile fields must be per-learner and need an Alembic migration.
- The CSP uses per-request nonces (design.spec.ts:24-35). No inline scripts in new UI.
- The navigation guard in AppShell (onClickCapture, NavigationGuardContext) must wrap all in-app links so writing autosave and recording protection keep working.

**Open questions**

- What does a real first visit look like on the Azure deployment (gpt-4.1-mini)? README.md:91-93 gives 1-3 minutes for a local 8B model; I did not verify live generation time.
- Does the owner want an actual placement check (e.g. a short receptive quiz built from stage reading/listening questions) or a self-selection of level? This affects whether onboarding can set curriculum progress (LEARNING_DESIGN.md:48 forbids self-report unlocking).
- Should curriculum, topic, mission and conversation work count toward the daily goal and streak (a revision of D-19's scope)? If so, at what point values?
- Are the static libraries (Story Time with 1 200 texts and 4 800 topic activities, which D-18 calls templated and reused) to be retired, hidden, or kept as 'Meer oefenen'? STATUS.md:26-27 calls them reference material.
- No screenshots exist in docs/previews for /leerpad, /learn/[stage], /missions, /progress, /woorden or /settings. Per DESIGN_PREVIEW.md the CI attaches them, but they are not in the repo, so I could not visually confirm those screens.
- Is the 'Praktijkgesprekken' missions system (4 hand-authored missions with sessions and skill_records) still strategic, or should it merge with topic conversations under one 'Spreken' area?
- Should the support language be persisted server-side? Learner.support_language (default 'fa') exists, but no endpoint writes it, and the client defaults to nl-en.
- I did not verify whether the API supports a learner-level daily-goal target. DAILY_GOAL is a module constant (today.py:16), so a configurable goal would need schema and API changes.
- CurriculumTest, VideoPlayer, TopicConversation and the mission step components were only partially reviewed. Their in-lesson copy may contain further jargon or formality inconsistencies.

### Practice experience and content

How it works today, end to end. A learner opens /leerpad (apps/web/app/leerpad/page.tsx). That page renders CurriculumPath, which shows a "suggested starting point" (the first stage not yet passed, so pre-A1 for a new learner), PracticeCoach with three recommended topics, and 12 stage cards. Opening /learn/[stageId] mounts CurriculumLesson. Its sidebar has 7 sections (8 at pre-A1, which adds Alfabet): Woorden, Grammatica, Lezen, Luisteren, Spreken, Schrijven, Story Time. It opens on Woorden by default (CurriculumLesson.tsx:31). Each skill tab defaults to SkillTopicPractice ("Onderwerpen"), a searchable grid of 100 topics per skill per stage, 12 tiles per page. Each tile shows only a title and category. The original single lesson sits behind a "Startles" toggle (CurriculumLesson.tsx:83-84).

Opening a topic mounts TopicReader (SkillTopicPractice.tsx:84-186). It is the same vertical stack for every topic:
- header and a collapsible "Wat oefen je hier?";
- then the skill-specific body:
  - Reading: a 2-paragraph text with a TTS button per sentence (SpokenPassage), then 2 radio multiple-choice questions.
  - Listening: a TTS clip (CurriculumAudio), an optional transcript, then 2 multiple-choice questions.
  - Speaking: the context texts, a long prompt, 3–4 criteria, CurriculumRecorder (click start/stop, server transcription), then submit for a model rubric.
  - Writing: a textarea, an optional WritingCoach model correction pass, then submit for a model rubric;
- a collapsed vocabulary list at the bottom;
- one submit button ("Rond lezen af" and similar);
- one pale feedback box.

Grading:
- Multiple choice is graded server-side by objective_result. With 75% required, both questions must be right (apps/api/src/dlp/domains/curriculum/service.py:34,160).
- Productive tasks go to the LLM rubric (assess_productive, service.py:206-246) and are always marked completed (topics.py:241).

After feedback, the only forward action is "Kies een ander onderwerp", which goes back to the grid (SkillTopicPractice.tsx:182).

Other practice surfaces:
- **Grammar:** CurriculumLesson.tsx:88. One multiple-choice question per grammar item, checked on the client, 55 questions in total.
- **WordLibrary:** 20 cards per page, each with a reveal toggle.
- **VocabularyRecall:** 5 sequential words with a typed answer that is never checked, then self-rating.
- **AlphabetPractice:** pre-A1 only. Listen, then tap a letter tile, with immediate feedback. Deterministic rounds.
- **Story Time:** the same texts as the topic readings, plus 1 multiple-choice question and a "mark read" button.
- **Missions** (/missions, LessonShell, 4 packs, all A2): reading, listening, push-to-talk conversation, writing and checkpoint steps, with HelpLadder and on-demand LLM FeedbackPanel.
- **/verhalen EpisodeReader and /videos VideoPlayer:** an LLM-generated serial and videos with read-aloud word matching, glossary save to the spaced-repetition deck, and 2–3 multiple-choice questions.
- **/woorden WordReview:** spaced repetition (SM-2) with reveal and 4 grade buttons, for saved words only.

Points and streak (apps/api/src/dlp/domains/stories/today.py) are awarded only by stories, videos and word review. Topic practice, lessons, grammar, missions and the alphabet award nothing.

Content shapes:
- **content/practice/<stage>.json:** {schema_version, stage_id, review_status, topics[100]}. Each topic is {id, title, category, objectives[], language_focus, vocabulary_ids[≈5], reading{text, questions[2]}, listening{text, questions[2]}, speaking{prompt, criteria[3-4], sample, sample_is_excerpt, min_words, max_words}, writing{same}}. Each question is {id, prompt, options[2|3]{nl,en,fa}, answer_index, explanation}.
- **content/library/<stage>.json:** {vocabulary[500]{id, term, topic, meaning{nl,en,fa}, example{nl,en,fa}}, stories[100]{id, title, topic, paragraphs[2], vocabulary_ids, question}}.
- **content/curriculum/path.json:** stages[12]{grammar[2-9]{explanation, examples, practice[1]}, vocabulary[20-43], lesson{story, reading_questions[3], listening, listening_questions[3], speaking, writing}, test{...}}.
- **content/conversations/pilot.json:** 10 blueprints only (a1 3, a2 3, b1 2, b2 2), with steps{goal, cue, hint, accepted, required_groups, choices[aliases, valid], example}.
- **content/missions/*/mission.json:** 5 steps with typed payloads, scenarios and help rungs.

Why it is monotonous: the measured facts.
- **Few mechanics.** The 4,800 topic activities use 3 mechanics: radio multiple choice, record-and-submit, and type-and-submit. Across the whole app there are 7 (those 3, plus conversation turns, reveal/self-rate cards, alphabet tile tap, and read-aloud match). Only the alphabet gives instant per-item feedback.
- **No randomisation.** There is none anywhere: Math.random appears only in request ids (lib/client/api.ts:20).
- **No adaptivity.** There is no difficulty adaptation and no in-topic session/round structure.
- **No typed-and-checked recall.** It does not exist anywhere; recognition multiple choice dominates.
- **Multiple choice is guessable.** At A1–B1 it has 2 options. The default nl-en mode prints the English translation under every option (LanguageSupport.tsx:9,40; CurriculumControls.tsx:16). That gives away the "Wat betekent ‘X’?" questions, which are 100 of 400 per stage at pre-A1, A1 and pre-A2. At pre-B2, B2 and pre-C1, 400/400 correct options are sentences copied verbatim from the text.
- **Heavy templating.** C1 has 4 distinct question prompts across 400 questions. Productive prompts and 2 of 3 criteria are boilerplate. Speaking sample equals writing sample in 100% of topics from pre-A1 to B1.
- **Heavy duplication.** Only 399 unique titles across 1,200 topic slots. Listening texts are identical across pre-A1/A1/pre-A2 and across A2/pre-B1/B1. Reading texts are identical across C1/pre-C2/C2. The library vocabulary is the same 500 terms for pre-A1, A1 and pre-A2. Story Time stories are the topic reading texts (100/100 at pre-A1).

These are the root causes of "limited and boring".

What can be generated without new authoring. The library vocabulary (term, meaning, example, topic grouping) and the topic bundle (5 vocab + 4 questions + 2 texts) can feed:
- match-pairs, cloze, listen-and-choose, dictation, typed recall, de/het sorting (strong at A–B levels), sentence-builder tiles, sentence ordering, odd-one-out, speed rounds and shadowing;
- mixed 8–12 item sessions.

Translate-to-Dutch and error-correction need an LLM judge or generator. Verb-conjugation drills are not feasible: the vocabulary banks contain 0–1 verbs per stage.

The base for a shared engine is already present:
- CurriculumQuestions/QuestionList (multiple-choice rendering);
- AlphabetPractice and letterQuestion (tile item with instant feedback);
- VocabularyRecall (round, missed-items retry, summary);
- WordReview plus the words API and SM-2 backend;
- PhraseAudio/PhraseAudioController (TTS with cache);
- CurriculumRecorder/useRecorder (capture and transcript);
- ReadAloud with read_aloud_result (word-match);
- HelpLadder, dutchSentences and today.add_points.

**Strengths**

- Large, fully trilingual content: every vocabulary card, question, option, explanation and task has nl/en/fa (library: 500 cards + 100 stories × 12 stages; practice: 100 topics × 4 skills × 12 stages) – ideal raw material for generated drills.
- Per-topic bundles are self-contained: TopicDetail returns title, objectives, 5 linked vocabulary cards with examples, a reading text, a listening text, 4 questions with explanations and two productive tasks (apps/api/src/dlp/domains/curriculum/topics.py:174-195).
- Robust audio stack: PhraseAudioController with tab cache (32 entries/8 MB), single-player audio focus across the app, 600-char chunking (apps/web/lib/client/phrase-audio.ts, audio-focus.ts) – any Dutch string can be spoken on demand.
- Robust recording stack: useRecorder with mime negotiation, mic request gating, stop tail; CurriculumRecorder returns asset id AND transcript to the caller (CurriculumControls.tsx:96-163).
- A working SM-2 spaced-repetition backend and UI (/woorden) plus daily points/streak/goal plumbing (stories/vocab.py, stories/today.py, TodayHome streak ring).
- Server-side idempotency and evidence model (newRequestId, observation records with failed_refs in coaching service) – a ready signal for adaptivity.
- Careful LLM safety pattern: model interprets, code validates quotes/choices (topic_conversations/workflow.py:69-86; curriculum/service.py:188-203).
- HelpLadder provides graduated hints with evidence; WritingCoach gives categorised corrections (spelling, grammar, word_order, punctuation, word_choice).
- Accessibility discipline (focus management after commit, aria-live, reduced-motion handling in stories.css:45) that a new engine should keep.

**Findings**

| # | Severity | Finding | Evidence | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | high | **Only 3 interaction mechanics in the 4,800-activity topic practice; 7 in the whole app** — Distinct mechanics: (a) radio MCQ submit-then-check (5 separate renderers: CurriculumQuestions, QuestionList, EpisodeReader.Questions, VideoPlayer.Questions, CurriculumTest exam mode); (b) record open answer → LLM rubric; (c) type open answer → LLM rubric; (d) conversational turns (10 topic blueprints + 4 missions); (e) reveal-and-self-rate cards (WordLibrary, VocabularyRecall, WordReview); (f) listen-and-tap tiles with instant feedback (Alphabet only, pre-A1 only, 26 letters); (g) read-aloud word match (episodes only). Topic practice uses only a/b/c. No drag/drop, no tile building, no matching, no typing-with-checking, no timed or game-like item anywhere. | apps/web/components/SkillTopicPractice.tsx:168-176 (reading text+MCQ, CurriculumAudio+MCQ, CurriculumRecorder, textarea); CurriculumControls.tsx:12-19 radio MCQ; whole-app extras: TopicConversation.tsx:167-188, VocabularyRecall.tsx:35-36, WordReview.tsx:64-73, AlphabetPractice.tsx:31, EpisodeReader.tsx:101-126 | Introduce a shared item-based exercise engine with at least 8–10 item types (see feasibility findings) and make topic practice a short mixed session rather than a single form. |
| 2 | high | **No active recall that is actually checked; recognition MCQ and self-rating dominate** — Every vocabulary interaction is 'look, reveal, judge yourself'. The learner can type in VocabularyRecall but nothing checks it. Receptive skills are 2–3 option MCQ. The only production is long open tasks graded by an LLM after a single submit. There is nothing in between (cloze, typed recall, dictation, word order) – the layer where most beginner learning happens. | apps/web/components/VocabularyRecall.tsx:35 input value never compared, :36 self-rate 'Nog eens oefenen'/'Ik herinnerde het me'; WordReview.tsx:72 reveal then 4 grade buttons; LearningLibrary.tsx:77 'Toon de betekenis' toggle; docs/LEARNING_DESIGN.md:16-19 self-assessment is a deliberate choice | Add typed-and-checked recall (tolerant comparison) and auto-checkable production items; keep self-rating as an optional SRS grade after an objective check rather than instead of it. |
| 3 | high | **MCQ is trivially guessable: 2 options at A1–B1, translations printed under options, 'Wat betekent' giveaways, verbatim answers at B2** — With two options and English support visible by default, a learner can answer most A-level questions without reading Dutch; vocabulary-meaning questions literally show the English word as an option gloss. At B2 the options are sentences copied from the text, so string-matching solves them. Random guessing passes a 2-question topic 25% of the time. Episode/video questions are Dutch-only (EpisodeReader.tsx:158-161) – inconsistent with topic questions. | Analysis of content/practice/*.json: options per question = 2 for all 400 questions at pre-a1..b1; 3 at pre-b2..pre-c1; 2/3 mixed at c-levels. 'Wat betekent ‘X’ in deze tekst?' = 100/400 at pre-a1, a1, pre-a2 (e.g. a1-t001-r2 options 'hier slaap je in' en:'bed'). Correct option verbatim in text: 400/400 at pre-b2, b2, pre-c1. LanguageSupport.tsx:9 default 'nl-en', :40 renders English under every LearningText; CurriculumControls.tsx:16 options rendered with LearningText (non-exam). Pass rule apps/api/src/dlp/domains/curriculum/service.py:34,160 (75% of 2 → both). | In practice items hide support translations on options until after answering (or behind a hint), use 3–4 options with same-topic distractors, and shuffle option order per attempt. |
| 4 | high | **Massive templating and duplication in the topic and library banks** — The learner meets the same texts and the same question stems repeatedly: moving from pre-A1 to A1 to pre-A2 gives the same listening clips, the same 500 words and the same topic titles; Story Time repeats the Lezen texts. docs/PRACTICE_LIBRARY.md:26-27 calls some reuse intentional spiralling, but the magnitude makes 'progress' feel like repetition. | content/practice: only 399 unique titles across 1,200 topics (each title reused in 3 stages: a1/pre-a1/pre-a2, a2/pre-b1/b1, pre-b2/b2/pre-c1, c1/pre-c2/c2); listening texts identical across pre-a1/a1/pre-a2 (100) and a2/pre-b1/b1 (100) → 800 unique of 1,200; reading texts identical across c1/pre-c2/c2 (100) and a2/b1 (96). c1 has 4 distinct question prompts across 400 questions; b1 has 'Welke uitleg past bij de aanpak in deze tekst?' and 'Welke reactie beantwoordt het verzoek in de boodschap?' 100× each; a2..b1 reading q1 == listening q1 in 100/100 topics. content/library: pre-a1, a1 and pre-a2 share the same 500 terms; pre-b2/b2/pre-c1 share 500; a2/pre-b1/b1 share 480; a2/pre-b1/b1 examples use only 54–57 sentence frames (e.g. 'Ik bewaar X zorgvuldig, zodat ik mijn aanvraag later kan opvolgen.' ×10). Story Time stories == topic reading texts (100/100 at pre-a1, 99 a1, 96 b1). | Treat the banks as an item pool for generated drills (which tolerate reuse well) instead of as 1,200 'distinct' lessons; reduce visible duplication (e.g. hide Story Time when identical to the topic text, or present cross-stage reuse explicitly as review). |
| 5 | medium | **Productive tasks are boilerplate-heavy, identical for speaking and writing, and always 'completed'** — The productive side repeats the same instructions every time and gives the same model answer for speaking and writing. Because any substantive attempt completes the topic, there is no sense of challenge or mastery, and every attempt costs one LLM call (assess_productive) with latency. | pre-a1/a1: speaking prompt always starts 'Spreek in deze situatie:' and writing 'Schrijf een kort bericht voor deze situatie:' (100/100); 2 of 3 criteria per task are the same text in all 200 tasks ('Behoud de juiste personen, voorwerpen, plaats en tijd…', 'Spreek rustig en begrijpelijk…'/'Maak leesbare zinnen…'); speaking.sample == writing.sample in 100% of topics at pre-a1..b1; c1 has 6 distinct criteria across 700. apps/api/src/dlp/domains/curriculum/topics.py:241 'completed = True'. | Precede open production with scaffolded auto-checked items built from the sample (sentence builder, cloze over the sample, shadow the sample), and show only the topic-specific criterion prominently. |
| 6 | high | **No rewards or feedback loop in practice: points/streak only from stories, videos and word review** — Completing topics, grammar checks, alphabet rounds, missions or Startles never moves the daily goal or streak shown on TodayHome. There are no sounds, no celebratory animation, no XP per item, no combo/streak within a session, no session summary in topic practice. Feedback is a single pale box (learning.css:166) with neutral copy. | add_points callers: apps/api/src/dlp/domains/stories/service.py (6), stories/vocab.py (1), videos/service.py (3); none in curriculum/topics.py or curriculum/service.py or practice/*. POINTS stories/service.py:46 {read 10, answer 3, choose 2, read_aloud 5}; videos/service.py:55; vocab.py:23 REVIEW_POINTS=1; today.py:16 DAILY_GOAL=20. Only animation in CSS: stories.css:41-44 writing dots. SkillTopicPractice.tsx:182 feedback heading 'Een onderwerp verder.' | Award points via today.add_points for topic/lesson/mission/exercise-engine items (D-19 permits 'points reward doing'), add per-item instant feedback states (correct/incorrect colours, micro-animation, optional sound), and an end-of-session summary card. |
| 7 | high | **No randomisation, no adaptivity, no session structure inside a topic** — Option order, item order and word order are fixed in JSON, so retrying a topic is identical. Nothing adapts difficulty to errors (coaching observations record failed_refs but only influence the 3-item PracticeCoach suggestion). A topic is one form: no rounds, no progress bar within the activity, no hearts/lives, no summary. | Math.random only in apps/web/lib/client/api.ts:20 (request ids); no shuffle anywhere in apps/web; lib/alphabet.ts:64-70 deterministic (round*7+3)%26 with fixed offsets [0,5,11,17]; VocabularyRecall.tsx:21-24 takes next 5 words sequentially from the current page; SkillTopicPractice.tsx:39 topics paged 12 at a time in file order; PracticeCoach shows 3 items (coaching/service.py:208). | Engine should shuffle items/options per attempt, re-queue missed items within the session (VocabularyRecall already does a missed-items loop), and use coaching observations to pick item types/words. |
| 8 | medium | **Confusing entry and choice overload in the stage page** — A new learner lands on a word bank, then on a 100-item grid per skill, with two parallel 'lesson' sources, and no 'continue/next' affordance. Nothing explains how a session helps them learn. The pedagogical loop in LEARNING_DESIGN.md (preview words → recall → read/listen → speak → write) is not what the UI sequences. | CurriculumLesson.tsx:31 default section 'words' (500-card WordLibrary); :81 sidebar 'STAP VOOR STAP' but no enforced sequence; :83 'Onderwerpen'/'Startles' toggle without explanation; SkillTopicPractice.tsx:53-66 100 topics, 12 per page, tiles show only index/title/category; :182 after feedback only 'Kies een ander onderwerp' (back to grid); CurriculumPath.tsx:20 suggested start = first unpassed stage (no placement); AppShell.tsx:16-41 nine navigation destinations; TopicReader puts vocabulary preview last in a collapsed details (SkillTopicPractice.tsx:178) although docs/LEARNING_DESIGN.md:9 says 'Preview relevant words' first. | Replace the default with a single 'Start today's session' that runs the loop (vocab preview items → recall drills → comprehension → guided production) for the next topic, with an obvious 'Volgende' after the summary. |
| 9 | low | **Disclaimer-heavy, mixed-register copy inside exercises** — Nearly every practice panel ends with a caveat about review status, synthetic voice or not being a score. Combined with 2–3 stacked language lines (LearningText) the screens read as dense and cautious rather than playful. | CurriculumControls.tsx:162 'Je opname wordt bij deze oefening bewaard…'; AlphabetPractice.tsx:17 'Nog niet nagekeken' badge and :35 course-note; VocabularyRecall.tsx:38 course-note; LearningLibrary.tsx:121; CurriculumLesson.tsx:96 'Nieuwe les · taalreview nog niet afgerond.'; ListeningStep.tsx:114 and FeedbackPanel.tsx:66 use formal 'u' while topic practice uses 'je'; StepHeader.tsx:21 ContentLabel on every mission step. | Consolidate caveats into one 'about this content' info point per page; keep exercise surfaces focused on the task; unify register (je). |
| 10 | medium | **Code duplication and inconsistencies a shared engine should remove** — Each surface hand-rolls question state, check, and feedback. Word counts differ between mission writing and topic writing for the same text. Any new exercise type would currently have to be added in 3–5 places. | Three MCQ renderers: CurriculumControls.tsx:12-19, QuestionList.tsx:26-129, EpisodeReader.tsx:147-168 + VideoPlayer.tsx:141-162 (near-identical, as are Glossary EpisodeReader.tsx:128-145 / VideoPlayer.tsx:122-139 and friendly() :13-20 in both); two countWords with different regexes (CurriculumControls.tsx:94 vs WritingStep.tsx:23); WritingStep.tsx:231 WritingCoach stageId hard-coded "a2"; CurriculumLesson and TopicReader each re-implement submit/feedback blocks (CurriculumLesson.tsx:61-69,94-95 vs SkillTopicPractice.tsx:129-152,181-182). | Extract a typed ExerciseItem model + renderer registry + session runner, and migrate existing MCQ/recall/alphabet into it. |

| 33 | high | **Feasibility: Mixed 8–12 item session with progress, hearts and summary** — Per topic: 2 match/listen-choose items from the 5 words, 2 cloze/typed recall, 1 dictation from a text sentence, 1 sentence builder from the sample, 2–4 comprehension items with instant feedback, 1 shadowing item, optional open production at the end. Hearts/lives would conflict with D-19 'never a judgement' – use non-punitive progress + retry queue instead, or consciously revise D-19. | TopicDetail bundle (topics.py:174-195): 5 vocabulary cards (term/meaning/example), reading text, listening text, 4 MCQ, speaking/writing sample; VocabularyRecall round/missed/summary loop (VocabularyRecall.tsx:18-28,37); today.add_points (stories/today.py:23) | Make this the default 'Start' action of every topic and of the leerpad. |

**Constraints this area must respect**

- D-19 (docs/DECISIONS.md:203-209): points reward doing and are never a judgement of the learner's Dutch; the word bank only contains words the learner saved. Hearts/lives or score-shaming would need a conscious revision of this decision.
- D-20 (docs/DECISIONS.md): read-aloud compares recognised words; no pronunciation or accent score may be shown. Shadowing items must keep that framing (also CURRICULUM.md:131-133, LEARNING_DESIGN.md recognition note).
- Final-check answers, listening transcripts, translations and model answers must stay withheld before submission (curriculum/service.py:283-297; LEARNING_DESIGN.md 'Support and independent evidence'). Practice-mode keys must never leak into the final test.
- Topic practice completion semantics: receptive completion requires the submitted answers to pass (75%); productive completion is a substantive attempt; completion is sticky (docs/TOPIC_PRACTICE.md:27-36; topics.py:247-251). Topic practice satisfies the stage prerequisite for the final check.
- LLM calls are allowance-metered (usage.reserve in assess_productive, writing feedback, conversation turns) and TTS has a 'limit' error path; new item types should prefer client-side checking and cache audio.
- Content is explicitly 'unreviewed'; generated drills inherit that status and must not claim CEFR calibration (TOPIC_PRACTICE.md:55-57, PRACTICE_LIBRARY.md:36-38).
- Dutch is the practice language; English/Persian are optional support and Persian must keep lang=fa dir=rtl (LanguageSupport.tsx:41; LEARNING_DESIGN.md).
- Belgian Standard Dutch (nl-BE) for TTS, sentence segmentation and LLM prompts.
- Accessibility conventions already enforced by e2e/axe tests (focus after commit, aria-live status, reduced motion) – any animation must respect prefers-reduced-motion.

**Open questions**

- Is the cross-stage reuse (same 500 terms for pre-A1/A1/pre-A2; same listening texts across three stages; Story Time == topic reading text) intentional product design or a builder shortcut? PRACTICE_LIBRARY.md:26-27 claims some intentional spiralling, but the extent was not discussed anywhere I found.
- Does the owner accept non-punitive mechanics only (per D-19), or should hearts/lives be introduced as an explicit design change?
- Is Azure Speech pronunciation assessment for nl-BE intended for production? Nothing in the repo uses it (not verified against the Azure adapter in apps/api/src/dlp/providers/speech_azure.py beyond its header).
- What are the production TTS and LLM allowance limits per learner per day? Dictation- and shadowing-heavy sessions would multiply speech/synthesize calls. The values were not read from config.
- Should practice-mode answer keys be sent to the client for instant feedback, or should a per-item check endpoint be added? This is a trade-off between security/evidence integrity and latency.
- The runtime UI was not observed (no dev server run). Visual monotony is inferred from code and CSS (topics.css, learning.css), not from screenshots. docs/previews has PNGs only for the today/episode/video screens.
- The content builders scripts/content_build/practice_*.py were not read in detail. Whether they can cheaply emit extra fields (accepted alternatives, verb forms, distractor sets) is unverified.
- How do the multiple :root palettes (globals.css, design.css, learning.css all define --accent with different values, imported in that order in layout.tsx:10-19) interact on practice screens? The effective accent is likely learning.css #2455b5. Not visually verified.

### Visual design system

The web app has no CSS modules, no utility framework and no design-token file. All styling lives in 10 global stylesheets imported in a fixed order by apps/web/app/layout.tsx:10-19: globals.css, design.css, learning.css, learning-tools.css, library.css, phrase-audio.css, topics.css, learning-agents.css, stories.css, videos.css. Together they are 2,845 lines, about 104 KB and about 1,190 rule blocks. Line counts: design 1711, globals 425, learning 317, stories 182, videos 53, library 41, topics 36, phrase-audio 36, learning-agents 25, learning-tools 19. learning-agents, library and topics are written minified with many rules per line, so their line counts understate their size.

The files are three historical redesigns stacked by cascade order, not one system.
(1) globals.css (17 Sep, release 0.1) is a functional base. Its :root has a beige/green palette: --accent #0b5d4b, --radius 10px, Segoe UI.
(2) design.css (22 Sep, "Redesign the learner workspace") redefines :root with sage green (--accent #225b49, --ink #223a35, --radius 18px, DM Sans). It adds the sidebar shell, a large illustrated "mission-hero" dashboard, a lesson layout, a speech studio, breakpoints and the reduced-motion and print blocks.
(3) learning.css (24 Sep) redefines :root a third time with navy/blue (--accent #2455b5, --ink #182c47, --radius 16px). It re-skins the shell (brand mark, nav current state, sticky header, button hover) and adds the curriculum path, lesson workspace, tests and results.
Later feature files add their own literal hex colours: library, topics, learning-agents, learning-tools and phrase-audio are all navy/blue, with 0 var() uses in four of them. stories.css (2 Oct) adds a warm orange token set and six gradient "tone" pairs for episode covers. videos.css reuses those tones.

Because the three :root blocks have equal specificity, the live tokens are a mix:
- from learning.css: bg, surface, ink, muted, line, accent, ok-bg/ink, radius
- from design.css: accent-ink, warn-bg/ink, fonts
- from globals.css: bad-bg/ink

There are no tokens for spacing, the type scale, weights, shadows, z-index, motion or breakpoints. The CSS contains 356 distinct hex values. Radii include 2–22px literals, 50% and 999px. There are 14 different breakpoints (430/480/520/600/640/650/700/767/768/850/960/1050/1250/1600).

Much of the green design.css layer is still live on screens the blue layer never re-skinned. Examples: the mission lesson step list (.step-list current #eaf2e9 / .step-number #315c45), checked answers (.question label:has(input:checked) #edf3e8), .dutch-text border #829c77, conversation bubbles (#f6f8f2/#e4edde), .mic-orbit, .information-callout, .label.neutral and the textarea borders. So the missions, speech-check, progress and settings pages mix sage green into a navy theme.

About 500 lines of design.css (about 30%) style an old dashboard that no longer renders: .mission-hero, .mission-art, .art-*, .hero-*, .focus-card, .skill-card, .skill-overview, .studio-card, .activity-card, .dashboard-top/bottom, .integrity-note, .text-link, .button.light, .step-segments, .status-dot, .sidebar-note, .language-pill. globals.css has dead .site-header, .site-nav, .mission-hero and .skill-overview rules. Many selectors are defined two or three times (.button, .card, .label, .brand, .site-main, .site-footer, .step-list button, .question label, .help-ladder .rung, .dutch-text, .conversation, .bubble, .lesson-layout, .eyebrow, h1/h2), and overrides depend on import order plus 11 !important declarations.

The shell is components/AppShell.tsx. It renders a fixed 224px white sidebar with a CSS text brand mark "t." and 9 nav links, each with a monochrome stroke icon from components/Icon.tsx (20 icons, stroke 1.65, currentColor). It also has a sticky header with a breadcrumb, a language select (LanguageSupport.tsx) and a profile button. Below 1050px a fixed bottom nav shows 6 items. Fonts are DM Sans Variable and Vazirmatn Variable, self-hosted through @fontsource-variable (layout.tsx:7-8); the CSP allows only font-src 'self'. html is fixed to lang="nl". Persian support text is set per element with lang="fa" dir="rtl" and the .fa class; layout CSS mostly uses physical properties. There is no dark mode and no color-scheme.

Motion is almost absent:
- one @keyframes (`dots`, the "writer is busy" indicator)
- five transitions (nav link background, .button, episode-card hover lift, goal-bar width, video scene highlight)
- no :active states anywhere
- no celebration moments

Reduced motion is handled by a universal kill switch at design.css:1684-1693.

Progress is shown as:
- a static border "streak ring" whose colour flips when active; it does not show the fraction of the goal
- linear bars: .goal-bar, .path-progress, native <progress> in topics
- 14 day dots
- a flat 3-column grid of 12 stage cards; there is no path map

Answer feedback in the curriculum and topic practice is a green or amber text block under each question (.practice-correct / .practice-hint). The options themselves are never marked correct or incorrect. Only the episode questions colour the whole question card (is-correct / is-wrong) and outline the right option. The .practice-feedback box has unused modifier classes complete/retry, so success and retry look identical.

What the screenshots in docs/previews show: a light grey canvas with uniform white cards (1px #dfe5ed border, almost no shadow), navy text and a single blue primary button. Colour comes only from the CSS gradient episode covers and video posters (orange tone-1, blue tone-3, gold tone-5) and the orange word count. That is 2–3 hues on mostly neutral surfaces. Hierarchy rests on type size alone. Rows of identical outline buttons ("Lees hardop", "Bewaar") make the reading pages monotonous. A first-run learner sees a home screen full of zeros: 0 dagen, 0/20 punten, 0 woorden, 14 grey dots. There are no images or illustrations: there is no public/ directory, and the CSP sets img-src to 'self' data: blob:. Many live labels are 7–10px (brand caption, step-skill, section eyebrows, .label, footer, mobile nav).

The e2e suite ties the design to the code. Ten specs run axe WCAG 2.1 AA checks, design.spec.ts screenshots at 1440/390/320 and asserts no horizontal overflow, and tests use about 70 distinct class selectors, 164 getByTestId calls and exact heading text.

**Strengths**

- Accessibility is enforced: axe WCAG 2.1 A/AA with zero violations at 1440/390/320 across 10 e2e specs, a no-horizontal-overflow assertion, a skip link, focus-visible outlines, 44px minimum touch targets on .button (globals.css:153), visually-hidden labels, a role=img streak with an aria-label, and aria-pressed/aria-current used as state hooks.
- Reduced motion is handled globally (design.css:1684-1693) and for the writing-dots animation (stories.css:45). A print stylesheet exists (design.css:1694-1711).
- Fonts are self-hosted variable fonts (DM Sans, plus Vazirmatn for Persian) through @fontsource-variable, which fits the strict CSP font-src 'self'.
- Bilingual and RTL support text is handled with lang plus dir=rtl and the .fa class (globals.css:203-208; LanguageSupport.tsx:35-43). HelpLadder uses dir=auto for mixed glosses.
- A few genuinely engaging patterns already exist to build on: gradient episode covers with pseudo-element art (stories.css:16-26), video posters with a play disc (videos.css:16-22), the transcript scene that tracks playback (.scene.is-active, videos.css:39-43), grade buttons tinted by intent (stories.css:154), branching-story choice buttons on a navy panel (stories.css:120-130), and the writing-coach del/ins diff (library.css:32).
- Responsive behaviour is thought through: the sidebar becomes a bottom nav at 1050px, step lists turn into horizontal scroll-snap strips on phones (design.css:1531-1547), and the unit sidebar becomes a segmented control on mobile (learning.css:237-241).
- Components consistently use semantic HTML (fieldset/legend for questions, dl for facts, ol for steps and path, details/summary for optional help), which keeps restyling safe.

**Findings**

| # | Severity | Finding | Evidence | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | high | **Three competing :root token layers; the live palette depends on import order** — No single file owns the tokens. There are no tokens for spacing, type scale, font weight, shadow/elevation, z-index, motion or breakpoints. The CSS holds 356 distinct hex values (design 116, learning 83, stories 38, library 33, topics 27, phrase-audio 24, learning-agents 22). Four feature files make 0 var() calls and hard-code the navy/blue palette. Radii are literals from 2 to 22px. --radius is effectively used only by the remaining globals.css rules. | globals.css:1-18 (--accent #0b5d4b, --radius 10px); design.css:2-17 (--accent #225b49, --radius 18px, fonts); learning.css:2-12 (--accent #2455b5, --ink #182c47, --radius 16px); stories.css:2-12 (--warm, --tone-1..6); import order layout.tsx:10-19. Live values: bg #f5f6f8, surface #fff, ink #182c47, muted #58667a, line #dfe5ed, accent #2455b5, accent-ink #fff, radius 16px, ok #eaf4ef/#216045 (learning.css); warn #faf1d9/#735218 and fonts (design.css); bad #fbe6e3/#8a2a1c (globals.css). ContentReviewQueue.tsx:101 references an undefined --border. | Create one tokens file (for example app/tokens.css imported first) with primitive scales (colour ramps, space-1..8, radius-sm..xl/pill, text-xs..3xl, weight, shadow-1..4, dur-fast/base/slow, ease-standard/emphasized, z-index) and semantic aliases (--surface-1/2/sunken/inverse, --text-strong/muted, --border, --accent, --success/warning/danger/info with -bg/-ink/-border, and per-skill accents). Wrap the stylesheets in @layer tokens, base, components, features, utilities so overrides stop depending on import order. Delete the :root blocks in globals.css, design.css and learning.css after migration. |

**Constraints this area must respect**

- axe-core WCAG 2.1 A/AA must report zero violations. 10 e2e spec files run AxeBuilder, and design.spec.ts runs it at 1440, 390 and 320px on today, leerpad, progress, settings and speech-check, plus the mission lesson. New colours must meet 4.5:1 for text and 3:1 for UI components.
- No horizontal overflow at 320px: document.documentElement.scrollWidth <= innerWidth (design.spec.ts:66-70).
- Heading texts asserted in tests must stay the same or be updated deliberately: today regex /aflevering|woorden wachten|Kies waar je vandaag mee begint/, 'Kleine stappen. Echte gesprekken.', 'Elke stap vertelt iets.', 'Jouw leeromgeving.', 'Jouw stem. Jouw Nederlands.' (design.spec.ts:4-22). Word review texts are also asserted ('Herhaal wat je bewaarde.', 'N KAARTEN IN DEZE RONDE', grade button names Nog eens/Moeilijk/Goed/Makkelijk, 'Klaar voor vandaag.').
- The streak ring must keep role=img with an accessible name matching /dag(en)? op rij/ (TodayHome.tsx:100; design.spec.ts:48).
- Keep data-testid values (164 uses), data-step/data-help/data-review/data-kind attributes, lang/dir attributes on support text, and the ~70 class selectors used by e2e tests (listed in the findings), or update those tests in the same change.
- data-testid='curriculum-path' must keep 12 li children (design.spec.ts:46, curriculum.spec.ts:7). The progress page must keep 12 .course-progress-row items with dt texts Lezen/Luisteren/Spreken/Schrijven (design.spec.ts:50-55).
- Touch targets stay at least 44px (.button min-height 44px, globals.css:153; mobile nav links min-height 48px, design.css:1349).
- Respect prefers-reduced-motion. The global neutraliser is at design.css:1684-1693; all new animation must degrade under it.
- CSP (proxy.ts): fonts self-hosted only (font-src 'self'); images only self, data: or blob: (no external CDNs or stock images); inline styles allowed.
- Persian support text must keep lang='fa' dir='rtl' (curriculum.spec.ts:28 asserts dir=rtl). The UI language stays Dutch (html lang='nl').
- docs/LEARNING_DESIGN.md:44 records a deliberate 'restrained navy/blue palette; high-contrast text'. A more colourful system is a conscious revision and should update that doc.
- Honesty and provenance notes are part of the product (for example .course-note 'nog niet nagekeken door een taaldocent', synthetic voice labels). A redesign may restyle them but should keep them visible.
- The web package has no CSS framework or animation library (package.json dependencies: next, react, react-dom, jose, two fontsource packages). Adding one (Tailwind, Framer Motion, Radix) is a new dependency decision.

**Open questions**

- docs/previews has screenshots only for Today, an episode and videos. The visual effect of the leftover sage-green rules on /missions/[id], /learn/[stage], /learn/[stage]/test, /progress, /settings, /speech-check, /woorden and /verhalen was inferred from the CSS, not seen. The Playwright report attachments (design.spec.ts:62-65) may contain them but were not inspected.
- today-phone.png shows 5 bottom-nav items and today-desktop.png has no 'Video's' sidebar link, so these previews predate the video feature. The current code has 6 mobile items (AppShell.tsx:14-46; grid of 6 at design.css:1333). How the 6-item nav fits at 320px with 9px labels was not visually verified.
- The ~1.9:1 contrast for the #f2b233 focus ring against white is a hand calculation from relative luminance and should be confirmed with a contrast tool.
- The ~500 dead lines in design.css were estimated from a grep of class names across components/ and app/ TSX files. Classes built dynamically through template strings could hide additional live uses (for example `icon-tile ${color}` was found that way).
- Whether the owner wants dark mode, a Persian-UI (fully RTL) locale, or only colourful light mode was not determined.
- Whether adding a styling or animation dependency (Tailwind, CSS Modules migration, Framer Motion, Radix primitives) is acceptable to the owner is unknown.
- Whether the 'restrained navy/blue palette' decision (LEARNING_DESIGN.md:44) may be revised is not recorded beyond the owner's request for a more colourful UI.

### Documentation, decisions and constraints

TRAJECTORY (git log -40, 18 Sep → 2 Oct 2026): it started as "release 0.1", a single A2 appointment mission (dentist base plus hairdresser transfer) built around evidence and honesty: the model proposes and code validates, typed input never counts as speaking, and feedback must cite evidence. Later commits added three more service missions, then release 0.2 (Taalstudio shell, Azure preparation, then private Azure runtime and guarded releases). After that came the 12-stage curriculum, the open library (500 cards and 100 stories per stage), topic practice (100 topics × 4 skills × 12 stages = 4,800 activities) and "learning support" (guided topic conversations, a practice coach, an admin content-review queue). On 2 Oct alone the project added the story serial "De Lindestraat", the SM-2 word bank, the daily plan with points and streak, live Azure tuning, the D-21 direct-order fix, the GitHub-deploy setup script and video lessons (Azure avatar). Breadth grew very fast. Every feature carries heavy honesty and limits text. The UX (onboarding, motivation, variety) was never the focus of a review.

DONE (STATUS.md "What works today"):
- Daily plan (/), story serial (/verhalen), video lessons (/videos), word bank (/woorden), 12-stage path (/leerpad, /learn/<stage>), topic practice, guided conversations, coach, admin queue, 4 missions (/missions), speech (push-to-talk → ffmpeg → whisper/Azure STT; Piper/Azure TTS).
- Azure deployed to RG dlp-production (West Europe) on 2 Oct. VERIFICATION lists release a6833ee as verified_live. Signed-in checks covered episodes by gpt-4.1-mini, grading, word review, nl-BE-DenaNeural synthesis plus recognition, and one typed lunch turn.
- Test counts: API 514 tests, web unit 27, Playwright 96 checks with axe scans.

KNOWN LIMITS (STATUS.md):
- All fixed Dutch and every generated episode is unreviewed.
- The static story libraries are templated and reused. D-18 says the serial is "the path forward".
- The local 8B model's Dutch and Persian are only "serviceable".
- There is no pronunciation scoring, by design.
- The phone acceptance run has not been done.
- deploy.yml has no production environment yet, so releases run from the laptop.
- The live avatar render has not been done.
- The Bicep video-seconds parameters only take effect on the next template deploy.

STATUS "Next steps, in order":
1. Do the first live video, then read the serial daily for two weeks and tune it.
2. Phone run (GO_LIVE §6).
3. Belgian Dutch reviewer, using DEMO.md.
4. Run setup_github_deploy.sh.
5. "Only then: more content, more missions."

Live checklist still open: the phone run, `verify_live.py --mode authenticated`, and the PostgreSQL point-in-time-restore drill.

IMPROVEMENT_REVIEW (22 Sep), "Next improvements, in order", and whether the code did each:
1. Real-device speaking/checkpoint run and Belgian Dutch review — NOT done. VERIFICATION: spoken turns and the checkpoint have not been run with real providers; phone acceptance is "not started"; all content is review_pending.
2. Benchmark real models on the 40 labelled cases before selecting a model — NOT recorded. benchmarks/language/README says numbers go into DECISIONS.md before a model is chosen, yet gpt-4.1-mini is live and DECISIONS has no benchmark entry.
3. Version-aware cross-tab draft conflict handling — NOT done. apps/web/lib/client/draft-saver.ts has no version, ETag or If-Match. The only `storage` listeners are in LanguageSupport.tsx:20 and LearningLibrary.tsx:25.
4. Attempt deletion with verified blob cleanup and a restore exercise — NOT done. The only DELETE route is routes_stories.py:212 (/words/{item_id}), and the restore drill is still in the STATUS checklist.
5. Confirm cloud allowance and real Azure auth, speech and storage — DONE (2 Oct live).
6. "Only then expand content or introduce another mission. Teacher workspaces and formal level assessments remain separate product work" — this ordering was overridden: 3 missions, 12 stages, 4,800 topics, the serial and videos were all added before items 1–4.

The earlier table rows (CI DB name, hint/evidence ordering, draft serialisation, session locking, learner-creation race, side-effect-free rejection, _test DB guard, deploy-input injection, home panel) are documented as fixed with tests.

AZURE SETUP (GO_LIVE, main.bicep, parameters.example.json, deploy_current.sh, VERIFICATION):
- Region and naming: West Europe; RG dlp-production; prefix dlp; ACR dlpacrhofmzccgjnr4s (Basic, no admin user); GitHub repo MahmoudAsadi97/Dutch_Learning_Platform.
- Hosting: one Container Apps environment (Consumption, VNet).
  - dlp-web: external ingress, 0.25 vCPU / 0.5 GiB, 0–1 replicas, built-in Easy Auth for Entra.
  - dlp-api: internal ingress only, 0.25 vCPU, exactly 1 replica so the PostgreSQL job loop stays warm.
  - Manual migration job dlp-migrate: 15-minute limit, no retry, separate identity.
- Database: PostgreSQL Flexible Server 16, B1ms, 32 GB, 7-day backup, no HA. It has a public endpoint disabled and is VNet-private. The runtime uses DML-only role dlp_app with TLS verify-full.
- Storage: Standard_LRS, private container learner-audio, shared-key access off, 14-day soft delete.
- Speech: S0 with local auth disabled and a custom subdomain; nl-BE STT; voice nl-BE-DenaNeural. The avatar is available in West Europe at about $1 per minute of video.
- Azure OpenAI: account kind 'OpenAI' (not a Foundry project), local auth disabled.
  - Deployment chat-small. VERIFICATION shows gpt-4.1-mini live.
  - Optional chat-strong. When it is absent, AZURE_CHAT_DEPLOYMENT_STRONG = chat-small.
  - SKU default 'Standard' (regional), capacity default 1, versionUpgradeOption NoAutoUpgrade.
  - Reached via the v1 endpoint with managed identity.
- Key Vault: RBAC, purge protection, per-secret grants to the api, web and migration managed identities.
- Monitoring: Log Analytics (30 days, 0.1 GB/day cap) and Application Insights.
- Budget: Cost Management alert at 80% and 100% (monthlyBudget default 50). This is an alert, not a cap.
- Auth: a single-tenant Entra app registration. Easy Auth `allowedPrincipals` is limited to the owner's object IDs, plus the email allowlist (OWNER_ALLOWLIST) in both the proxy and the API. CURRICULUM_ADMIN_EMAILS grants tester previews.
- Paid use: PAID_USAGE_ENABLED=true has been set live.
- Bicep usage defaults: 80 calls/day, 2,000 total; 80k tokens/day, 2M total; 1,200 audio s/day; 300 video s/day, 7,200 total. The actual production overrides are unknown.
- Deploy flow: from WSL with a signed-in `az`, `bash scripts/deploy_current.sh`. It requires a clean tree and green main CI for the exact SHA. It then runs ACR builds (RELEASE_SHA build-arg), azure_release.py deploy (migration job first, then `az containerapp update --image` for api and web, setting only MAX_AUDIO_SECONDS and CURRICULUM_ADMIN_EMAILS), and finally verify_live anonymous. The OIDC deploy.yml exists but its GitHub environment has not been created.

**Strengths**

- Every decision is recorded with its reason and cost (DECISIONS D-01..D-22), so a planner can tell which rules are principled and which are expedient.
- The trust boundary is consistent and well explained: the API is never public, a 60 s HS256 assertion with CSRF header, identity headers are stripped, Easy Auth plus an email allowlist, and production refuses fixtures at startup (ENGINEERING §5, config.py:149-176).
- Strict honesty vocabulary: VERIFICATION statuses separate fixture/CI evidence from live evidence; ContentLabel marks unreviewed content; synthetic audio and virtual-presenter labels.
- Cost control is real: SQL-enforced reserve/commit/release counters, a PAID_USAGE_ENABLED gate in every paid path (stories, videos, coach, content review, topic conversations), and production refuses to start without it.
- The existing 'agent' patterns are bounded and testable: linear 3-node LangGraph graphs (interpret → validate → compose) and complete_once with output ceilings and no automatic paid replay.
- Azure infrastructure is codified (Bicep) with managed identities, a VNet-private DB, a separated migration identity, a migration-first release, readiness tied to the migration head, and a live commit visible at /health.
- A deterministic story validator plus a rewrite loop is a reusable 'models propose, code validates' gate for generated content (D-18, STORY_ENGINE).
- Accessibility is taken seriously: axe WCAG A/AA scans run in the Playwright suites at 1440/768/390/320 px.

**Findings**

| # | Severity | Finding | Evidence | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | high | **The daily plan promises a conversation, but practice never appears in it or earns points** — The streak, points and daily-goal loop (D-19) only rewards stories, videos, read-aloud and word reviews. Topic practice, curriculum lessons, missions and guided conversations (the 'practice' the owner found boring) give no points, have no place on Today and have no next_step. The coach (PracticeCoach) sits on /leerpad (CurriculumPath.tsx:32), not on the home page as LEARNING_AGENTS.md says. A first-time user sees a waiting screen ('De schrijver is bezig…', 1–3 min locally) and a set of links, with no explanation of how the parts fit together. | README.md:3-5 ('one conversation to practise'); TodayHome.tsx:60 ('Eén aflevering, een paar woorden, één gesprek'); routes_stories.py:66-76 (next_step ∈ read/choose/review/wait/explore); add_points is called only in stories/service.py:396-520, videos/service.py:493-516 and stories/vocab.py:123; POINTS in stories/service.py:46 and videos/service.py:55 | Plan a unified daily plan that includes one practice/conversation step and awards points for completed practice actions. D-19 allows this as long as points reward doing and never judge the learner's Dutch. Update README, LEARNING_AGENTS ('Home: Jouw volgende oefening') and STATUS after the change. |
| 2 | high | **Honesty rules limit what gamification can show** — XP, streaks, badges for actions, goals and progress rings are compatible with these rules. An overall level, a 'fluency %', CEFR badges, pronunciation stars or an accuracy-based leaderboard are not. D-20's reason is tied to the transcript-only design. Adding Azure Speech pronunciation assessment would need a new decision that revises D-20 and the README honesty rules; whether nl-BE is supported was not verified. | README.md:44-46 'No pronunciation scores, no aggregate level, no certificate'; D-17 'No fictitious scores, certificates or reviewed-content claims'; D-19 'Points reward doing and are never a judgement'; D-20 'No accent or pronunciation score is computed or shown, because a transcript cannot carry one'; ENGINEERING 'Learning-path boundary' 'There is no aggregate score'; CURRICULUM 'no aggregate score that can conceal an unpassed skill'; DEMO 'never a level' | Design gamification around effort and completion (actions, streaks, per-skill 'practised' counts, words reviewed). If a pronunciation or level signal is wanted, record a new D-number decision that consciously revises D-20 and README §Honesty, and keep the four skill records separate. |
| 3 | high | **A tool-using AI agent goes against the explicit LEARNING_AGENTS boundaries and infra assumptions** — Today's 'agents' are bounded single-call interpreters with code as the authority. A real tutor agent with tools (look up the learner's history, choose or generate exercises, schedule reviews) is possible only if the tools are code-validated, read-mostly and learner-scoped. It needs a recorded decision revising LEARNING_AGENTS 'Responsibilities and limits'. It also needs: tool-calling support in providers/base.py and chat_openai_compatible.py; usage reservations per step (an agent loop can take many calls against an 80/day Bicep default); a stated ceiling on loop length or recursion; and possibly a new Azure resource (Foundry project or Agent Service), which breaks the repeated 'no new Azure service' pattern (LEARNING_AGENTS, TOPIC_PRACTICE, PRACTICE_LIBRARY, GO_LIVE §1 'There is no … Service Bus …'). | LEARNING_AGENTS.md:47-48 'It has no open tool loop, arbitrary database tool, web access or ability to write lesson files'; :41-45 roles table (the model may only interpret, reorder exactly three activities, or suggest issues); :96-97 'No new queue service or agent hosting resource is needed'; D-12 rejects a single call that both decides and replies; practice/workflow.py:123-131 and topic_conversations/workflow.py:118-126 are linear graphs; the chat client uses JSON mode via chat/completions (chat_openai_compatible.py:180-184), with no tools parameter; GO_LIVE §2.4 says reasoning-only deployments are not interchangeable; main.bicep:237-243 kind 'OpenAI' (not a Foundry project); pyproject has no openai or agent SDK | If agents go ahead: keep 'models propose, code validates' (tools return validated data; the agent cannot award passes, mark content reviewed or write content files), add a D-23 decision, extend usage accounting to multi-step loops, update LEARNING_AGENTS, ENGINEERING §4 and §7, GO_LIVE §1/§2.4, and main.bicep (raise usage defaults and capacity, possibly a Foundry resource). |
| 4 | medium | **No onboarding, and the interface chrome is Dutch-only (docs make Dutch UI a stated choice)** — An English-first or Persian-first welcome and orientation flow does not break 'Dutch stays the practice language'. It does change README's 'The interface is in Dutch'. Learner.support_language (DB default 'fa') is not used by the UI, which reads localStorage with default 'nl-en'. That gives two sources of truth for the support language. | README.md:41-42 'Dutch stays the practice language. The interface is in Dutch.'; LEARNING_DESIGN 'Dutch remains the practice language'; LanguageSupport.tsx:5-9 (modes nl-en / nl-fa / nl-fa-en only; Dutch always shown); AppShell.tsx:16-42 Dutch-only nav labels; app/layout.tsx:41 lang='nl'; no onboarding or placement found (grep 'onboard\|welkom' in apps/web); identity/models.py:13-27 Learner has no goals or level fields; stories/service.py:55-58 default_stage falls back to a1; AccountSettings shows only name, email and support language | Add a first-run orientation (goal, level self-placement, support language, how the parts help) shown bilingually through LearningText.
