**Constraints this area must respect**

- Models propose, code validates: dates, availability, bookings, task completion, usage limits, submissions, passes and points are decided in application code; the model never decides (README 'Architecture'; ENGINEERING §1; D-12; ARCHITECTURE_WALKTHROUGH §2 'Where the truth lives').
- Conversation turn shape: LangGraph propose_action (model) → validate_action (code, apply_action) → compose_reply (model, anchored on the fixed line). A reply that contradicts the code is replaced by the fixed line. If the first call fails, the turn fails with a 502 (no simulated conversation). Failed turns do not count against the turn limit. 2 model calls and 1,800 tokens are reserved per turn. Prompt versions are stored. Reason: the code must stay the authority over the appointment (D-12; ENGINEERING §6.1).
- Service missions: character replies use authored phase lines and validated choices (no invented prices or remedies); one interpretation call per turn; a valid option named straight away counts as stating the need, unless the scenario sets reason_before_choice; the 'not on offer' line is only for options that are not on offer (D-21; FOUR_SKILL_CONTENT 'Implementation and boundaries').
- Learning agents (LEARNING_AGENTS 'Responsibilities and limits'): the conversation partner only interprets and quotes; the coach only reorders exactly three allowed activities; the content-review assistant only suggests issues. The application decides ownership, turn order, facts, assistance, replies, completion, candidates and review status. 'No open tool loop, arbitrary database tool, web access or ability to write lesson files.' Reason: language understanding is fallible; code anchors give a conservative check.
- Single-call rules: complete_once means one attempt, no repair, no retry (topic conversation 400 output tokens; coach 600; editorial batch 1,600; 1–5 topics). Story episode at most two model calls. Feedback is one call. Browsing, starting a conversation, hint/repeat and the default coach plan make no model call. No automatic paid replay after uncertain failures (LEARNING_AGENTS 'Cost and recovery'; STORY_ENGINE 'Cost and limits'; D-14).
- Paid use is switched on deliberately with PAID_USAGE_ENABLED, never because credentials are present. Production refuses to start without it (config.py:162). Story, video, coach, content-review and topic-conversation generation refuse Azure calls without it (D-17; README 'Going live'; STORY_ENGINE).
- Usage counters reserve before every external call and are enforced inside the SQL UPDATE. Counters are application estimates, not an invoice. A budget alert is not a hard currency cap. Video seconds are a counter ($1 per minute avatar) and a failed render returns them. At most two videos in progress per learner (D-07; D-17; D-22; ENGINEERING §7; GO_LIVE §2; VIDEO_LESSONS 'Cost and limits').
- No pronunciation or accent score anywhere, 'because a transcript cannot carry one'. Read-aloud only reports recognised words; points are given once per paragraph at ≥50% recognised (D-20; README 'Honesty rules'; CURRICULUM 'Language and assessment review'; LEARNING_DESIGN 'Support and independent evidence').
- No aggregate level or score, no certificate, no CEFR mastery claim, no fictitious scores. Skill records show practised/checkpoint_passed with their evidence, never a level. Stage names are 'not an independently validated CEFR award' (README; D-17; DEMO 'What not to claim'; CURRICULUM; ENGINEERING 'Learning-path boundary'; LEARNING_AGENTS 'No helper awards a final-check pass, locks a stage or declares CEFR mastery').
- Points reward doing and are never a judgement of the learner's Dutch. Daily goal 20 points per Europe/Brussels day. The streak counts consecutive active days (D-19).
- Four separate skill records, never merged. Typed input never counts as speaking practice. Feedback may only contain points that cite resolvable evidence; the rest is dropped and counted. Feedback is on a step, never an assessment of the skill (D-14; D-16; ENGINEERING §6).
- Labelling: every fixed Dutch text is a LocalizedText with review_status, and the UI labels anything not 'reviewed'. Everything the model writes is labelled as generated. Synthetic voices are labelled. The video presenter is labelled virtual ('geen echte persoon'). Never mark content reviewed because tests pass (README; ENGINEERING §3; VIDEO_LESSONS 'Honesty'; FOUR_SKILL_CONTENT; TOPIC_PRACTICE).
- Learner-facing screens must not show model/provider IDs, prompt versions, evidence UUIDs, model latency or session UUIDs; diagnostics stay in Settings › Technische ondersteuning and the export (FOUR_SKILL_CONTENT; CURRICULUM; LEARNING_AGENTS 'What the learner sees').
- Assessment integrity: reading and listening are graded on the server. Final-check answer keys, listening scripts and sample responses are withheld before submission. Submitted final work is immutable during a retry. A model or provider failure never awards a pass. Admin previews never become student passes. A self-report never unlocks a stage. Reading/listening 75% thresholds are internal course rules (CURRICULUM; ENGINEERING 'Learning-path boundary'; LEARNING_DESIGN 'Design choices').
- Session semantics: one active session per learner, mission and variant. A checkpoint with retry:false allows exactly one session. A retried request ID replays the stored turn. Failed turns are kept (D-13).
- Trust boundary: the web app is the only public entry; the API is never public (internal ingress). Every proxied request carries a 60-second HS256 assertion (issuer dlp-web, audience dlp-api, jti). The X-Requested-With: fetch header plus Sec-Fetch-Site/Origin checks are enforced in both tiers. Browser-sent Authorization, Cookie and X-MS-CLIENT-PRINCIPAL* headers are dropped (D-03; D-04; ENGINEERING §5; README architecture table).
- Identity: fixture identity only in development or test, bound to localhost. Production refuses DEV_AUTH_ENABLED, fixture providers, the fixture video renderer and the memory blob store. Production requires Azure chat, speech and blob, a real allowlist, and TLS to PostgreSQL (D-05; config.py:145-178).
- Privacy: Application Insights exports route template, status and duration only. There are no request bodies, identities, prompts or recordings in telemetry, and Uvicorn access logs are disabled in the cloud. The content-review model sees only authored content, never learner answers, recordings or history. No real recording is committed. Scans and OCR stay in .local/ and outside Git. Agree retention and deletion before inviting anyone else (ENGINEERING 'Release 0.2 conventions'; GO_LIVE §6, §8; LEARNING_AGENTS 'Admin editing workflow'; D-09; SOURCE_COVERAGE).
- Belgian Dutch norms: Belgian Standard Dutch is the target (nl-BE locale; nl-BE-DenaNeural or Piper nl_BE-nathalie). Regional gij/ge only when labelled in the glossary. Do not label a shared standard form wrong just because it is also used in the Netherlands. Dutch remains the practice language; English and Persian are support languages, and Persian is rendered RTL (D-18; STORY_ENGINE; SOURCE_COVERAGE step 3; LEARNING_DESIGN; README).
- Interface language: 'The interface is in Dutch' (README 'What it does'). Support translations follow the header choice; changing support mode preserves current work (LEARNING_DESIGN).
- Content provenance: OCR output is never promoted into lessons. Source scans, illustrations and text are not redistributed. All published content is original. Dated facts from the sources must not be copied as current guidance (SOURCE_COVERAGE; CURRICULUM 'Relationship to the supplied scans').
- The story serial is the chosen way forward over more templated content. The model proposes and validator.py decides. A failed draft is rewritten once, and a second failure is shown as a failure. The next episode waits for the learner's choice or 12 hours. At most two unread episodes are written ahead (D-18; STORY_ENGINE).
- The word bank is the learner's own choice: nothing enters it unless they save it. SM-2 scheduling; a lapse returns after 10 minutes (D-19).
- Generative video models were rejected: they are English-first, produce seconds-long clips without Dutch narration, cost $0.10–0.50 per second and are not available in the region used. The Azure avatar is used instead, and video scripts go through the story validator plus a 20-second minimum (D-22).
- Test-database safety: API and E2E test databases must end in _test; destructive setup refuses anything else. Tests use the real PostgreSQL and fixture providers (IMPROVEMENT_REVIEW findings table; README 'Checks'; ENGINEERING §3).
- Evidence vocabulary: verified_local, verified_ci, verified_live, integration_pending, review_pending. Never infer real provider validation from a fixture, a dry run, a configured endpoint or a compiled template (VERIFICATION; ENGINEERING 'Release 0.2 conventions'; D-10).
- Release discipline: migration-first. Both images are always built. dlp.release migrate is the only schema path. The runtime uses a DML-only role. Migrations must be additive and backward-compatible; no blind Alembic downgrade. Release only after green main CI for the exact commit. Never set publicWeb=true at bootstrap, and keep publicWeb=true with real image refs on later template deploys (GO_LIVE §4–§8; D-17).
- Model deployment rules: the client needs Chat Completions with JSON mode, temperature and max_tokens. Reasoning-only deployments are not interchangeable. Do not silently use a Global deployment if regional processing matters. Pin model and version with NoAutoUpgrade. A strong model needs both name and version (GO_LIVE §2.4; main.bicep:32-40, 244-256).
- Owner's ordered roadmap: phone run, live video, Belgian Dutch reviewer, GitHub deploy setup — 'Only then: more content, more missions' (STATUS 'Next steps'). IMPROVEMENT_REVIEW item 6 says the same, and 'Teacher workspaces and formal level assessments remain separate product work'.
- Design choices: one visible next step, separate four-skill progress, support tools inside the relevant practice section rather than more main navigation, a single native audio player (no overlapping playback), recording and submission guard navigation, restrained navy/blue palette with high contrast, WCAG 2.2 as the target with automated axe checks as a supplement only (LEARNING_DESIGN 'Design choices'; ENGINEERING; D-17).
- Web security and CSP: nonce-based scripts; style, font, img, media and connect sources are 'self' only (plus data: and blob: for images and media); fonts are self-hosted (@fontsource). No CDN or remote assets (ENGINEERING 'Release 0.2 conventions'; apps/web/proxy.ts:10-15).
- Code conventions: Python 3.11, type hints, Pydantic extra='forbid' for content, ruff line length 130. TypeScript strict, ESLint Next rules, no state-setting inside effect bodies. Every request has an X-Request-Id (^[A-Za-z0-9._-]{8,64}$) used for idempotency. All DB connections are pinned to UTC. scripts/run.py is the cross-platform task runner (ENGINEERING §3; D-11; D-14).
- Effectiveness claims: completion and app usage alone do not show learning. Evaluate transfer, delayed recall, help used, correction accuracy and learner experience before claiming effectiveness (LEARNING_DESIGN 'Sources and validation boundaries').

**Open questions**

- Exact production chat model, version, SKU and capacity, and whether chat-strong is deployed. infra/parameters.local.json is ignored; VERIFICATION only names gpt-4.1-mini.
- Actual production usage limits. Bicep defaults are 80 calls/day and 80k tokens/day unless the owner overrode them; the config default is 200/200k.
- Which commit is live. VERIFICATION records release a6833ee, but later 2 Oct commits (D-21 fix, Azure adapter reporting, video lessons 795697b) may or may not have been deployed; STATUS says the live avatar render has not been done.
- The owner's monthly budget amount and remaining Azure credits or allowance. The docs only say monthlyBudget defaults to 50 as an alert amount.
- Whether the owner wants to keep the 'interface is in Dutch' rule or wants English or Persian UI chrome during onboarding.
- Whether the owner accepts revising D-20, for example Azure Speech pronunciation assessment. Its nl-BE locale support was not verified here.
- Whether a Foundry project or Agent Service resource is acceptable, given the repeated 'no new Azure service' stance and the current kind 'OpenAI' account.
- Whether the app must remain single-user (Easy Auth allowedPrincipals plus the owner allowlist) or should support more learners. Retention and deletion policies are not yet agreed (GO_LIVE §8).
- Whether scripts/setup_github_deploy.sh has been run since 2 Oct, which would make the deploy.yml 'production' environment exist.
- Whether the owner's Entra account is a personal Microsoft account added as a guest or member. GO_LIVE §2.6 mentions this case, but the actual setup is not documented.

### Azure agent platform research (web, read 2026-10-05)

REPO GROUNDING. The API reaches models through one hand-written httpx client, `OpenAICompatibleChatModel` (apps/api/src/dlp/providers/chat_openai_compatible.py). Ollama and Azure use the same client. For Azure it posts to `{endpoint}/openai/v1/chat/completions` when `api_version == "v1"` (lines 42-48), which is the Bicep default (infra/main.bicep:344). Every request sends `temperature` and `max_tokens`. When a schema is given, the client puts the Pydantic JSON schema into a system message and sets `response_format={"type":"json_object"}` (JSON mode, not strict structured outputs) (lines 174-184). It then validates on the client and runs a bounded "repair" retry loop (lines 194-215). `complete_once` (lines 142-161) sends at most one request for the newer flows. Authentication uses managed identity with the `https://ai.azure.com/.default` scope (.env.example:55, registry.py:41-54). Two tiers exist, `chat` and `chat_strong` (registry.py:57-77). Speech uses the REST short-audio endpoints with `nl-BE` STT and the `nl-BE-DenaNeural` voice, with no Speech SDK and no pronunciation header (speech_azure.py:58-93, 117-168). The "agents" are deliberately not autonomous. `topic_conversations/workflow.py` is a 3-node linear LangGraph (interpret → validate → compose, lines 89-126). The model only interprets; code validates quoted evidence and picks authored replies (docs/LEARNING_AGENTS.md:47-52, DECISIONS.md D-12). The infrastructure is Bicep with these pieces: West Europe, a `Microsoft.CognitiveServices/accounts` kind OpenAI, deployments `chat-small`/`chat-strong` with `chatDeploymentSku` defaulting to `'Standard'` (regional) and `versionUpgradeOption: 'NoAutoUpgrade'` (main.bicep:35-36, 244-256), Speech S0, a single-replica 0.25 vCPU API with internal ingress, App Insights, and a budget alert. Per-learner caps are enforced in SQL with reserve/commit/release (domains/usage/service.py). Telemetry is privacy-first: azure-monitor-opentelemetry with httpx and FastAPI auto-instrumentation disabled and 10% sampling (observability.py:12-28).

WHAT THE PRIMARY SOURCES SAY (Oct 2026). (1) Microsoft Agent Framework reached 1.0 GA on 3 Apr 2026 for Python (`pip install agent-framework`, provider package `agent-framework-openai`) and .NET. It is the direct successor to Semantic Kernel and AutoGen. Its concepts are agents, sessions, context providers/memory, middleware, tools/MCP, and graph workflows with checkpointing and human-in-the-loop. It emits OpenTelemetry GenAI semantic-convention spans (`invoke_agent`, `chat`, `execute_tool`); prompt and response capture is off by default (`ENABLE_SENSITIVE_DATA=false`). Its own guidance says: "If you can write a function to handle the task, do that instead of using an AI agent." Its Python `OpenAIChatClient` targets the Responses API and is the recommended client. (2) Foundry Agent Service is GA in West Europe and Sweden Central. Prompt agents carry no extra charge (you pay for tokens and tools). Hosted agents went GA on 9 Jul 2026, run per-session VM sandboxes, and are billed per vCPU-hour and GiB-hour. Agent state lives either in Microsoft-managed storage (Basic setup) or in your own Cosmos DB, AI Search and Storage (Standard setup). The structured-outputs doc states that structured outputs are NOT supported with Foundry Agents Service. (3) The Responses API is GA under `/openai/v1/responses` in westeurope and swedencentral, and Microsoft recommends it for new apps. Chat Completions is not deprecated. Both support strict `json_schema` structured outputs, with these limits: all fields required, `additionalProperties:false`, no min/max/pattern keywords, at most 100 properties and 5 nesting levels. (4) Model reality for this repo: in westeurope, regional "Standard" offers only gpt-4.1-mini among chat models. gpt-4.1-mini and gpt-4o-mini are already "Deprecated" (closed to new customers) and retire on 2027-04-14; gpt-4.1-nano retires on 2026-10-14. Newer models (gpt-5.4-mini/nano, gpt-5.5, gpt-5.6-luna/terra/sol, gpt-6-luna/sol) are available in westeurope only as Data Zone Standard (EU) or Global Standard. GPT-5.x reasoning models reject `temperature` and `max_tokens`, so the current client is incompatible with them. GPT-6 Luna is priced at $0.10/$0.50 per 1M tokens Global and $0.12/$0.60 EU Data Zone; GPT-5.6 Luna at $0.20/$1.20 Global. (5) Real-time voice: GPT Realtime 2.1 and 2.1-mini are GA over WebRTC, WebSocket and SIP, but in Europe they are deployable only in swedencentral and francecentral, not westeurope. Their voices are OpenAI voices, not Belgian ones, and Dutch is "supported" only in the sense that it passed a WER below 50% bar. Voice Live, a managed STT + LLM + TTS pipeline, runs in westeurope with text models over the EU data zone plus Azure Speech voices, so nl-BE-DenaNeural/ArnaudNeural work there. Its automatic multilingual mode excludes Dutch, so the language must be set explicitly. It is billed per token in Pro, Standard and Lite tiers (about 10 input and 20 output audio tokens per second), and its content filtering cannot be disabled. Pronunciation assessment supports nl-NL but NOT nl-BE. It works on the REST short-audio endpoint via the `Pronunciation-Assessment` header with at most 30 s of audio, prosody is en-US only, and it costs the same as standard STT. (6) Evaluation and observability: Foundry evaluations, monitoring and tracing are GA (March 2026, per a techcommunity post whose body could not be fetched). Cloud evaluation can score your own datasets or traces. Default guardrails filter at Medium severity in four harm categories and turn on jailbreak Prompt Shields, but the classifiers are trained on EN, DE, JA, ES, FR, IT, PT and ZH, not Dutch. Prompt caching is automatic for prefixes of at least 1,024 identical tokens; GPT-5.6 and later also charge for cache writes and accept `prompt_cache_key`. API Management's `llm-token-limit` and semantic-cache policies exist, but this app's SQL counters already cover per-user caps. (7) Language-tutor patterns: Duolingo's Roleplay, Video Call and Explain My Answer use curriculum-expert-written scenarios and several narrow prompts per turn (ask, state, change subject, close) inside a flow chart with a beginning, middle and end. They give post-conversation feedback on accuracy and complexity, use adaptive length (about 1 to 3 minutes), apply no penalties, and keep humans reviewing AI content. Duolingo says plain "chat with a chatbot" practice lacks level calibration and structure.

NET IMPLICATION. The repo's "model interprets, code decides" design already matches current best practice. The upgrades that actually move the product forward are not a new agent platform. They are: (a) migrate the client to the v1 Responses API with strict json_schema and a currently supported EU Data Zone model (Luna-class for interpretation and grading, a Sol or Terra-class model for writing); (b) add tutor behaviours as bounded, code-orchestrated steps (explain-my-mistake, post-conversation feedback, adaptive next activity), optionally with Agent Framework in-process inside FastAPI; (c) optionally add a Voice Live "live conversation" mode in westeurope with nl-BE Azure voices; (d) optionally add nl-NL scripted pronunciation assessment for read-aloud, with an explicit caveat that it uses Netherlands Dutch. Foundry hosted agents add a second state store, cost and network complexity, and give nothing a single-owner app needs.

**Strengths**

- The 'model interprets, code decides' pattern (DECISIONS.md D-12; topic_conversations/workflow.py validate_meaning) is exactly what current guidance recommends over open agent loops. Microsoft's Agent Framework overview says: 'If you can write a function to handle the task, do that instead of using an AI agent.' Duolingo Roleplay also uses expert-authored scenarios with several narrow prompts per turn.
- It already uses the GA v1 surface (/openai/v1/chat/completions, no dated api-version) and the documented Entra scope https://ai.azure.com/.default with managed identity (chat_openai_compatible.py:44-46; .env.example:54-55; main.bicep:344). That makes a move to /openai/v1/responses small.
- The narrow provider interface (providers/base.py ChatModel/SpeechToText/TextToSpeech) and the single registry (registry.py) mean a Responses-API or Voice Live adapter can be added without touching domain code.
- Per-learner cost caps already exist as atomic SQL reserve/commit/release (usage/service.py:1-4, 104-172), with complete_once preventing hidden retries. An API Management AI gateway is unnecessary for a single owner.
- Telemetry is already privacy-safe (observability.py docstring and lines 20-28) and pins azure-monitor-opentelemetry>=1.8.10 (pyproject.toml:35), the version Microsoft's Agent Framework docs require for HTTPX/OpenAI span correlation.
- Speech already uses nl-BE STT and nl-BE-DenaNeural TTS through REST with Entra tokens (speech_azure.py). The REST short-audio endpoint is also where pronunciation assessment would plug in, through a single header.
- The Bicep deployment SKU parameter already allows 'DataZoneStandard' (main.bicep:35), so moving to EU Data Zone models is a parameter change, not a redesign.

**Findings**

| # | Severity | Finding | Evidence | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | high | **The deployable model set has moved: in West Europe 'Standard' offers only deprecated gpt-4.1-mini, and NoAutoUpgrade means a hard stop at retirement** — Any fresh deployment, such as a new subscription or a rebuilt environment, may be unable to create gpt-4.1-mini at all. An existing deployment will return 410 after 2027-04-14. Current-generation models in West Europe require DataZoneStandard (EU processing, which honours GO_LIVE.md:50-51 'Do not silently select a Global deployment if regional processing matters') or GlobalStandard. GPT-6 announcement (https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/gpt-6-astra-sol-and-luna-for-production-agents-in-microsoft-foundry/, 22 Sep 2026), short-context prices per 1M tokens: Luna $0.10 in / $0.50 out Global, $0.12 / $0.60 EU Data Zone; Sol $2.00 / $10.00 Global, $2.40 / $12.00 EU. The post describes Luna as built 'for high-volume work … extraction, summarization, request routing'. GPT-5.6 (https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/gpt-5-6-now-available-in-microsoft-foundry/, 9 Jul 2026): Luna $0.20/$1.20, Terra $2/$12, Sol $5/$30 Global (Sol temporarily $4/$20 from 1 Sep to 30 Nov 2026). | Repo: infra/main.bicep:35-36 (chatDeploymentSku default 'Standard'), :248 and :254 (versionUpgradeOption 'NoAutoUpgrade'); infra/parameters.example.json:6 (westeurope). https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/foundry-models/concepts/models-sold-directly-by-azure-region-availability (ms.date 2026-09-03): the westeurope Standard/Regional table lists only gpt-4.1-mini (2025-04-14) among chat models; westeurope Data Zone Standard and Global Standard list gpt-5.4, gpt-5.4-mini, gpt-5.5, gpt-5.6-luna/sol/terra, gpt-6-luna/sol, gpt-6.1-sol. https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/openai/concepts/model-retirement-schedule (2026-09-21): gpt-4.1-mini and gpt-4o-mini are 'Deprecated', retiring 2027-04-14; gpt-4.1-nano retires 2026-10-14; gpt-5.4-mini retires 2027-09-21; gpt-5.6-luna 2028-01-11. https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/openai/concepts/model-retirements: 'Deprecated' means new customers and new subscriptions cannot deploy; NoAutoUpgrade means the deployment stops working at retirement. | Plan to switch chatDeploymentSku to 'DataZoneStandard' and pick a Luna-class model (gpt-6-luna or gpt-5.6-luna) for chat-small, which handles interpretation, grading and translation. Pick a Sol or Terra-class model for chat-strong (stories, video scripts, content review, writing feedback). Prefer a versionUpgradeOption that will not silently break at retirement, or add a Models-API lifecycle check to preflight. Expect roughly a 20% EU Data Zone premium over Global for GPT-6 pricing. |
| 2 | high | **The current chat client is incompatible with current reasoning models (temperature, max_tokens) and uses JSON mode rather than strict structured outputs** — Moving chat-small to any GPT-5.x model, without code changes, would produce HTTP 400s for temperature and max_tokens. Strict json_schema would remove most of the repair loop (fewer billed retries, simpler accounting in complete_once). **Constraints this area must respect**

- D-12 / LEARNING_AGENTS.md:41-49: the model interprets and phrases, the application decides. No open tool loop, no arbitrary database tool, no web access, no ability to write lesson files. Any 'agent' must respect this or the owner must consciously revise it.
- D-16: typed input never counts as speaking practice; spoken evidence must come from a transcript. A live voice mode must still produce stored transcripts.
- D-20: no pronunciation score is computed. Adding Azure pronunciation assessment (nl-NL only) consciously revises this decision and must be labelled as NL-Dutch reference, never as CEFR or skill completion.
- D-17 and GO_LIVE.md:44-47: paid usage is switched on only by PAID_USAGE_ENABLED, never by the presence of credentials; budgets are alerts, not caps.
- GO_LIVE.md:50-51: do not silently select a Global deployment if regional processing matters. Prefer DataZoneStandard (EU) over GlobalStandard for new models.
- main.bicep:321, 376: the API has internal-only ingress behind the Next.js signed proxy and exactly 1 replica at 0.25 vCPU / 0.5 GiB. Long-lived WebSockets (Voice Live/Realtime) need a relay path and resource sizing.
- observability.py:1 and LEARNING_AGENTS.md:19-20: never export prompts, bodies, headers, full URLs or user identifiers; raw model names, prompt IDs and evidence UUIDs stay out of learner-facing feedback.
- Every provider call must reserve usage before and commit or release after (usage/service.py). complete_once semantics (no hidden retries) apply to newer flows.
- MAX_AUDIO_SECONDS=60 (Azure short-audio REST ceiling); pronunciation assessment on REST requires at most 30 s.
- Pydantic schemas used with strict json_schema must have all fields required (nullable instead of optional), additionalProperties false, and no length/pattern constraints enforced by the service; keep those checks in code.

**Open questions**

- Which chat model and version is actually deployed in resource group dlp-production (West Europe, live since 2 Oct 2026)? If it is gpt-4.1-mini, it is 'Deprecated' and retires 2027-04-14 (gpt-4.1-nano retires 2026-10-14); if gpt-4o-mini, same date. Not verifiable from the repo.
- GPT-6 temperature support is contradictory in https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/openai/how-to/reasoning: the GPT-6 feature table lists temperature/top_p as supported for gpt-6-sol/luna, but the 'Unsupported parameters' section says reasoning models other than GPT-6 Astra don't support temperature. Needs a live test.
- Exact per-token prices for gpt-realtime-2.1 / 2.1-mini and for the Voice Live Pro/Standard/Lite tiers could not be read; both pricing pages render values as '$-'. Per-minute cost of a live conversation is therefore unverified.
- Hosted-agent compute rates ($0.0994 per vCPU-hour, $0.0118 per GiB-hour) appear only in search-result snippets; the pricing page fetch showed '$-'. Unverified, though irrelevant if hosted agents are not used.
- Whether the owner's subscription has EU Data Zone quota for gpt-6-luna / gpt-5.6-luna (the gpt-5.6-luna doc notes a quota request may be needed depending on quota tier).
- Whether Voice Live accepts nl-BE explicitly as input_audio_transcription language with azure-speech (the docs say any STT locale is supported, but an nl-BE session was not tested), and whether it can reuse the existing S0 Speech resource with managed identity or needs a Foundry/AIServices resource.
- Whether the Voice Live WebSocket can be reached directly from the browser with a short-lived token (the doc describes server-to-server middle-tier use; the Azure OpenAI Realtime API documents WebRTC via /openai/v1/realtime/client_secrets). This affects whether a relay through Next.js is mandatory.
- Quality of gpt-realtime and gpt-5.x Luna models on Belgian Standard Dutch learner speech and text is unknown; Dutch passes only a WER < 50% bar for gpt-realtime. A small owner-run benchmark is needed.
- Whether nl-NL pronunciation assessment gives useful, non-misleading feedback to a learner targeting Belgian Dutch; needs owner testing.
- The GA date and details of Foundry observability/evaluations come from a techcommunity post whose body could not be fetched (March 2026 per the search snippet).
- The latest PyPI version and minimum Python version of `agent-framework` were not confirmed (repo requires Python >=3.11).

## Corrections from the verification pass

Claims the sceptics could not confirm as stated. Where a correction conflicts with an area finding above, the correction wins.

### Practice content and the endpoints an exercise engine would use. Repo root: C:\Users\Nima\Desktop\MINE\My_projects\Dutch Learning Platform\Final_Project_v1 (all paths below are relative to it). Content statistics come from read-only python one-liners run over content/*.json.

- **partly** — Topic detail (curriculum/topics.py) strips answer_index and explanation from reading/listening questions; keys come back only after the whole topic is submitted.
  - Correction: Stripping happens per skill activity (reading or listening), not per topic. Keys come back on any complete submission of that activity, even a failed one, so a learner can fail once, read the keys and resubmit. Content overlap also makes many keys public anyway. At pre-a1, a1 and pre-a2 both reading questions of every topic can be answered from data the browser already gets: the library story detail and the topic's own vocabulary list.
  - Evidence: The code does strip them: apps/api/src/dlp/domains/curriculum/topics.py:176-180 (`question.pop("answer_index")`, `question.pop("explanation")`). This is pinned by apps/api/tests/test_topics.py:152-159 (`"answer_index" not in detail.text and "explanation" not in detail.text`). Keys come back in `explanations` on EVERY submission of that one skill activity, pass or fail: topics.py:219-221. objective_result needs an answer for every question of that activity (service.py:152-154 returns 422 otherwise). Content still leaks the keys. (a) At pre-a1, a1, pre-a2, pre-b2, b2 and pre-c1, one reading question per topic (r1 at pre-a1, r2 at pre-b2) matches the Story Time question exactly: same prompt, same options and same answer_index (100/100 per stage). GET /library/{stage}/stories/{id} returns that question with its answer_index (library.py:160-164). Example: pre-a1-t001-r1 is the question of story pre-a1-s001. (b) At pre-a1, a1 and pre-a2 every topic has one 'Wat betekent ‘X’' question (100 per stage). Its correct option equals meaning.nl of a word in the same topic-detail response's `vocabulary` array (topics.py:189), which the UI shows in <details className="topic-vocabulary"> (SkillTopicPractice.tsx:178).
- **partly** — The library vocabulary/word bank has no 'save to SM-2 word bank' action; words.save is only reachable from episode/video glossaries and manual entry in WordReview.
  - Correction: The backend is already ready for library saves. SaveWordBody.source_kind accepts Literal["story","library","manual","video"] (stories/schemas.py:166), and the vocab.py:3 docstring names 'the library'. What is missing is a UI button and a client function that sends source_kind "library" and source_id (e.g. the card id). The current words.save cannot do that because source_kind is fixed to "manual".
  - Evidence: Confirmed: WordLibrary (LearningLibrary.tsx:54-81), VocabularyRecall, the topic vocabulary list (SkillTopicPractice.tsx:178) and the stage core word list (CurriculumLesson.tsx:87) have no save action. The `words.save` client (lib/client/stories.ts:80-81) is called only from WordReview.tsx:51 and hard-codes `source_kind: "manual"`. Episode and video glossaries do NOT use words.save. They call separate endpoints, stories.saveWord → POST /stories/episodes/{id}/words (routes_stories.py:159-166) and videos.saveWord → POST /videos/{id}/words (routes_videos.py:145-149). Both reach vocab.save_word on the server.
- **refuted** — The vocabulary banks contain almost no verbs (so conjugation drills cannot be generated from existing content).
  - Correction: pre-a1..pre-c1 have roughly 40-50 infinitives per stage (about 8-10% of cards); c1, pre-c2 and c2 have essentially none. Verbs are stored as infinitives only, with meaning.en lacking 'to' and no present, past or participle forms or separable-prefix markup. Conjugation drills therefore need a conjugation rule engine or new authored data. LibraryWord is extra="forbid" (library.py:17), so new fields require a schema change.
  - Evidence: Heuristic count of infinitive-shaped terms (single word ending in -en, no article, minus obvious non-verbs). Roughly 48 per stage in pre-a1, a1 and pre-a2 (e.g. komen, blijven, lezen, schrijven, kopen, betalen, kunnen, mogen, moeten, willen, zullen). About 50 in a2 and pre-b1, though a2 includes some plural nouns (peren, bananen). About 42 in b1 and about 51 in pre-b2, b2 and pre-c1 (uitstellen, afwegen, onderhandelen...). path.json stage vocabulary also has verbs: pre-a1 has wonen, spreken, luisteren, lezen, schrijven, herhalen, voorstellen, spellen. Only c1, pre-c2 and c2 are all article-led noun phrases (e.g. 'de inspraak').

### learner identity, level and how a new persisted feature is added

- **partly** — The Learner model has no level, goal or onboarding fields; it has support_language (default 'fa') which no API route ever updates and the web never reads.
  - Correction: No level, goal or onboarding columns: correct. No route writes support_language: correct. 'The web never reads it' needs a correction. The API already returns it from GET /progress and GET /export, the web types it in LearningData, and AccountSettings already fetches it through useLearningData. The web just never uses the value. Its vocabulary also doesn't match the web's: the server default is 'fa', while the web modes are 'nl-en' | 'nl-fa' | 'nl-fa-en'. String(10) is still long enough for 'nl-fa-en'.
  - Evidence: apps/api/src/dlp/domains/identity/models.py:18-27 has only these columns: id, subject, email, display_name, identity_provider, support_language (String(10), default="fa"), target_language (default="nl-BE"), notes, created_at, last_seen_at. A grep for support_language finds only reads: routes_progress.py:31 (GET /progress) and :57 (GET /export). No route writes it. On the web side the field is declared at apps/web/lib/client/learning.ts:15 (LearningData.learner.support_language) and arrives in the GET /progress payload that useLearningData fetches. No component uses it: AccountSettings.tsx:81 shows the localStorage `mode` instead. The daily goal is a constant, DAILY_GOAL = 20 (stories/today.py:16), not a learner field.
- **partly** — Level is chosen independently in four places: stories level select on TodayHome.tsx, PracticeCoach.tsx localStorage ('taalstudio.practice-stage', default a1), VideoLibrary.tsx (default_stage), CurriculumPath.tsx (first unpassed stage).
  - Correction: There are four selectors, but only three sources of truth. The video default is not independent: it is the stories series stage_id, so changing the story level on TodayHome also changes the video default. Only the in-page video select is local and unpersisted. The practice-stage localStorage key is per learner: 'taalstudio.practice-stage.<learner uuid>'. For a brand-new learner the defaults conflict. CurriculumPath suggests pre-a1, because pre-a1 is STAGE_IDS[0] and nothing is passed yet. Stories, videos and PracticeCoach all default to a1. Two minor extra stage selectors also exist: the admin-only ContentReviewQueue.tsx:89-91 and the URL route /learn/[stageId].
  - Evidence: TodayHome.tsx:139-143 select#story-level, value=plan.series.stage_id, onChange calls stories.level (POST /stories/level, persisted on story_series.stage_id). PracticeCoach.tsx:11-16: the key is `taalstudio.practice-stage.${learnerKey}` (per learner), and the fallback is initialStage="a1". Its only caller, CurriculumPath.tsx:32, passes no initialStage. VideoLibrary.tsx:63: `setStage(current => current || value.default_stage)`, where videos/service.py:470-472 sets default_stage = stories.ensure_series(...).stage_id. The video select (:114) is unpersisted React state; the chosen stage is sent per request in POST /videos. CurriculumPath.tsx:20: `next = data?.stages.find(stage => !stage.passed) ?? data?.stages.at(-1)`. passed comes from curriculum/service.py:95,110 (passed_stages).
- **partly** — Alembic head is 0007 (video lessons); schema changes only run through dlp.release migrate; runtime DB role is DML-only.
  - Correction: Head 0007 and the DML-only runtime role are correct. 'Only through dlp.release migrate' is true for the cloud only. Locally (scripts/run.py migrate/dev) and in tests (conftest.py), schema changes run through `alembic upgrade head` directly, without the grants step. The grants use ON ALL TABLES and are re-issued on every migrate, so a new table gets DML grants automatically after the release job runs.
  - Evidence: Head: migrations/versions has 0001-0007, and 0007_video_lessons.py:12-13 declares `revision = "0007"`, `down_revision = "0006"`. The release path: release.py:45 `command.upgrade(config, "head")`, infra/main.bicep:300 `['python','-m','dlp.release','migrate']`, docs/ENGINEERING.md:30 says it is 'the only cloud schema-update path'. Grants: release.py:58 `GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ON ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA public TO dlp_app`, :60 `REVOKE INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ON alembic_version`, :61 `REVOKE CREATE ON SCHEMA public FROM PUBLIC`. Local and test paths also run plain alembic: scripts/run.py:160-162 task_migrate runs `python -m alembic upgrade head` (also :309, :354), Makefile:8 `migrate`, apps/api/README.md:9, and tests/conftest.py:92 runs `alembic upgrade head` against the test DB.

### Chat model seam (apps/api/src/dlp/providers/*) and Next.js /api proxy (apps/web/app/api/[...path]/route.ts): readiness for tool calling, strict structured output and streaming. All paths below are relative to repo root C:\Users\Nima\Desktop\MINE\My_projects\Dutch Learning Platform\Final_Project_v1

- **partly** — All model calls go through ChatModel in providers/base.py with exactly two methods, complete (retry + JSON repair) and complete_once (exactly one HTTP request); ChatMessage roles are only system|user|assistant.
  - Correction: ChatModel has one abstract method (complete), one concrete method with a delegating default (complete_once), and describe(). The 'retry + JSON repair' and 'exactly one HTTP request' semantics belong to OpenAICompatibleChatModel only. FixtureChatModel (and benchmark ExpectedAnswerModel/WrongAnswerModel) inherit the delegating complete_once. ChatMessage carries no name/tool_call_id/tool_calls fields. Apart from preflight's /api/tags probe, every completion goes through ChatModel.
  - Evidence: base.py:12 `Role = Literal["system", "user", "assistant"]`; base.py:24-27 ChatMessage(frozen) has only `role`, `content`. base.py:48-92 ChatModel: class attrs `name="chat"`, `model=""`; abstract `complete` (52-63); concrete `complete_once` (65-89) whose base implementation just delegates to `self.complete(...)`; plus `describe()` (91-92). Retry/repair lives only in OpenAICompatibleChatModel._complete (chat_openai_compatible.py:163-243); its complete_once passes max_attempts=1 (142-161). All domain call sites use .complete/.complete_once (grep: practice/workflow.py:65,109; topic_conversations/workflow.py:95; feedback/service.py:164; curriculum/service.py:236; curriculum/writing.py:139; coaching/service.py:311; content_review/service.py:221; stories/service.py:278,478; videos/service.py:208); benchmarks/language/harness.py:167-186 also goes through build_chat/ChatModel. Only other model-server HTTP: preflight.py:104 `httpx.get(f"{base}/api/tags")` (lists Ollama models, not a completion).
- **partly** — The Next.js proxy returns the upstream body as a stream (so SSE would pass through), buffers the request body, passes no AbortSignal/timeout upstream, and forwards content-type.
  - Correction: The code facts are correct, but SSE support is not turnkey. `Accept: text/event-stream` passes. `Last-Event-ID` is dropped, so reconnect/resume does not work. request.signal is not forwarded, so a browser disconnect does not cancel the upstream (paid) generation. No X-Accel-Buffering-style header is forwarded. next.config.ts does not set `compress`, and nothing in the repo shows whether Next's response compression buffers text/event-stream; verify that. The browser helper apiFetch hard-codes `Accept: "application/json, audio/wav"` (lib/client/api.ts:40-44), and apiJson parses JSON, so a streaming consumer needs its own helper.
  - Evidence: route.ts:128 `return new Response(upstream.body, { status: upstream.status, headers: responseHeaders })`; route.ts:106-112 `body = await request.arrayBuffer()` with a 6 MiB cap (MAX_BODY_BYTES, line 35, returns 413); route.ts:116 `fetch(target, { method, headers, body, cache: "no-store", redirect: "manual" })` has no signal and no timeout; FORWARDED_REQUEST_HEADERS (19) = content-type, accept, x-requested-with, accept-language, range; FORWARDED_RESPONSE_HEADERS (20-34) include content-type, content-length, cache-control; route.ts:127 sets cache-control no-store if absent; runtime nodejs, dynamic force-dynamic (14-15).

### App shell, navigation, interface language, and what the e2e tests pin about them (apps/web)

- **partly** — All interface chrome is hard-coded Dutch; English/Persian appear only via <LearningText text={{nl,en,fa}}> from components/LanguageSupport.tsx; <html lang="nl"> is fixed in app/layout.tsx.
  - Correction: Interface chrome is hard-coded Dutch and <html lang="nl"> is fixed. English and Persian appear in two ways: through <LearningText>, and through direct useLanguageSupport() reads (showEnglish/showPersian) in EpisodeReader, VideoPlayer, WordReview, HelpLadder, SkillTopicPractice and ReadingStep, plus the switcher's own English/Persian labels. No i18n layer or dictionary exists. LanguageProvider's server snapshot is always 'nl-en' (LanguageSupport.tsx:24), so learners in the nl-fa modes get a client-side switch after hydration.
  - Evidence: Chrome strings are Dutch literals everywhere (AppShell.tsx, page.tsx files, components). The root element is <html lang="nl"> at app/layout.tsx:41. However, many components read useLanguageSupport() directly instead of going through LearningText: EpisodeReader.tsx:89-95 and 129-137, VideoPlayer.tsx:108-116 and 123-131, WordReview.tsx:27 and 69, HelpLadder.tsx:27 and 68-76, SkillTopicPractice.tsx:30 and 60, ReadingStep.tsx:36-98, and AccountSettings.tsx:11 (mode). WordReview.tsx:84-85 has fixed lang=en / lang=fa inputs. The LanguageSwitcher itself renders English and Persian: label `Taalhulp <span lang="en">/ language</span>` and options 'Nederlands · English' / 'Nederlands · فارسی' (LanguageSupport.tsx:48-52).
- **partly** — AppShell intercepts link clicks (navigation guard) so writing autosave/recording finish before navigation.
  - Correction: The guard only intercepts anchor clicks inside .app-frame, which covers both shell and in-page links. It does not cover browser back/forward, programmatic router.push, or the skip link (layout.tsx:43, outside .app-frame). Mission writing drafts are flushed and then navigation proceeds, or is blocked if the save fails. Curriculum lessons and tests do not wait for recording or saving to finish; they block navigation and show an error. beforeunload is handled only in WritingStep.tsx:69-75. Any new nav built from buttons, a portal outside .app-frame, or router.push would bypass the guard.
  - Evidence: AppShell.tsx:71-103: `onClickCapture={navigate}` on div.app-frame (line 108). It acts only when a guard is registered. It ignores modified or non-left clicks, download links, target=_blank, other origins, and same-pathname links. Otherwise it calls preventDefault, awaits guard.current(), and calls router.push only if the guard returns true. Guards are registered through NavigationGuardContext (lib/client/navigation.ts). WritingStep.tsx:76-84 flushes persistRef.current(text) and returns false if the save fails; it reaches the shell through LessonShell.tsx:105-110. CurriculumLesson.tsx:38-45 and CurriculumTest.tsx:26-33 do not wait: while busy or recordingBusy they return false and set the error 'Rond je opname af en wacht tot je werk is bewaard voordat je verdergaat.'
- **partly** — Every route page.tsx is a thin server component rendering one client component; 41 of 42 components are "use client"; there are no loading.tsx files.
  - Correction: 41/42 'use client' and zero loading.tsx are confirmed. Pages are server components, but four of them (missions, progress, settings, speech-check) compose server-rendered headings with several client components, and those headings are pinned by e2e. No pages have metadata titles for speech-check, missions/[missionId], learn/[stageId] or learn/[stageId]/test. layout.tsx sets `dynamic = "force-dynamic"` (line 37).
  - Evidence: components/ holds 42 .tsx files, and only Icon.tsx lacks "use client" (checked with head -3). find app -name 'loading.*' returns nothing. Most of the 13 page.tsx files render a single component, but missions/page.tsx:6 renders an inline <header> plus MissionCatalog. progress/page.tsx:6 renders CurriculumProgress plus <details><summary>Voortgang in praktijkgesprekken</summary><ProgressOverview nested/></details>. settings/page.tsx:7-34 renders a header plus AccountSettings, StatusPanel and UsagePanel. speech-check/page.tsx renders a header, an aside callout and SpeechCheck. app/error.tsx is "use client"; app/not-found.tsx is a server component.

Every listed class has zero references, and so do mission-copy, little-orbit, live-dot, orbit-two, spark-one, spark-two, step-segments, circle-arrow, status-dot, skill-card-top, skill-card-bottom, skill-index, focus-note, focus-persian, button.light, review-count and stage-lock-reason. The only reference to stage-locked is a test: curriculum.spec.ts:10 `expect(path.locator('.stage-locked')).toHaveCount(0)`.
